DeepQuarry Final API

Against origin/master 6dcd1e30f9 1 October 2026 Replaces the OM layer, the sys layer, interactions and every legacy declaration form

This is every public form game code writes once the migration is done. That covers declaring a type, scheduling work, holding and changing state, linking entities, performing actions, reacting to things, drawing, talking to players and to Rust. Each section ends with what it replaces. Section 17 maps the old forms that matter to their new ones, and says which lint retires each; the full table, checked mechanically against the API surface on master, is doc/rewrite/api_mapping.tsv. Section 19 is the plan that gets us there.

Vocabulary

Several words meant more than one thing in earlier drafts, and some clashed with BYOND built-ins. Each now has one meaning here, and the other uses have their own names. The table is the place to look when a word seems to be used two ways.

WordThe one meaningWhat the other uses became
partOne unit of op behaviour, acting in one or more of the four op stages (section 9).A machine's installed components are components(slots), req_component(T) and component_swap. A layer of a look is look.layer() and look_layer().
stageAn op stage: Match, Require, Wait or Do. A build stage is a rung of a construction ladder (stage(), STAGE_*, section 12).The construction step() is stage(), which no longer clashes with BYOND's step().
act, actionA /datum/act context: an op or a world action, anything that can be refused (section 8).The verb-panel entry that lists gameplay abilities is "Abilities".
holdThe verb hold(): a runtime contribution to a stat, kept by a source (section 5).What a provider or storage takes is accepts =. The item in the actor's hand is A.held.
statA value composed from contributions by one rule, declared with STAT(T, name, RULE). Body factors (BF_*) are stats too, as STAT_* ids.BYOND's stat() and /mob/var/stat are not ours to reuse, which is why the declaration is an uppercase macro.
settingA tracked var that a player or an admin chose (TRACKED, toggle_setting()).The configurable values of a capability are its params. The setting() entry is gone.
keyAn op key is a literal string, a capability key is CAP_X, a tag is TAG_X. The build keeps the three kinds apart.Stats, capability state, sources and build stages have their own ids (STAT_X, COVER_OPEN, SRC_X, STAGE_X).
sourceWhoever holds a contribution to a stat or a grant: a datum, or a SOURCE_DEF flyweight such as SRC_AI_CONTROL. Text and null are errors.The context's A.source is the same thing. A lasting hold by one actor uses that actor as its source.
condition, requirementA condition is a boolean. A requirement is a condition plus a reason. needs() takes requirements; when() and the rest take conditions (ground rule 6).req_stat, req_set and req_clear are req_is() and req_at_least().
capability, bundleA capability is a named, configurable bundle of entries that something can address by key (configure, extend, without, grant). A bundle is any proc returning entries or parts.Wrap a bundle in a capability only when someone must address it. machine_basics, wall_machine and maintenance_hatch are capabilities.
system/datum/system: world-level state and periodic work owned by the kernel (section 2).The old "sys layer", world services and subsystems are gone. Host machinery such as the database and tgui transport is made of systems.
notice, hookA notice announces that something happened (/datum/notice/<past tense>). A hook is a handler attached to an action, a notice or a state change (section 10).There are no OM events and no signals.
route, affordance, intentThe route is how an actor reaches a target (ROUTE_*). An affordance is what a provider adds beyond the route (AFF_*). An intent is what a gesture means (INTENT_*).The old ACT_* gesture names are INTENT_*. AFF_INTERFACE and AFF_TK are dropped; the route says it.
on =Where an effect lands: ON_TARGET (default), ON_HOLDER, ON_ACTOR, ON_HELD. while_slotted() adds ON_CONTENTS.ON_SELF never had a definition and is gone.
request, prompt, askA request is anything that waits for an answer. A prompt is a request a player answers. asks() is how a trigger makes one; request() is how code outside a trigger does.io_request(), ask() and the OM prompt calls are one form (section 2).

The model

Six ideas cover the whole API. Everything else in this document is a constructor for one of them, or a library built from them.

Entries

What a type declares, in one inheriting list per type: CAPABILITIES(T, ...). Capabilities, ops, relations, triggers, outputs, look layers. Built once per type and interned. Identifiers (stats, actions, sources) are declared once, each with its own macro.

Parts

The unit of behaviour inside a trigger. Each part acts in one or more of four op stages: Match, Require, Wait and Do. Interned values that nest into bundles.

Stats

A value composed from contributions by one rule, declared once with STAT(T, name, RULE). Its effective value lives in a plain var that only the engine writes, and recomputes inline the moment anything it depends on changes. Built on the existing OM contribution store.

Sources

Whoever holds a runtime contribution: a datum (an item, a spell, an admin, a capability's op) or a named SOURCE_DEF flyweight. One source holds once. Releasing or deleting it takes back everything it held, except timed holds, which run their course.

Triggers

Things that run parts. Ops (chosen by an actor), actions (refusable world events), notices (it happened), changes, timers and periodic work. Every trigger has a typed context, and every handler takes it: x(datum/act/A).

Systems

World-level owners of global state and periodic work, scheduled by the kernel with boot order, budgets and membership. They replace subsystems, world services, world lanes and the OM scheduler. The host machinery (input, database, tgui transport, garbage, air, lighting) is made of them too.

client input→input inbox (typed event)→gesture→bind profile + stance→intent (INTENT_*)→candidates: target, held item, actor→1 Match: actor gate, reach gate, route, provider, when→priority→2 Require→3 Wait (cancels early)→4 Do→notice
setter / hold / grant / relation write→mark readers dirty (only if something reads it)→dependent stats recompute inline+drain: transition conditions, then on_change hooks, then outputs queue→phase R: draw, ui_data, push_to_rust, outputs()

Execution model. Writes are immediate. A write marks its readers dirty, in time proportional to the number of readers, and publishes nothing that nobody reads. A stat recomputes eagerly and inline: when a contribution, a hold or a read it depends on changes, it recomputes at once, in rank order, before the writer's next line, so a stat var read is always current. Transition conditions, hooks and outputs run at a drain, in a rank the build assigns from the generated reads graph (tracked vars, then stats, then conditions, then hooks, then outputs). The drain runs at the end of the current trigger and at the start of phases D, P and R, and phase R runs only outputs. A cycle among stats and conditions is a build error, and an iteration cap per drain logs the key chain and spills the rest to the next tick. Section 7 has the full model.

Which form do I use

You needUseSection

Ground rules

  1. One form per job. Each need has exactly one form, listed in this document. A second form is a defect: the lint for the old one is shrink-only, and reaches zero by the end of the migration.
  2. Every key is a declared identifier. Stats, tracked vars, capabilities, capability state, sources, build stages, actions and messages are declared once, by a macro that generates their id. A string key (an op key, a timer key) is a literal the build step resolves. An unresolved key is a build error, and there are no computed keys.
  3. Declarations are pure and per type. Entries never read instance state. Anything that depends on an instance is a condition (when(), a contribution's condition) or a grant, evaluated at runtime. A capability's param can name a var or a proc, which is resolved per instance at runtime.
  4. State changes only through setters, stats, holds and relation accessors. These are the only things that publish. Raw writes to tracked state are build errors.
  5. Reads are generated, never typed. The build derives what every condition, requirement and output reads, including reads through A.actor, A.held and A.target, and through global helpers annotated READS_FROM(arg). The only hand-written reads are READS_AS, for a producer key that is not a var, and that READS_FROM annotation. Capability state needs neither.
  6. Conditions and requirements. A condition is a boolean. A requirement is a condition plus a reason, so a refusal always says why. needs() takes requirements; when(), contributes(), every(when =) and the rest take conditions. Both are pure: test builds run them under the same no-write, no-publish, no-message guard as outputs, so menus, examine and early cancels can ask at any time.
  7. One handler signature. Every proc the engine calls takes (datum/act/A): effects, requirements, conditions, hook handlers, timers, every() work and request callbacks. A.holder is always set. Section 9 has the table of which fields each place sets.
  8. Every effect runs inside a trigger. Effects never sleep. Waiting is a wait() part, a timer (after(), delayed()) or a request (asks(), request()). Only the kernel and the request layer suspend a chain. There is no spawn, gameplay sleep, INVOKE_ASYNC or do_after.
  9. Global state lives in systems. There are no new globals, subsystems, world services, Master Controller or fire(). The host machinery is systems too.
  10. Memory scales with distinct declarations, not with types or instances. Everything declarative is interned, and a type that adds nothing shares its parent's compiled tables.

1Declarations

#define CAPABILITIES(T, entries...) // registers T's entry list with the table builder: no instance is created, and there is no ..() to forget
  • One list per type. A type's entries are in one CAPABILITIES line. A second line for the same type is a build error. A one-line data-only subtype is just CAPABILITIES(T, configure(...)).
  • Inheritance is automatic: the builder compiles each type from its parent's compiled table plus its own list, so the parent's entries always come first and ancestors are not re-run per type.
  • Build time: the table builder walks the list once per type, interns every entry, and splits it into the runtime indexes: op resolver, trigger index, relation table, stat table, look layers and the verb table (for verb_entry() commands). It records __FILE__:__LINE__ for every entry, and test builds keep an origin list per interned entry, so the explain tools can say where anything came from (section 15).
  • Sharing: a type whose list adds nothing shares its parent's compiled tables.
  • Boot: capabilities initialize inside Initialize's ..(), before the type's own code that follows it. Initial stat values are computed silently, with nothing published, until map load completes.

Declared identifiers

Entries compose behaviour. The names they refer to are declared once, on their own lines, and each declaration generates the id. Nothing refers to a stat, a source or a build stage by an undeclared string.

IdentifierDeclared byWritten asSection
StatSTAT(T, name, RULE, ...): declares var/name, its default and the idSTAT_NAME5
Tracked varTRACKED(T, a, b, c): generates set_<v>() for each varnameof(v) on src4
Per-instance listLIST_STATE(T, var, LAZY | ROWS | SET | TABLE): the var and its allocation policythe var6
CapabilityCAPABILITY_DEF or CAPABILITY_TYPECAP_X11
Capability statecap_keys(CAP_X, KEY = MSG(...)): ids, accessors, reasons and read registrationCOVER_OPEN; the accessor <cap>_<key>(holder), such as cover_open(holder)11
Opa literal in op("key", ...)"key", or "cover.open" for a capability's op9
Taga TAG_X defineTAG_X9
Sourcea datum, or SOURCE_DEF(name)SRC_NAME5
Build stageSTAGE_DEF(apc, secured)STAGE_APC_SECURED12
ActionACTION(fall, turf/landing, mob/living/landed_on)/datum/act/fall, act_fall(), /datum/notice/fell8
MessageMSG_DEF(path, text)MSG(path)13
SystemSYSTEM_DEF(name)SSname2
  • Op keys are literals. A capability's ops are namespaced automatically: cover()'s "open" is "cover.open". A type's own ops are bare ("breaker").
  • Capability keys are (CAP_X, discriminator). The discriminator defaults to the first discriminating param: a bay, a var, a slot id or a system. An identical repeat (the same interned signature) dedupes. The same key with different params is a build error naming both lines.
  • Three kinds of key: op keys, capability keys and tags are distinct. The build resolves every literal reference in extend, without, configure, performs, perform_op, on_op and priority(above()). perform_op with an unknown key at runtime is an error, not a no-op.
  • Sources are a datum or a SOURCE_DEF(ai_control) flyweight (SRC_AI_CONTROL). Text or null is an error. An op's holds() and grants() default their source to the op itself (holder plus op key), owned by the target's store. source = SRC_HELD_ITEM opts in to "the item owns it", and an op with consumes() and an item-sourced hold is a build error. A per-actor source (source = A.actor) is the pattern for "this AI's bolts". Section 5 has the full rules.
  • Typed names elsewhere: sequence facts are typed vars on the sequence's context (F.alive), request fields are vars on the request datum set by named args, and UI args arrive as typed proc params (set_channel(datum/act/A, channel, mode)). The build checks the names against the declarations.
  • nameof(v) stays for tracked vars and relations on src. A hold or contribution on a type that does not declare the stat is an error naming the type and the stat.
ACTION(fall, turf/landing, mob/living/landed_on)     // a generator marker, not a plain macro. The build step emits /datum/act/fall,
                                                     // act_fall(holder, landing, landed_on) and the notice /datum/notice/fell

var/datum/act/fall/F = ACT_TRY(src, fall, T, M)      // the context; ACT_PASS (nothing hooks it, nothing allocated); or null (refused or taken over)
if(!F)
	return
...do the fall...
act_done(F)                                          // publishes the notice. act_cancel(F) releases without publishing

The notice is named for the verb in the past tense (/datum/notice/fell, inserted, buckled); override it with notice =. A lint requires act_done or act_cancel on every path of the proc that called ACT_TRY. Section 8 lists the world actions.

Precedence

The same order applies everywhere, lowest to highest:

  1. library defaults (tool profiles, a capability's own parts);
  2. the op's own parts;
  3. group extends (extend(CAP_X | TAG_X | /datum/act/x)), in list order;
  4. extend("key").

At each level a subtype beats its parent. When candidates come from different entities, ties go target first, then held item, then actor, then subtype before parent, then capability declaration order. Requirements always accumulate, so no level can drop one that another level added, except by naming it (extend("key", drop = "id")). Single values (a wait, a priority, a message) take the most specific declaration. without(key) applies at its position in the list.

Capabilities granted at runtime (by a species, a trait, while_slotted or a body effect) are a second table beside the type's own, with the same precedence, resolved when the click happens. extend(CAP_X, ...) addresses a capability by key, so it applies to a granted one as well.

Interning and memory

  • Flyweights. Parts, requirements, ops and capabilities are interned by signature, so tool(TOOL_MULTITOOL) is one datum everywhere. That's already true today for capabilities and /datum/req.
  • Per-instance cost: stat vars; one bitfield per boolean stat for contribution results; the hold list, allocated only while something is held; per-instance capability data (a typed /datum/cap_data/<cap>), allocated on first use; relation vars; and the lists declared with LIST_STATE.
  • Bit limits. DM numbers are exact to 24 bits, so a stat's static-contribution bitfield and a capability's state keys each fit 24 bits. More is a build error naming the stat or the capability.
  • Procs stay off base types. Library behaviour lives on capability datums or global procs taking the holder, never on /atom: each /atom proc costs about 0.5 MB, and B2 measured 13 MB saved for 27 procs.

Replaces:

  • Declaration procs: capabilities(), reactions(), relations(), ownership(), derived(), declared_capabilities(), CAPABILITY().
  • Macro families: DECLARE_INTERACTIONS and EXTEND_INTERACTIONS, every lifecycle DECLARE_*, REGISTRY_MEMBERSHIP, DECLARE_VERB*, DECLARE_APPEARANCE*, DECLARE_UI* and MAP_RESOLVER (as an entry).
  • Entries that were never one form: stat(), setting(), derive() and derive_<v>(), capability(KEY, entries..., settings = list()), and hand-written READS_AS for capability state.

2Kernel and systems

The tick

One kernel replaces the Master Controller, SSbehaviours (the OM scheduler), world lanes and the subsystem fire() model. Each server tick runs fixed phases under one budget:

PhaseRuns
K hostHost systems, in a fixed order: the input inbox first, then the database, tgui transport, garbage, ticker and lighting
N nativeThe native frame: the air system (the LINDA and Rust atmos driver), then the verdigris system's one vg_frame(elapsed, budget); its outbox becomes publishes, notices and band crossings
U urgentReserved slice for kernel_urgent() and on_cross(..., urgent = TRUE)
D deadlinesDue timers (after()), hold expiries, op waits ending
P phased workevery() work items and sequences (Life), ordered by runs_after edges, paid from lane budgets
R presentationQueued outputs, once per entity: draw, ui_data, push_to_rust and outputs() (looks, UI pushes, HUD, Rust pushes). Nothing else runs here
G garbageDeferred deletion work
  • The drain (section 7) runs at the end of every trigger and at the start of phases D, P and R. Hooks and outputs wait for it.
  • Lanes (LANE_SIMULATION, LANE_PRESENTATION, LANE_WORLD, ...) split the budget.
  • Latency classes L0–L3 decide what's shed first under load. L0 is never shed: the input inbox, the host systems and urgent work. A system's class defaults from its lane.
  • Metrics: every work item, step and system reports its cost through metrics() into the kernel profiler, which replaces the OM profiler.

Systems

SYSTEM_DEF(power)                               // /datum/system/power, reachable as SSpower
/datum/system/power
	needs = list(/datum/system/atmos)              // boot order: one graph, validated (cycles are init errors)
	roles = list(POWER_ROLE_LOAD, POWER_ROLE_SOURCE)  // the membership roles this system accepts
	lane = LANE_SIMULATION
	latency_class = LATENCY_L2

CAPABILITIES(/datum/system/power,
	every(MACHINE_SERVICE_INTERVAL, then(PROC_REF(balance))),          // world-level periodic work
	every(2 SECONDS, then(PROC_REF(step_member)), members = CAP_POWERED_BY), // once per member; the member is A.target
	on_notice(/datum/notice/round_started, then(PROC_REF(reset_grid))),
)

/datum/system/power/proc/balance(datum/act/A)
/datum/system/power/proc/step_member(datum/act/A)
/datum/system/power/proc/reset_grid(datum/act/A)

/datum/system/power/initialize()           // boot, after everything in `needs`
/datum/system/power/on_members_ready()     // after map members joined
/datum/system/power/on_shutdown()
  • Access: the SSx name that SYSTEM_DEF(x) generates. system(/datum/system/x) returns the same datum for generic code and tests. A system's state is VAR_PRIVATE; other folders call its api.dm procs only (system_boundary_lint).
  • On demand: a system with no work parks its items. every(when =) wakes it.
  • Membership is a relation, not a roster:
    • join(system, E, source, role) and leave(system, E, source, role);
    • members_of(key, role);
    • declared statically with membership(joins = ...).
    Roles are declared by the system with roles = list(...), and a join or a members_of with an undeclared role is a build error. A join lists the entity in members_of(key, role) and calls the system's on_member_join(E, role); leaving calls on_member_leave(E, role). Joins count sources: one source joins once, the entity stays listed while any source remains, and removal is O(1). A static membership() joins at holder init and leaves at destroy, or when its when() stops holding. A registry (REGISTRY_*) is a membership key with no system. A member passes no callbacks: a system reads its members through its own api (for example power_draw(E)). powered() is a capability over powered_by(system, role).

Host systems

The machinery that wraps BYOND and the process is made of ordinary systems, declared with SYSTEM_DEF, with phase = KERNEL_PHASE_K (or KERNEL_PHASE_N) and latency_class = LATENCY_L0:

SystemPhaseOwns
inputKThe input inbox and client/Topic (below)
databaseKThe connection, round records and schema check; /datum/io/sql as backend (below)
tgui transportKWindow pool, prewarm, assets, open, close and transfer (section 13)
garbageKThe GC queue and hard-delete accounting; deferred deletion runs in phase G
tickerKRound state
airNThe LINDA and Rust atmos driver, in the native frame
verdigrisNvg_frame and its outbox
lightingKThe lighting update queue
  • There are no subsystems and no Master Controller. SUBSYSTEM_DEF, fire() and SS_KERNEL_HOSTED are deleted, so "no subsystems" is literally true. The Master Controller's MC_* metric defines keep their math under kernel names: KERNEL_AVERAGE, KERNEL_RUNNING and the rest of KERNEL_*.
  • Vendored code (TGS and the like) lives in code/host/vendor/, exempt from every lint and from the layer rules (section 15).

The input inbox

One inbox replaces SSinput, SSverb_manager, the speech lane and the click holdback.

  • Every client entry point (Click and its modifiers, drag, client/Topic, verbs, tgui actions, say and emote, held keys) records a typed input event. There are no opt-in macros and no callbacks. The inbox owns client/Topic.
  • Order and budget: an event resolves on the spot if the input budget has room, and queues otherwise. Phase K drains the queue in arrival order under the input budget. Input is never shed.
  • Resolution happens at drain time. Each event goes through the action path (section 8) when it is drained, not when it arrived, so a target that has gone by then is dropped with the gate's reason.
  • Metrics: the tick meter's per-input wait metrics (arrival to resolution) live here.

Requests and waiting

asks(/datum/request/x, field = v, ...) // inside a trigger: a suspension point in the op's Do list (section 9) request(owner, /datum/request/x, then(PROC_REF(done)), field = v, ...) // outside a trigger; done(datum/act/A) gets the answered request as A.answer sql_write(query, params) // parameterized only; format_table_name() for tables; nothing waits on it await(datum/waiter W) // suspends the calling chain; only the kernel and the request layer may suspend waiter_resolve(W, result) / waiter_cancel(W)
  • One concept. /datum/request is anything that waits for an answer. Its kinds are /datum/prompt/* (a player answers), /datum/io/sql, http, rustg_job and path (a backend answers), and /datum/client_query/winget, measure_text and the like (a client round trip).
  • What they share: typed fields; entity fields held as handles that read null once the entity is gone; a timeout (a field of every request); a re-check when the answer arrives; cancellation when the owner dies; and metrics.
  • Fields are typed vars on the request datum, set by named args and checked at build time. A prompt takes answerer =. A.answer is the answered request, typed (var/datum/prompt/text/rename/R = A.answer). Section 13 lists the prompt kinds.
  • Field values. Inside a declaration (asks() in an op) a field value is a literal, nameof(var) (read from the holder when the op reaches asks()) or PROC_REF(x) (x(datum/act/A) is called then and returns the value). In request(), which runs in code, they are ordinary expressions.
  • SQL kinds declare their query once: query = "SELECT ... WHERE id = :book_id", where each :field binds the request's typed var of that name, and typed vars for the result columns, filled from the first row. A query that returns several rows also fills rows, one list per row (16.13).
  • Examples: asks(/datum/io/sql/load_notes, ckey = PROC_REF(actor_ckey)) in an op; request(src, /datum/prompt/yes_no/ghost_role, then(PROC_REF(role_answered)), answerer = ghost, role = R) from a system.

The database

  • One system. system(/datum/system/db) owns the connection, the round records and the schema check, with /datum/io/sql as its backend. Content never opens a query itself.
  • Content has two forms: asks() or request() with a /datum/io/sql/* kind (a typed datum holding its parameterized query and its result fields), and sql_write() for writes nobody waits on. NewQuery() and Execute() are deleted.
  • db_query_now() blocks its caller. It exists for the two places BYOND itself waits: world/IsBanned and the boot schema check. The lint allows it nowhere else, and accepts no ALLOW.
  • Login lookups are asynchronous. A client is "ready" when its data arrives, not when it connects.

Chunked work and paths

job(owner, PROC_REF(step), budget =, then =) // step(datum/act/A) returns JOB_MORE or JOB_DONE; the job dies with its owner request(bot, /datum/io/path, then(PROC_REF(have_path)), from = start, to = goal)
  • Chunked one-shot jobs: a long job that must give the tick back is a job(). budget caps how much of a tick one step may use, as a percent of a tick. The map loader, site generation, the POI queue and zone cleanup are its first users, and replace their stoplag() and INVOKE_ASYNC. The loader is a host job, because BYOND's New() may sleep inside it.
  • Pathfinding: /datum/io/path is a request kind served by a budgeted path system. Each request is its own record, so there is no mutex and no spin-wait, and bot AI can be a non-sleeping every() step.

Faults, parsing and boot

  • Faults: safe_call(PROC_REF(x), args...) runs a proc and returns a /datum/result (ok, value, error) instead of letting a runtime escape. It is for code at a boundary that can fail by design. try_parse_json(text) returns the parsed value, or null for malformed text. Tests use EXPECT_RUNTIME(...) to assert a runtime. Raw try/catch lives only inside the kernel, where it isolates a faulting handler.
  • Boot-phase blocking IO: before the first tick nothing is waiting on the tick, so a system's initialize() may block on IO. The database system's boot step reads the schema, and the admin and rank tables that are needed before the first tick, with db_query_now(), and shell() is allowed there too. After the first tick the lint rejects both; db_query_now() remains legal only in world/IsBanned.

Replaces:

  • The Master Controller, SUBSYSTEM_DEF, fire() and SS_KERNEL_HOSTED; every subsystem, including SSinput and SSverb_manager (now the inbox).
  • SSbehaviours; OM lanes and world_lanes.dm.
  • /datum/world_service, GLOB.x_service, LAZY_SERVICE, boot_after and order_after.
  • /datum/cadence, CADENCE_* and PERIODIC_*.
  • System rosters (system.members, cap_system).
  • Requests: om_io, io_request, the ask() calls outside triggers, om_sql_write (now sql_write), NewQuery() and Execute().
  • Hand-rolled yields: stoplag() and INVOKE_ASYNC in the map loader, POI queue and zone cleanup (now job()); the pathfinder mutex.
  • Bare try/catch outside the kernel (now safe_call and try_parse_json).
  • The OM profiler.

3Time

Clocks

ClockMeaning
CLOCK_WORLDReal server time. Never paused.
CLOCK_OWN (default)The entity's own time. Pauses while it's suspended; runs at its clock_rate stat.
CLOCK_BIOBiological time. It runs at clock_rate times clock_rate_bio, so stasis slows or stops it on top of the entity's own clock. Stasis sources hold clock_rate_bio, a MIN stat with base 1, so the deepest stasis wins.

A clock's rate is a stat, so stasis beds, cryo, bellies and admin freezes are holds on that stat, not special code paths: while_slotted(SLOT_BUCKLE, holds(STAT_CLOCK_RATE_BIO, 0.1), on = ON_CONTENTS) on a bed, or hold(M, STAT_CLOCK_RATE_BIO, 0, source) with the admin as source.

A timed hold's lasts runs on the clock the hold names (clock =, default CLOCK_OWN). A hold on a clock-rate stat always runs on CLOCK_WORLD, because a stasis hold timed on the clock it slows would never end. There is one deadline per entity per clock. Every remaining-time read (hold_left, after_left, COOLDOWN_TIMELEFT, status_remaining) returns deciseconds on the clock of what it reads.

The forms

There are five time forms: after(), every(), hold(lasts =), COOLDOWN and STAMP/ELAPSED. Everything else here is a value they take, or belongs to another section. Named intervals such as MACHINE_SERVICE_INTERVAL and LIFE_CYCLE are plain defines.

NeedFormNotes
Do something once, laterafter(owner, delay, then(PROC_REF(x)), key =, clock =, with =); cancel_after(owner, key), after_pending(owner, key), after_left(owner, key)Stored as a timer relation: dies with the owner. x(datum/act/A) runs with A.holder = the owner. A keyed timer replaces an earlier one with the same key. Keys are literals, and a cancel_after on a key no after declares is a build error. with = list(...) arrives as A.args; it is for scalars and flyweights only, a datum in it is a lint error (reach entities through a relation), and the default is null, so nothing is allocated.
Do something later, inside an opdelayed(t, parts...)after() on the holder's clock, owned by the holder, so it dies with the holder (section 9).
Do something repeatedly while a condition holdsevery(interval, parts..., when =, lane =, key =), ordered with runs_after =interval is a number or a PROC_REF (computed per run). when is a condition: its reads are generated, and the work parks and wakes by itself. The handler gets A.dt, the time since its last run, so scale work by it. An entity's every() runs on its own clock (CLOCK_OWN). key = lets a subtype retune or drop it with extend("scan", ...) or without("scan"). Inside a system, members = CAP_X runs it once per member, with the member as A.target.
State that lasts a whilehold(E, STAT_X, value, source, lasts =)Section 5. Re-applying follows the stat's reapply rule. A timed hold outlives its source by default; an untimed one dies with it.
Rate limit, nothing reacts to itCOOLDOWN_DECLARE / COOLDOWN_START / COOLDOWN_FINISHED; for keys that are data (ability ids), COOLDOWN_KEYED_DECLARE / COOLDOWN_KEYED_START / COOLDOWN_KEYED_FINISHEDNo timer, one compare, defined as sugar over STAMP/ELAPSED. The keyed form is a lazy table that prunes itself, and the one place a key is data rather than a declared identifier. If anything must react when it ends (a UI "ready" light), use a hold instead. A per-op rate limit is the cooldown(t) part (section 9).
"How long since"STAMP(v) / ELAPSED(v)The one timestamp form.
A timed player actionA wait(t) part on the opRe-checks everything when it ends, and cancels early when a requirement now refuses (section 9).
A random durationbetween(min, max)Usable wherever a duration is: lasts =, wait(), every(). It rolls when it is applied (each run, in every()), never at declaration, because declarations are interned and pure.
The time on an entity's clockclock_now(E, clock)Replaces om_clock_now.
A long job that must yieldjob(owner, PROC_REF(step), budget =, then =)Section 2.
An answer from a player or a serviceasks() / request(), with their timeoutSection 2.
Coalesce a noisy outputoutputs(..., at_most = t)At most one run per t per entity; held changes arrive together.
Must run before the next tickkernel_urgent(member, work, deadline); on_cross(..., urgent = TRUE)Reserved slice, kernel and systems only; a breach is a metric.
Per-entity ordered frames (Life)A /datum/sequence with seq_step() entriesBelow.

The old spellings, and what each became

The old forms table had 14 rows. Each is listed here with what it became; two more spellings lived in section 9.

Old spellingBecame
1. after(owner, delay, then(...), key =, clock =, with =), with cancel_after, after_pending, after_leftKept as after(). Keys are literals the build checks, and with = is scalars and flyweights only, delivered as A.args.
2. every(interval, parts..., when =, members =, phase =, after =, budget =, lane =)every(interval, parts..., when =, lane =, key =) with runs_after = for order. after = is runs_after =. members = remains only inside a system. phase = and budget = are gone: the lane sets the budget.
3. hold(..., lasts =)Kept. Re-apply follows the stat's reapply.
4. COOLDOWN_DECLARE / START / FINISHEDKept, as sugar over STAMP/ELAPSED, plus the COOLDOWN_KEYED_* family for data keys.
5. STAMP(v) / ELAPSED(v)Kept: the one timestamp form.
6. wait(t) partKept, as the Wait stage of an op (section 9).
7. lifetime = N / expire(N)expire(N), the lifecycle verb (section 6), which is an after() underneath. The type var is gone: a type that always expires calls expire(N) from Initialize. It works on any entity.
8. between(min, max)Kept, as a value that rolls when applied.
9. clock_now(E, clock)Kept.
10. callable(owner, PROC_REF(x), args) / invoke()Deleted. then(PROC_REF(x)) is already a deferred call, and requests carry their own typed fields.
11. after_init(delay, parts...)Stays as the entry for an after() armed when the instance initializes. It is not a separate mechanism.
12. output(..., at_most = t)outputs(PROC_REF(x), at_most =, when =) (section 7).
13. request_urgent / on_cross(..., urgent = TRUE)Kept for the urgent slice, with request_urgent() renamed kernel_urgent() so that "request" means only /datum/request. Kernel and systems only.
14. seq_step in a /datum/sequenceKept for per-entity ordered frames. after = is runs_after = and the facts are typed.
Section 9: then_later(delay, parts...)delayed(t, parts...): the holder's clock, owned by the holder, dies with it.
Section 9: cooldown(t) partStays an op part. It is the same one compare as COOLDOWN.

Sequences

/datum/sequence/life
	interval = LIFE_CYCLE              // one frame per cycle of the mob's CLOCK_BIO
	max_catchup = LIFE_MAX_CATCHUP
	min_relevance = RELEVANCE_NEAR     // parked out of the sweep below this

/datum/seq_frame/life                  // the frame: pooled, a /datum/act subtype. Its facts are typed vars
	var/placed
	var/alive

/datum/seq_frame/life/compute_facts()  // once per frame, before any step
	placed = isturf(holder.loc)
	alive = holder.stat != DEAD

CAPABILITIES(/mob/living/carbon/human,                 // excerpt: the full list is in 16.4
	seq_step(PROC_REF(life_breathing), runs_after = LIFE_INPUT, when = PROC_REF(placed_and_alive),
		should_run = PROC_REF(life_breathing_due)),          // the reads of both are generated
)

/mob/living/carbon/human/proc/placed_and_alive(datum/act/A)
	var/datum/seq_frame/life/F = A
	return F.placed && F.alive
  • Ordering: steps are procs on the entity type, ordered by runs_after edges and anchors.
  • Sleeping: a step sleeps when should_run returns FALSE, and wakes when one of its generated reads publishes. A rewake timer is optional (rewake =).
  • Frames: the facts (placed, alive) are typed vars on the pooled frame, computed once per frame, and when is any condition over them. A step returns STEP_ABORT to end the frame.
  • Parking: a member is in the sweep only while it has an awake step and is relevant.
  • Missed wakes: the missed-wake audit fails test builds.
  • Outside the sweep: run_step_now(E, PROC_REF(step)) runs one step immediately (after a revive, say), through the same facts and gates. The rest of the sequence machinery is internal: facts are frame vars and plans are built once per type, so seq_condition, seq_replan and seq_plan_key are gone.

Replaces:

  • Timers: om_after and its variants (unique, replace, stagger, drift, list), after_slot, OWN_TIMER, om_cancel_timer, om_deadline (now internal), om_qdel_after (now expire), callable/invoke and om_callable/om_run, and then_later (now delayed).
  • Periodic work: PERIODIC_START, DECLARE_PERIODIC_WHILE(_ALL), DECLARE_REPEAT, periodic_cadence/periodic_interval/periodic_step, machine_step, MACHINE_WAKE/SLEEP.
  • Tasks: om_task_start, om_task_periodic, om_task_slices (the last is now job()).
  • Timed state: timed_set, EXPIRY_* (except the stamp), hand-written revert timers.
  • Life machinery: DECLARE_START_TIMER, OM pipelines, stages, plans, variants, string facts and seq_condition.

4Tracked state

Two concepts cover all state. Settings and plain state are vars one party writes: they are TRACKED. A stat is effective state that several things can force: a value composed from contributions by one rule (section 5). Two declarations refine them. cap_keys() generates a capability's state bits as typed keys, and LIST_STATE declares how a per-instance list is allocated. Nothing else holds state.

TRACKED(T, a, b, c) // variadic: for each var, generates T/proc/set_<var>(value): compare, write, mark dependents dirty STAT(T, name, RULE, ...) // composed state: declares the var, its base and the id STAT_NAME (section 5) cap_keys(CAP_X, KEY = MSG(x), ...) // capability state: generates the key ids, accessors and default reasons LIST_STATE(T, var, LAZY | ROWS | SET | TABLE) // a per-instance list with a declared allocation policy
  • Settings and plain state are ordinary var lines made TRACKED. The var line gives the default and TRACKED generates the setter. The lint rejects any write outside the setter, and admin edits go through it (vars_write, below). A setter has no hand-written body. The side effects of a change are an on_change hook (section 10), and a value that must be limited or normalised is a stat.
  • Settings vs effective state. A var is a setting (what someone chose) or effective state (what is currently true), never both. Effective state that more than one thing can force is a stat. The turret's enabled is a setting; its armed is a stat.
  • Base vars. Three base vars are tracked: anchored (its side effects are an on_change hook), broken (written only by atom_break() and atom_fix(), section 14) and client on mobs (published by Login and Logout). density and opacity are stats, because more than one source forces them.
  • Derived values are stats: STAT(T, v, FORMULA, formula = PROC_REF(f)) is one formula, evaluated in the stat layer, with generated reads. There is no separate derive form and no magic derive_<v>() name.
  • Capability state is declared once, next to the capability, and each fact exists once. emagged, locked and the cover and panel bits are keys of their capabilities, not machine fields as well. A bit that several things can force is a stat instead.
  • Keys that aren't one var (MOB_KEY_EQUIPMENT, OCCUPANTS_KEY) are published by their producer with PUBLISH_CHANGE(E, key). An accessor proc that stands for such a key declares it with READS_AS (section 7).

Capability state: cap_keys

cap_keys(CAP_COVER, OPEN = MSG(cover/closed), REMOVED = MSG(cover/removed))
// generates the ids COVER_OPEN and COVER_REMOVED, the accessors cover_open(holder) and cover_removed(holder)
// (always <cap>_<key>(holder): panel_open, lock_locked, emag_emagged),
// a default reason for each key, and the read registration
toggles(COVER_OPEN)   sets(COVER_REMOVED, TRUE)   needs(req_is(COVER_OPEN))   when(COVER_OPEN)
  • Storage: each key is a bit in its capability's own state word, held in per-instance capability data that exists once a bit is set. A capability has at most 24 keys (DM numbers are exact to 24 bits), and more is a build error. The id carries (capability, bit), so two capabilities never collide.
  • Publishing: a write publishes that key on the holder, if anything reads it. Reads are generated: no hand-written READS_AS for capability state.
  • Reasons: the message on a key is the default reason for the requirement the library itself writes on that key: req_is(COVER_OPEN) says the cover is closed, and req_is(COVER_REMOVED, FALSE) says it has been removed. A requirement written the other way round gives because = (section 9).
  • Writing keys: keys are written with the op parts toggles() and sets() (section 9) or through the generated accessors. Raw writes to the state word are build errors.

Per-instance lists and records

A list that really is per-instance and is neither a relation to other entities nor a constant table is declared, so the instance-list lint passes without an annotation. An undeclared var/list/x = list() on an instance is the only thing the lint still rejects.

PolicyStorageWritten withFor
LAZYNull until the first add, and null again when emptied. empty_differs = TRUE keeps an empty list distinct from null, for checks that treat the two differentlyrel_add, rel_remove, rel_clear (section 6)Logs, notes, per-mob tables that are usually empty
ROWSAn owned list of /datum/record rows (row = /datum/record/x)The owns_many writesBoard state, law sets, records
SETA list used as a set: adding a member twice is a no-oprel_add, rel_removePlain membership with no sources. When the members have sources (a language granted by a trait), it is a set stat (section 5) instead
TABLEA shared per-type table (TYPE_TABLE), copied on the first writerel_set to replace it; the first rel_add copiesSpecies and type defaults that an instance may rarely edit
 
LIST_STATE(/datum/mind, objectives, LAZY)
LIST_STATE(/obj, req_access, LAZY, empty_differs = TRUE)   // an empty list and null mean different things to access checks
LIST_STATE(/obj/machinery/computer/chess, board, ROWS, row = /datum/record/chess_square)

Writing a var by name

vars_write(E, name, value, source = SRC_VV) // the one by-name write: VV, SDQL, state serializers, the map loader, DNA and record copy
  • The only computed key. Every other key in this design is a declared identifier the build resolves. vars_write resolves its name at runtime because VV, serializers and the loader have nothing else, so a lint allows it only in those files, each with a reason code.
  • It routes by what the var is. A tracked var goes through its setter. A stat becomes a hold_override by source, so VV shows "override by <admin>" and the edit is removable. A relation or LIST_STATE var goes through the rel_* writes. A plain undeclared var is written as it is.
  • No restore-by-initial. Nothing writes initial(var) back to undo a change. A change that must be undone is a contribution or a hold, which is released and restores the composed value.

Which form do I use

NeedUse
A value only one thing setsTRACKED var
A value several things can forceSTAT, fed by contributes and hold
A value computed from other stateSTAT(..., FORMULA)
A bit of one capability's statecap_keys
An effect while a condition holdscontributes on a stat, or when(cond, entries...)
An effect for a periodSee the table in section 5
A list that changes per instanceLIST_STATE
A link to another objectref_one / ref_many / owns_one / owns_many / link
An object that sits inside the holderslot
Membership in a systemmembership / powered_by
React once when state changeson_change

Replaces:

  • OM_FIELD, OM_FLAG_FIELD, OM_DERIVE_FIELD, OM_FIELD_SETTER, 3-argument TRACKED, SETTER and hand-written setters, and TRACKED_BRIDGED. Until the machine and Life pipelines that consume the legacy channels are deleted, a TRACKED setter also raises the CHANGE_* channel recorded for it (section 19).
  • om_changed and changed(E, CHANNEL), the CHANGE_* channels, and the 368 manual *_changed() calls.
  • derive() with derive_<v>(), setting(), and from = lists on stats.
  • cap_set's catch-all publish, machinery fields duplicated as capability bits, hand-written READS_AS for capability state, and stat_add/stat_remove for owned bits.
  • Trait var_changes loops that write vars[] and restore with initial() (section 5).

5Stats, holds, grants and overrides

A stat is a value composed from contributions by one rule. Its effective value sits in a plain var that only the engine writes. It recomputes eagerly and inline when a contribution, a hold or a read it depends on changes, before the writer's next line, and it publishes when its value changes. The engine is the existing OM contribution store (contribution.dm): rows of (stat, source, value, expiry, key), timed and held entries, release on source deletion, combine rules, re-apply rules and composites. Old om_apply and om_hold callers and the new forms share that store, so they interoperate while the migration runs. This section is its final API.

Declaring

STAT(/obj/machinery, operable, ALL)                        // declares var/operable and the id STAT_OPERABLE (an int)
STAT(/obj/machinery, power_draw, SUM)                      // loads add
STAT(/obj/machinery/porta_turret, armed, ALL)
STAT(/mob/living, acts_via, MASK_AND, base = ROUTE_ALL)
STAT(/mob/living, clock_rate_bio, MIN, base = 1)           // stasis: the deepest source wins, not the product
STAT(/mob/living, stun, MAX, reapply = REAPPLY_MAX, units = LIFE_CYCLE)   // a status: numeric intensity
STAT(/area, lights_nightshift, ANY)
STAT(/obj/machinery/power/apc, charge_percent, FORMULA, formula = PROC_REF(compute_charge_percent))   // derived: one formula
  • One line declares everything. STAT declares the var, its base, its id STAT_<NAME> and its rule, so the var line and the rule can't disagree. It is a file-level declaration like TRACKED, not an entry. Body factors (BF_*) are STAT_* ids in this same namespace.
  • A stat belongs to its type. A contributes() line or a hold that names a stat the type doesn't declare is an error naming the type and the stat: a build error for a declaration, a runtime error for a hold. A subtype can't redeclare a stat with another rule (build error).
  • Base. The base is the value when nothing contributes. It comes from the rule (the table below) or from base =. A type changes it by setting the var in its own definition: light_range = 4 is a type-level contribution of 4 at default priority, read when the instance initializes. A map edit of a stat var becomes an instance contribution whose source is the map (the generated varmap, section 15).
  • Reads are plain var reads, and always current, because the stat recomputed inline when its inputs changed (section 7).
CAPABILITIES(/obj/machinery,
	contributes(STAT_OPERABLE, PROC_REF(has_power)),            // a condition: x(datum/act/A) returns TRUE or FALSE; reads generated
	contributes(STAT_OPERABLE, not(nameof(broken))),
	contributes(STAT_OPERABLE, req_built_final()),              // a library requirement's boolean view; its reason is not used here
)
CAPABILITIES(/obj/machinery/porta_turret,                    // excerpt: the full list is in 16.3
	contributes(STAT_ARMED, nameof(enabled)),                   // a tracked setting
	contributes(STAT_ARMED, STAT_OPERABLE),                     // another stat
)
CAPABILITIES(/obj/machinery/floodlight,
	contributes(STAT_LIGHT_RANGE, 4),                           // a fixed value on a numeric stat
)
CAPABILITIES(/obj/machinery/door,
	when(DOOR_OPEN, contributes(STAT_DENSITY, FALSE, priority = PRIORITY_FORCE)),   // TOP stat: an open door is not solid
)
// inside the absorbed capability that a belly grants:
contributes(STAT_ACTS_VIA, ROUTE_NONE, reason = MSG(vore/absorbed))   // MASK_AND: no route; the actor gate (section 8) shows the reason
contributes(STAT_X, cond | value | STAT_Y, priority =, reason =, key =) contributes_to(nameof(rel), STAT_X, cond | value, priority =, key =) // a contribution to a stat on a related entity
  • Conditions. A condition is boolean with no reason. On a boolean stat a contribution is a condition. On a numeric stat a PROC_REF(x) returns the number, and its reads are generated the same way, so it recomputes when they change. A requirement (section 9) is a condition plus a reason, and a contribution never needs one.
  • reason = is a message type shown by a gate that reads the stat (acts_via, can_act, operable). When the gate refuses, the strongest refusing contribution or hold supplies the message.
  • priority = orders contributions where order matters (TOP, and overrides). It is an integer. Named levels: PRIORITY_DEFAULT (0), PRIORITY_FORCE (100), PRIORITY_ADMIN (1000).
  • key = names the entry so a subtype can drop it with without("key") (section 9).
  • contributes_to puts a contribution on a stat of the entity a relation names, for example the APC's night-shift setting on its area's lights_nightshift. It is released when the relation changes or either end is deleted.
FormAccepted as a condition
nameof(v), STAT_X, a cap keyA truthy test of that var, stat or key
not(c), all_of(c...), any_of(c...)Combinators over conditions
PROC_REF(x), CAP_PROC(x)x(datum/act/A) returns TRUE or FALSE. A.holder is the entity. PROC_REF runs on the holder; CAP_PROC runs on the capability datum
A library requirement datumIts boolean view

Strings are never conditions. Outside a trigger the kernel passes a reused evaluation context with holder set, and conditions run under the same no-write, no-publish, no-message guard as outputs. A condition that matches something (a contribution's value, when() on an op, look_layer(when =)) is evaluated when it is read. A condition that drives a transition (an entry-level when() attaching entries, every(when =) starting work, an on_change edge, while_slotted) is evaluated at the drain (section 7).

The combine rules

RuleEffective valueBaseValue typeTie-breakVV override
ALLTRUE when every contribution allowsTRUEBooleanNone: every contribution countshold_override, either way
ANYTRUE when any contribution holdsFALSEBooleanNonehold_override, either way
SUMThe sum of the contributions0NumberNoneThe override replaces the sum; it does not add into it
PRODUCTThe product of the contributions1NumberNoneThe override replaces the product
MAX / MINThe extreme contribution, or the base when there are noneThe stat's base =NumberNoneThe override replaces the extreme
TOPThe value of the highest-priority contribution, or the base when there are noneThe stat's base =AnyHighest priority; then the most recentAn override is a contribution at PRIORITY_ADMIN
SETThe union of the membersThe empty setTokens (paths, ids)None: a set has no tiesGrant or revoke by the admin; no override
MASK_ANDThe bits every contribution allowsAll bits (base =)Bitmask, at most 24 bitsNoneThe override replaces the mask
MASK_ORThe bits any contribution sets0Bitmask, at most 24 bitsNoneThe override replaces the mask
FORMULAThe result of one formula proc, evaluated in the stat layerThe formula's ownAnyNone: it takes no contributionsThe override replaces the result until released

Body-factor rules map onto this table: MULT is PRODUCT, ADD is SUM, MAX and MIN are unchanged, and FLAGS is MASK_OR. The two indexed factor families become one stat per index: BF_ARMOR(kind) is a SUM stat per armour kind (STAT_ARMOR_BLUNT to STAT_ARMOR_BLAST), and BF_INCOMING(category) a PRODUCT stat per injury category (STAT_INCOMING_PHYSICAL, STAT_INCOMING_THERMAL, ...). The body domain's injury_armor(kind) is the one place that picks one of them by a kind known only at runtime. acts_via is MASK_AND: an actor can act through a route only if every contribution allows it.

Base stats, declared once

StatOnRuleBaseFed by
operable/obj/machineryALLTRUEPower, broken, build state, EMP and admin holds
power_draw/obj/machinerySUM0Each capability's load
can_act/mob/livingALLTRUEThe status stats (stun, paralysis, sleep), unconsciousness
can_move/mob/livingALLTRUEConscious and upright, restraints, grabs
acts_via/mob/livingMASK_ANDROUTE_ALLAbsorption (ROUTE_NONE), possession, remote control
density/atomTOPFALSEThe type's own density = TRUE, open doors, phase shift, lying down
opacity/atomANYFALSEThe type's own opacity = TRUE, smoke, closed doors
invisibility, light_range/atomMAX0Cloaks, phase shift; lamps, coatings, glow
suspended/atom/movableANYFALSEAbsorbed prey, bodies kept for reforming
clock_rate/atomMIN1Suspension, admin freezes
clock_rate_bio/mob/livingMIN1Stasis beds, cryo, bellies
stun, paralysis, sleep, .../mob/livingMAX, units = LIFE_CYCLE0Statuses (below)
  • One stun path. Statuses contribute to can_act and nothing holds can_act by hand: contributes(STAT_CAN_ACT, not(STAT_STUN)) on /mob/living. stunned is has_status(STATUS_STUN), not a second stat.
  • Density is a stat. A thing forced non-dense by phase shift, an open door or a collapsed mob needs a contribution to density, so it can't stay a tracked setting. anchored stays TRACKED (section 4). Side effects of a density or opacity flip (adjacency, lighting) are on_change hooks on the stat.
  • There is no busy stat. An actor's busy state is its pending op (section 9), a kernel record with a busy policy. A thing that is occupied for a while (a slot machine spinning, a scanner recharging) declares a stat named for that state, STAT(/obj/machinery/slot_machine, spinning, ANY), holds it with lasts =, and its ops refuse with req_is(STAT_SPINNING, FALSE, because = ...).
  • Clocks. CLOCK_OWN runs at clock_rate. CLOCK_BIO runs at clock_rate times clock_rate_bio, so stasis slows biology on top of the entity's own clock. Both stats take the deepest source (MIN), so two stasis beds never stack to 1%.
  • Base stat declarations live in code/base/ (section 22).

Holds: runtime contributions

hold(E, STAT_X, value = null, source = null, lasts = null, priority = PRIORITY_DEFAULT, clock = CLOCK_OWN, reason = null, bound = FALSE) hold_until(E, STAT_X, value = null, source = null, until =, priority =, clock =, reason =, bound =) // an exact deadline hold_override(E, STAT_X, value, source =, priority = PRIORITY_ADMIN) // replaces the composed value release(E, STAT_X, source =) // source = SRC_ALL drops every source's hold on the stat release_all(E, source =) // everything one source holds on E held_by(E, STAT_X) // the sources: a fresh list, safe to hold while releasing held_by_source(E, STAT_X, source) // TRUE if that source holds it; allocates nothing hold_left(E, STAT_X, source) // time left on a timed hold, in deciseconds of the hold's clock
  • The source is required. It is a datum or a SRC_* flyweight (below), and null or text is an error, so no hold is unreleasable. It is the fourth argument, and it is written named (source = x) wherever the value is skipped, as it is on a boolean stat.
  • Boolean stats take no value. On an ALL stat a hold is a veto (forces FALSE). On an ANY stat it forces TRUE. Passing the forcing direction is accepted; the other direction (TRUE on ALL, FALSE on ANY) is a lint error because it can never change anything. hold(src, STAT_BOLTED, source = SRC_AI_CONTROL) bolts the door.
  • Forcing the other way. hold_override is for any stat except SET. While an override exists the stat's value is the highest-priority override's value (ties: most recent). The contributions keep being tracked, so releasing the override restores the composed value. This is how an admin forces an ALL stat TRUE, or sets a SUM to 0.
  • Re-applying from the same source follows the stat's reapply = rule (below). One source holds once per stat.
  • Reasons. A hold on a stat a gate reads takes reason =, a message type. When the gate drops every candidate, the strongest refusing hold's reason is shown (section 8).
  • Admin edits of a stat var are hold_override by the admin, visible in VV and removable. They're never a raw write the engine would overwrite.
reapply =Holding again from the same sourceUse for
REAPPLY_MAX (default)Keeps the stronger value for the stat's rule and the later expiry. Never shortensStatuses, EMP, most holds
REAPPLY_EXTENDTakes the new value and adds the new duration to the time remainingDoses that stack in time
REAPPLY_REPLACETakes the new value and the new expiry; may shortenA mode forced by whoever called last

hold_until is exact whatever the stat's rule says: it replaces the value and sets the expiry, and it may shorten. It is what status_set and status_adjust use, so a timer can be reset or cut.

Clock and units

CallUnit
lasts =, until =Deciseconds on the hold's clock = (write 4 SECONDS). until is an absolute time on that clock: clock_now(E, clock) + n
hold_left()Deciseconds of that same clock. On a CLOCK_BIO hold it is biological time, and world time left is that divided by the clock's rate
status_* amounts, status_remaining()The status stat's units =. LIFE_CYCLE is 6 seconds of the mob's CLOCK_BIO, so stasis pauses every status
  • One deadline per entity per clock. An entity keeps at most three expiry queues (world, own, bio), each with one next deadline.
  • A clock-rate stat can't be held on the clock it feeds. A hold on clock_rate or clock_rate_bio takes CLOCK_WORLD only. A stasis hold on CLOCK_BIO would stretch its own duration and never end at rate 0, so that is an error.

Sources

SOURCE_DEF(ai_control)                                        // SRC_AI_CONTROL: a flyweight source, no strings
hold(src, STAT_BOLTED, source = SRC_AI_CONTROL)
hold(M, STAT_SLOWDOWN, 0.5, source = src, lasts = 4 SECONDS)   // a numeric stat takes a value
SourceUseAn untimed hold ends
A datum (capability instance, item, spell, mob)An effect tied to that thingOn release, or when the datum is deleted
A SRC_* flyweight from SOURCE_DEFA role or system that has no instance: AI control, a wire, SRC_STATUS, SRC_VVOn release
The op token (the default for an op's holds() and grants())This op's effect on this targetOn releases(), or when the target is deleted
A.actor (source = ON_ACTOR in an op part)Per-actor effects: this AI's boltsOn release, or when the actor is deleted
A slot entry (while_slotted, section 10)An effect while something is containedWhen the item leaves the slot: removed, transferred, spilled or deleted
  • Defaults. An op's holds() and grants() default their source to the op token: (holder, op key), owned by the target's store. The token names the holder without referencing it, so deleting the baton or the pill does not release the effect. source = SRC_HELD_ITEM opts in to "the item owns it". An op with consumes() and an item-sourced hold is a build error, because the item deletes itself at commit and would release its own effect.
  • Timed holds outlive their source. A hold with lasts = or until = runs its course when its source is deleted: a stun from a projectile that deletes itself on hit still lasts its 4 seconds. Untimed holds die with a datum source. bound = TRUE opts a timed hold in to dying with its source. Slot-entry sources are always bound.
  • Who owns a stat's holds. The stat's declaring capability or type documents it. The doors() capability, for example, declares bolted and owns it, and an AI's bolts are held with source = A.actor so each AI releases only its own.
  • SRC_ALL is a wildcard accepted by release() and status_end() only (a full heal).
  • Releasing a source with many holds. A release recomputes the stats it touches inline, like any write. A source holding more than HOLD_RELEASE_BATCH holds (default 256, a night-shift or admin hold across a z-level) releases in slices in phase G, coalesced per target, so a deletion never spikes a tick. The remaining holds still apply until their slice runs.
  • Toggling. toggles_hold(STAT_X, value, source =) (no value on a boolean stat) is an op part (section 9) that releases the source's hold if it has one and places it otherwise. It replaces the held_by() read-modify-write.

Statuses

status_at_least(M, STATUS_X, units, intensity = 1, source = SRC_STATUS) // at least this long; never shortens or weakens status_set(M, STATUS_X, units, intensity = 1, source = SRC_STATUS) // exact: hold_until; 0 units ends it status_adjust(M, STATUS_X, delta, source = SRC_STATUS) // adds to or cuts that source's time; at or below 0 ends it status_end(M, STATUS_X, source = SRC_STATUS) // source = SRC_ALL ends every source's hold status_remaining(M, STATUS_X) // units until it lapses on its own has_status(M, STATUS_X) immune_to(STATUS_X, when =) // an entry: a contribution that zeroes the status
  • A status is a numeric stat. A stat declared with units = is a status, and its id is also written STATUS_<NAME>. The effective value is the intensity (the strongest source wins under MAX), and the time left is the hold's expiry. has_status is "the effective value is above 0".
  • The sugar is defined over holds. status_at_least is hold(M, STAT_X, intensity, source = source, lasts = units * LIFE_CYCLE, clock = CLOCK_BIO) for a LIFE_CYCLE status, under REAPPLY_MAX. status_set is hold_until. status_adjust changes that one source's remaining time, and starts a hold from a positive delta if the source has none. status_end is release. status_remaining is the longest time left across all sources, in units.
  • Sources. Calls without source = use SRC_STATUS, so the roughly 700 existing callers share one hold per status and stack by REAPPLY_MAX as they do today. A cause that needs its own timer (a baton, a spell) passes its own source, and status_adjust and status_end then touch only that source unless SRC_ALL is named.
  • Immunity. Each status stat has a companion boolean ANY stat STAT_<NAME>_IMMUNE, generated by the same STAT line. While it is TRUE the status reads 0 and status_* calls and holds on it are refused. immune_to(STATUS_X, when = cond) is the entry that contributes TRUE to it, and a runtime immunity is a hold on the companion stat from a source (a mutation, an item). A hold that is already running keeps its clock while the immunity lasts.

Grants: holds on set stats

grant(E, what, source =, lasts = null, bound = FALSE) revoke(E, what, source =) granted(E, what)
You grantwhat (a typed token)Set it joinsRead it with
A capabilityA /datum/capability/x path (a type check, never text parsing)The instance's capability setgranted(E, /datum/capability/x)
A traitgranted_trait(TRAIT_X)The traits set stathas_trait(E, TRAIT_X)
A languagegranted_language(LANG_X)The languages set statgranted(E, granted_language(LANG_X))
An access idgranted_access(ACCESS_X)The access set statgranted(E, granted_access(ACCESS_X))
A client, OOC or admin verbgranted_verb(path)The verb set, applied by the verb storeThe verb store
Hide a verbhidden_verb(path)The hidden set. A hide beats a grantThe verb store
  • Typed dispatch. Each token routes to its own set stat, so a trait and a language with the same id can't collide. Grants take sources and expiry like any hold, and revoke is the release for a grant. The default sources "grant" and "join" are gone. join(system, E, source, role) and leave(...) for system membership (section 2) count sources the same way. observe() is a grant of a hook capability (section 10).
  • Capabilities. Granting a capability attaches its entries to that one instance: ops, triggers, contributions, look layers. Revoke or expiry detaches all of them. An instance's tables are interned as (type tables, sorted granted set), so a grant or revoke is a table-id swap plus the attach cost, and a click on a mob with six granted capabilities costs one index lookup. Hook and op candidates for an action are the target's type table plus every capability granted to it, with the same precedence (section 10).
  • Verbs. Gameplay abilities are ops with menu() (section 9), not granted verbs. The verb set holds client, OOC and admin commands only.
  • Traits. add_trait(E, T, source) is grant(E, granted_trait(T), source = source), remove_trait is the matching revoke and has_trait is granted. The three names stay as sugar. The separate trait store is what goes.
  • Effects on another entity. A lasting effect one entity puts on another is a granted capability, not a set of loose holds. The capability's entries carry everything the effect does, and revoking it, its expiry or the end of its source takes all of it back at once. A belly absorbing prey is the model: absorption moves the prey into the belly's second slot, which declares while_slotted(SLOT_BELLY_ABSORBED, absorbed(), on = ON_CONTENTS), so the slot entry is the source of everything the absorbed capability does (16.6).

Conditional entries

when(condition, entries...) // an automatic grant whose source is the condition

The condition's reads are generated, so the framework attaches and detaches the entries itself, at the drain when the condition flips. No code calls a "changed" proc. Species, preferences, worn items and modes all use this: when(PROC_REF(spont_vore_on), spontaneous_vore()), when(EMAG_EMAGGED, emagged_menu()).

Wrapping only ops, when(cond, op(...), op(...)) means the same as putting when(cond) on each op: they exist only while the condition holds. Both compile to one applicability check evaluated when resolution reads it, so nothing is attached or detached at runtime (section 9).

Domain APIs over stats and grants

Domain APIUnderneath
status_*, has_status, immune_to (kept)Defined above, over holds on numeric status stats
add_trait, remove_trait, has_trait (kept)grant, revoke and granted over the traits set stat
apply_body_effect(type, lasts, origin), remove_body_effect, has_body_effect (kept)An effect that only changes factors, with stacks, is a hold on a SUM stat (value = stacks). An effect that needs ticks or ops is a granted capability: grant(M, body_effect_capability, source = origin, lasts = lasts), with every() ticks and per-application state as capability data
factor(BF_X), factors = alist(...), species_factors, worn_factorsNumeric STAT_* ids with the table's rules. Reads are plain var reads. The body keeps its flat cache as the fast path until its sources contribute through declarations
set_stasis(level, source), om_suspend (kept as set_stasis)hold on STAT_CLOCK_RATE_BIO (MIN); hold(M, STAT_SUSPENDED, source = src)
Trait var_changescontributes() inside the trait's capability. A var a trait changes must be a stat (a numeric one with SUM, PRODUCT, MAX or MIN, a flag var as MASK_OR), so releasing restores the composed value instead of clobbering another source. A plain var is a build error naming it
operable(), incapacitated(), update_canmove()Replaced by plain reads: E.operable and !M.can_act. update_canmove() is gone, since can_move recomputes inline when its inputs change

For an effect that lasts, which form

NeedUse
A named condition for a while (stun, slow, jitters)status_at_least on a status stat

An effect while something is in a slotwhile_slotted (section 10)
An effect while a state holdscontributes or when(cond, entries...)
