A rate limit that nothing reacts toCOOLDOWN (section 3)
Something that must happen once, laterafter() (section 3)

Overrides, in one place

OverrideForm
A subtype changes a capability's paramsconfigure(KEY, param = value), or configure(KEY, variant = /datum/capability/x/variant) to swap in a subtype keeping the params
A subtype changes an opextend(key, parts...): requirements accumulate, single values replace. extend(key, drop = "id") relaxes a named requirement, and extend(key, replaces(parts...)) swaps the effect list
A subtype removes something inheritedwithout(key). Every entry takes an optional key = so it can be addressed
A subtype changes a statIts base: set the var. Add a contribution: contributes. Drop an inherited one: without("key"). Its rule: a build error
A map changes an instanceA map var edit on a capability param becomes per-instance capability data, and an edit of a stat var becomes an instance contribution (the generated varmap)
Runtime: something forces a valuehold on any stat (on a boolean stat the hold is the forcing direction), hold(..., priority =) on a TOP stat, or hold_override to replace the composed value
Runtime: something adds behaviourgrant(E, capability, source =, lasts =)
An admin edits a varvars_write, which becomes hold_override by the admin for a stat

Cost and limits

  • Per instance. A boolean stat keeps one bit per static contribution, and only the contribution whose reads changed is re-evaluated. A type can have at most 24 static contributions to one stat (DM numbers are exact to 24 bits), and more is a build error: group them with all_of or any_of. A mask stat has at most 24 bits. The hold list exists only while something is held.
  • Per change. A flip recomputes the stats that read it, inline and in rank order, which is O(dependent stats), and only the contribution whose reads changed is re-evaluated. Transition conditions, hooks and outputs are only marked for the drain (section 7).
  • At init each stat takes its base and its static contributions silently: no notices and no hooks run until map load completes, then one settling drain runs the transition conditions and hooks that have subscribers (section 7).
  • Reads of a stat in generated reads are like reads of any other var.

Replaces:

  • The contribution store's raw API: om_apply, om_hold, om_release, om_has, om_value_of, om_apply_until (now hold_until).
  • Grants: om_grant, om_grant_for, om_revoke, om_has_grant, the GRANT_* kinds, text-parsing grant_kind(), the default sources "grant" and "join", the relation ledger's grant counts, and add_trait/has_trait as a separate store.
  • The old stat forms: stat(nameof(v), RULE, ...) entries, from =, req_stat, and the BF_* ids.
  • Timed state: timed_set, timed EXPIRY_* state, emp_disable's expiry var and the EMPED bit.
  • Machine on/off state: stat bits as gameplay state, powered() as a gate, cap_powered(), is_lit(), update_canmove() and per-type on/active/enabled/disabled overloads.
  • Other parallel state: /datum/status_effect, the push and alpha source stores, hand-rolled re-enable timers, and the EFFECT_CAN_MOVE composite (now a declared stat).

6References and lifecycle

Relations

Every object-typed var is a relation, a flyweight, or local scratch. Relations are declared with flat constructors, one per meaning:

ref_one(nameof(v), type, on_other_deleted = CLEAR | DELETE_ME, on_unlink =, by = nameof(id_var)) ref_many(nameof(v), type, ...same...) owns_one(nameof(v), type, starts =, on_destroy = DELETE | SPILL | PRIVATE_COPY, copy_on_write = FALSE) owns_many(nameof(v), type, ...same...) link(/obj/machinery/power/apc::hacker, /mob/living/silicon/ai::hacked_apcs) // paired, declared once for both types link(..., hot = TRUE) // engine hot-path pair (lighting): no index, no publish
DeclarationMeaningWhen the holder is destroyedWhen the other end is deleted
ref_one, ref_manyA reference. The holder doesn't own the valueThe view is clearedon_other_deleted = CLEAR (default) drops it. DELETE_ME deletes the holder too. on_unlink = PROC_REF(x) runs x(datum/act/A), with A.target the other end, whenever a link goes: unlinked, replaced, or the other end died. It does not run for a holder being destroyed
owns_one, owns_manyThe holder owns the value or values. starts = is the starting occupant (below)on_destroy = DELETE (default) deletes it. SPILL drops an owned atom to the holder's location. PRIVATE_COPY deletes only a private copy and leaves a shared prototype aloneAn owned value has one owner. Deleting it from elsewhere clears the var
link(A::v, B::w)Both ends name each other. The pair is declared once, and the cardinality comes from the two vars (a list var is a "many" end). One write links both ends, and linking a new partner to a single end unlinks the old one on both sidesBoth sides are clearedThe other end clears
link(..., hot = TRUE)An engine hot-path pair (lighting corners and sources): direct lists on both sides, not indexed, nothing published, symmetric teardown declared once. A read through it is not reactive, and a lint allows it only under the engine and domain directoriesBoth sides are clearedThe other end clears
  • Keyed relations. by = nameof(id_var) makes a keyed reference. The holder and the target type both have a tracked var of that name holding an id, and the relation points at the live target whose var equals the holder's. It re-links when either side materializes, when the id is set, and when the target is deleted. A type without the var is a build error. This is the one keyed form: rel_key and keyed = are gone.
  • Owned or contained. Something that sits inside the holder in space (a cell, an occupant, belly contents) is a slot(). owns_* is for datums with no location (a reagent holder, wires, a prefs record). OWN_SPILL and OWN_CONTAINED are gone. on_destroy = SPILL on an owns_* var is for an owned atom that needs no capacity, filter or exposure.
  • Copy on write. copy_on_write = TRUE goes with on_destroy = PRIVATE_COPY. The var holds a shared prototype until a writer asks for a private copy with rel_private. With FALSE the value is only ever replaced whole by rel_set.
  • Reads: plain var reads. Reads through a relation (power_area.lights_nightshift) are generated as hops, and re-subscribe when the relation changes.
  • Flyweights: a var typed as a registry or flyweight type needs no declaration.
  • Internal relation kinds are the framework's own, never written by content: grants, listeners, members, timers and contained items.

Writing a relation: one verb set

Every write is type-checked (a value that is neither null nor an istype of the declared type is refused and reported) and publishes both ends. The verbs dispatch on the declared kind, so the caller never remembers how a var was declared.

WriteAcceptsWhat it doesOld forms it replaces
rel_set(E, nameof(v), x)ref_one, owns_one, a single link end, and a var typed as a registry or flyweight typeStores x (or null). The old value is cleared or disposed of by the declared policy. For a link, both sides are written. A registry or flyweight value is shared, never ownedrel_set, own_set, rel_link, rel_unlink (single end), proto_set, proto_replace, shared_set
rel_add(E, nameof(v), x, key =)ref_many, owns_many, a many link end, LIST_STATEAdds one member. key = puts it under a key in an associative owns_many, and replacing a key disposes of the old valuerel_add, own_add, own_put
rel_remove(E, nameof(v), x)ref_many, owns_many, a many link end, LIST_STATEDrops one member. An owned member is disposed of by on_destroy; use rel_take to keep itrel_remove, own_remove
rel_take(E, nameof(v), member = null, key = null)owns_one, owns_manyDetaches a value without disposing of it and returns it unowned. On a many, member = picks one member and key = one keyed member; with neither it detaches every member and returns them as a fresh list. The caller adopts or deletes what it getsown_take, own_take_member, own_take_all
rel_clear(E, nameof(v))Every kindEmpties the var. Owned values are disposed of by on_destroy, links are unlinked on both sidesrel_clear, own_clear, rel_unlink (all)
rel_move(old_holder, nameof(a), new_holder, nameof(b), member = null, key = null)owns_*Re-owns a value in one write, so it is never destroyed or orphaned on the way. member = or key = picks a member of a many; a many destination adds it (under the same key, for a keyed one)own_transfer, own_move
rel_private(E, nameof(v))owns_one(copy_on_write = TRUE)Returns the holder's private copy, making it on the first callproto_private, proto_copy
rel_is_private(E, nameof(v)), owner_of(x), is_registered(x)ReadsWhether the holder has a private copy; the one entity that owns x; whether x is a shared registry instance, which is copied before anyone writes to itproto_is_private, owner_of, is_registered

An accessor that isn't in this table is internal to the framework, and a lint bans it outside code/engine/. own_key, own_locate, entity_clone (the serializers' copy) and is_flyweight are internal. shared_set is rel_set on a registry-typed var. own_values is a plain read of the var (copy the list first if members may delete while you iterate). on_owned_release is on_unlink. keyed_set_id is the id var's own set_<var>(), since by = relinks when it changes.

Naming an entity in data

ref_token(E) // a generation-checked token for an entity resolve_token(token) // the entity, or null if it was deleted or its ref recycled
  • The rule. An entity never travels in data as a ref or a string. A var that holds an entity is a relation. A number, text or list that must name an entity, because a player can copy it or it is saved (an integrated-circuit pin, a task record, a saved reference), carries a token. A token is a plain value, safe to copy, compare and store within a round, and it never keeps the entity alive.
  • Resolving is the only way back: resolve_token returns null for a deleted entity, and a recycled ref never matches an old token.
  • The same rule covers with = payloads and request fields: scalars, flyweights and tokens only (section 3). Contexts and pending ops hold their entities through kernel relations, never raw refs (section 7).

Containment and slots

slot(id, accepts =, capacity =, exposure = SLOT_EXPOSURE_EXTERNAL, at = BAY_X, on_destroy = SPILL | DELETE | TRANSFER(resolver) | TO_LATENT | KEEP_WITH(slot)) move_into(holder, slot_id, item, actor = null) // puts item into the holder's slot, through /datum/act/insert slot_remove(holder, slot_id, actor = null) // takes the slot's item out, through /datum/act/remove slot_transfer(item, to_holder, slot_id) // moves a contained item straight into another slot, in one ledger write slot_empty · slot_item · slot_contents · slot_clear container_of(thing) · contents_of(holder, type) · locate_within(holder, type) · locate_on(turf, type) · is_inside(thing, container) · contents_count(holder) · FOR_CONTENTS(holder, x)
  • Slots are entries backed by the containment ledger. Storage, equipment, cell bays, occupants, belly interiors and mech hardpoints are all library capabilities built on slot(). at = BAY_X puts the slot in a compartment bay, so the reach gate (section 8) decides who can touch what is inside.
  • Equipping and inserting are actions (/datum/act/insert, /datum/act/equip), so a constraint such as "takes only cells" or "fits these body types" is a requirement: extend(/datum/act/insert, needs(...)), or accepts = on the slot, which is a type list or a requirement for what a list can't say.
  • A slot entry is a source. while_slotted(SLOT_X, entries..., on = ON_CONTENTS | ON_HOLDER) (section 10) places holds and grants with the slot entry as their source, and they end when the item leaves the slot, however it leaves (section 5).
  • No raw reads. Raw contents and loc reads are banned; use the spatial read API. container_of(thing) is what a thing is inside: the holder of its slot, or its turf.

Starting contents

starts = T | nameof(type_var) | list(T...) | pick_one(list) | PROC_REF(x) | when(cond, T) starts_args = list(...) // constructor arguments for what starts creates

starts is a parameter of owns_one, owns_many and slot, and of the capabilities built on them. It replaces every Initialize that exists only to create a child.

  • Forms. T is a type. nameof(type_var) names a var that holds the type, so a mapper can edit it: the APC's cell_type, which is the one way to change its starting cell. list(T...) is for a many or a slot with capacity. pick_one(list) is a random pick, weighted by the values of an associative list: pick_one(list(/obj/item/a = 3, /obj/item/b = 1)). PROC_REF(x) is for computed contents: x(datum/act/A) returns a type or a new instance. when(cond, T) starts it only if the condition holds at init.
  • Creation. Contents are created in nullspace and moved in through the relation write, so a created cell is never ejected by the move. starts_args are passed to the constructor.
  • Before the parent's init. Work that must exist before the parent type's init code reads it (a part made in nullspace, an inherited icon cleared) is the capability hook on_holder_preinit(A).

Lifecycle

MomentForm
Per-instance setupInitialize(mapload, ...) returning ..(), for per-instance state only. Starting contents come from starts =; capability setup from on_holder_init(A)
Placed in the world / removedon_materialize() / on_dematerialize()
After init, timedafter_init(delay, parts...) (an after() armed when the instance initializes, section 3)
Map-load-only atomsmap_resolver(PROC_REF(x)). Resolved atoms are never initialized
Random spawnsDECLARE_LOOT tables, loot_spawn(), loot_search() (kept; already declarative)
DeletingA verb when one fits: consume(item, actor), replace_with(new_type, args...), expire(N), slot_clear(slot), ledger_empty(). Otherwise qdel(). Never del(). replace_with creates a new_type where the holder is, passes args to its Initialize, then deletes the holder; it works across kinds, so an obj can become a turf (a girder raising a wall, 16.10)
Consequences of deletionon_destroy(force) calling ..(); lifecycle_prerelease() for teardown that must read declared vars; lifecycle_keep(force) to refuse. The destroy transaction clears relations, deletes owned values, cancels timers and holds, and closes UIs
Short-lived records/datum/pooled with take(type) / release(); fields reset to their initial values
Per-type constant dataTYPE_TABLE, shared caches (DECLARE_SHARED_CACHE, CACHED_*), variants (kept; caching tools, not state)

Order for one instance

StepWhat runs
1The map's var edits apply, and each type's Initialize runs its code before ..()
2The root of the Initialize chain: each stat takes its base and its static contributions, unevaluated and unpublished
3on_holder_preinit(A) of each capability, in entry order
4starts = contents are created and moved in
5on_holder_init(A) of each capability, with A.mapload set while the map loads
6..() returns, and each type's code after ..() runs. It sees every capability initialized
7When map load completes, one settling drain evaluates what has subscribers. Hooks and notices are silent until then (section 7)
8on_materialize() when placed in the world, on_dematerialize() when removed
9On deletion: on_holder_destroy(A) of each capability, lifecycle_prerelease(), on_destroy(force), then the destroy transaction

Replaces:

  • Ownership declarations: ownership(), OWN/OWN_SPILL/OWN_CONTAINED, PROTO, REL/REL_LIST/REL_PAIR/REL_PAIR_LIST/REL_KEYED, owns()/shares()/proto().
  • Typed relations: rel_one(...) and rel_many(...) with their ten parameters, RELK_* and back = (now link), rel_key and keyed = (now by =), watch = (reads are generated), /datum/om/relation/*, om_link.
  • The write families own_* and proto_*: they are the rel_* verbs above.
  • Starting contents: DECLARE_DEFAULT_CHILD, DECLARE_GAS (now gas_store()), DECLARE_BIND, starts_from and starts_at (now starts =).
  • Registries: DECLARE_REGISTRY and REGISTRY_MEMBERSHIP (now membership()).
  • Slots: /datum/slot_def, SLOT_DROP_HOLDER, the third requirement language for slots (hold_spec, HOLD_ONLY, REQ_* predicates).
  • Pools: POOL_DECLARE/POOL_RESET. Entity handles in data: om_handle and om_resolve (now ref_token and resolve_token).
  • Behaviours attached to a type with om_attach (now entries) or at runtime (now grant).

7Change tracking and dependencies

Writes are immediate. A write recomputes the stats that depend on it at once, inline, and marks everything else that reads it. The rest of the follow-up work, flipping transition conditions, running hooks and refreshing looks, UIs, the HUD and Rust pushes, runs at fixed points. Every dependent is a proc or a declaration whose reads come from its body.

The execution model

StepWhat happens
WriteA setter, stat change, hold, grant, relation write or capability key changes state at once. Through the generated reverse index it recomputes the stats that read the key (next row) and marks the transition conditions and hooks that read it for the drain, so the cost is O(readers). A key nobody reads costs one lookup.
RecomputeStats recompute eagerly and inline: when a contribution, a hold or a read a stat depends on changes, the stat recomputes at once, in build-time rank order, before the writer's next line (bounded by the cycle check and DRAIN_MAX_PASSES). DM has no property getters, so this is how a stat var stays a plain var read and is always current. Conditions used for matching are evaluated when read: when() on an op, look_layer(when =), menus and requirements, at resolution or output time.
DrainAt fixed points the kernel drains what is dirty, in rank order. The points are the end of the current trigger and the start of phases D, P and R. Conditions that drive transitions are evaluated here: an entry-level when() attaching or detaching entries, every(when =) starting or stopping work, while_slotted, a waiting op's requirements, and the edges of on_change. A flip applies its effect at once (attach, detach, start, stop, wake, cancel). Then the on_change hooks run, once per drain for the keys that changed. Outputs are only queued.
Phase RRuns outputs only, once per entity per frame: draw, ui_data, push_to_rust and outputs(). Outputs can't write state, so nothing in phase R can dirty anything.
RankWhatEvaluated
0Tracked vars, holds, relations, capability keysWritten by code, never evaluated
1Stats, including FORMULAInline, as soon as anything they read changes
2ConditionsOn read (matching) or at a drain (transitions)
3HooksAt a drain
4OutputsPhase R
  • Ranks come from the build. The generator gives each stat, condition and hook a rank one above the highest rank of anything it reads, from the reads graph. A cycle in the stat and condition graph is a build error that names the loop.
  • Hooks may write. A write inside a drain marks more readers dirty and never starts a nested drain: the running drain loops until nothing subscribed is dirty. It stops after DRAIN_MAX_PASSES (8). Beyond that the engine logs the key chain and spills the rest to the first drain of the next tick. A runaway loop is a logged error, never a hang.
  • Inline recompute is measured, not assumed. It pays at write time what a getter would have paid at read time. E3's phase 1 spike measures that cost on a fan-out case, one APC channel flip over 50 machines (apc_flip_50), against the old path (section 19).
  • Derived values live in the stat layer. A FORMULA stat is rank 1 and current at any read, so draw always sees settled values. Phase R has nothing left to settle.
  • Expiry and native changes are triggers of their own: a hold expiring in phase D, or a value arriving from Rust in phase N, marks readers dirty and a drain follows.
  • Initial evaluation is silent. At init each stat takes its base and its static contributions. Nothing publishes and no notice or hook runs until map load completes. Then one settling drain evaluates what has subscribers. Capabilities initialize inside Initialize, before the type's own code after ..() (section 6).
  • Notices are delivered synchronously when act_done() publishes, in publish order, with a depth cap of 8 beyond which an error is logged and the notice is dropped (section 8).

An example. An APC's equipment channel turns off. set_equipment() writes the tracked var, and inside that call each machine in the area whose operable reads has_power recomputes, in rank order: O(machines that read it). A machine whose operable flipped marks what subscribes to it (an every(when = STAT_OPERABLE) work item, an on_change(STAT_OPERABLE, EXIT, ...) hook). At the end of the trigger the drain parks the work and runs the hook. draw is queued and runs once per machine in phase R.

The roles

RoleWhat it isFormsRe-runs
OutputComputes something from state and applies it. It must not write tracked state.draw(look), ui_data(A), push_to_rust(), outputs(PROC_REF(x), at_most =, when =). examine(A) is on demand, not an outputOnce per entity per frame in phase R, after any read changed
ConditionA pure boolean that gates something (section 9).Stat contributions, when() (entry-level or on one op), every(when =), look_layer(when =), requirement procs, sequence steps' when =Matching conditions when read; transition conditions at the drain. A flip recomputes a stat, starts or stops work, wakes a step, cancels a wait, or redraws
Hook"This changed: do this once."on_change(cond, ENTER | EXIT | ANY, parts...)At the drain, once for the keys that changed. ENTER and EXIT fire on the condition's flip
KnobWhereDefault
at_most =, when =outputs(): at most one run per interval per entity, held changes arriving together; a condition that gates itNo limit; always on
lasts =, until =, clock =, priority =, bound =, reapply =Holds and stats (section 5)No expiry, CLOCK_OWN, PRIORITY_DEFAULT, FALSE, REAPPLY_MAX
DRAIN_MAX_PASSES, HOLD_RELEASE_BATCHEngine constants: passes per drain, and holds a dying source releases per slice8, 256

Publishing

  • Who publishes: setters, stats, holds, grants, relation writes, capability keys and the slot ledger. Native values arrive from Rust in phase N as CHANGED(entity, key).
  • Demand gating: a key is published only if something reads it, so an unread var costs one lookup.
  • Producer keys: a producer can publish a key that isn't a var with PUBLISH_CHANGE(E, key), and the key reaches the readers subscribed to E. An accessor proc that stands for that key declares READS_AS(proc, key). When the accessor reads through a relation, it names the hop: READS_AS(pad_occupied, OCCUPANTS_KEY, via = nameof(pad)). Readers then subscribe to the key on the entity the relation names, and the subscription follows the relation when it changes. The build checks that the accessor's own generated reads are covered by the key it declares.
// A global helper is followed through the argument it reads from:
/proc/cell_charge_percent(obj/item/cell/C)
	READS_FROM(C)
	return C ? 100 * C.charge / C.maxcharge : 0

// So this condition's reads are generated: the cell relation hop, and C.charge
/obj/machinery/power/apc/proc/cell_low(datum/act/A)
	return cell_charge_percent(cell) < CELL_BAY_LOW_PERCENT

Generated reads

LayerRule
GenerationA query in the analysis engine (section 15), run incrementally before the DM compile. It writes code/engine/_generated/reads.dm, which is never edited by hand. Keys are interned to integers at build time. Evaluation is one generated typed accessor call, and nothing uses vars[], call() or text keys at runtime.
CoverageEvery output, condition, contribution and hook condition named anywhere in a declaration.
Resolving a readThe holder's own vars. Hops through declared relations (ref_*, owns_*, link except hot, slot contents). Procs of the same type, and of a hop's type (followed). Global helper procs, followed through READS_FROM(arg). Accessor procs with READS_AS (not followed). Native keys (native("temperature")). Stat vars. The tracked vars of a system (SSx.var): a system is a singleton, so the hop is static and reactive.
Context hopsA.actor, A.held and A.target are typed hops, so a requirement that reads the actor's hands or the held item's charge is generated. They are subscribed per pending op and dropped when it ends. A wait or an open prompt re-runs its requirements when one of these reads publishes.
ConstantsA var the analysis engine proves is never written after init (a type constant, a capability param) may be read in a condition. It is not reactive, because there is nothing to react to.
Not reactiveA.args, world.time and GLOB reads never subscribe: a condition that depends on time uses every() or after(), and one that depends on a global reads it through a system. This is documented behaviour, not a gap.
Unknown readA build error with a fix hint: make it tracked, add a relation, add READS_FROM(arg) to the global proc, or add READS_AS. An unannotated global call inside a requirement, condition or output is the same error.
PurityThe generator rejects, in any followed body of an output, condition, requirement or formula, a call to a setter, hold, release, grant, revoke or rel_*, or an assignment to a tracked var. Test builds also run requirements, conditions and outputs under a no-write, no-publish, no-message guard, which catches what a static scan can't follow.
Standard outputsdraw, ui_data, push_to_rust and examine are base procs the engine calls by name. The build checks each override against the base signature, so a mistyped override is an error and not a silent no-op. Other outputs are declared with outputs().
Hand listsRejected wherever generation applies.
RuntimeIn test builds:
  • the drift audit re-runs outputs, and fails on a difference nobody published;
  • the missed-wake audit checks sleeping steps;
  • the condition audit re-evaluates stat contributions and pending ops' requirements.
In production the drift audit samples near players and corrects.

What a condition costs

CostRule
A writeAn inline recompute of the stats that read the key, in which only the contribution whose reads changed is re-evaluated, plus a mark for each transition condition and hook that reads it. One lookup when nobody reads it.
A readA stat read is a plain var read. A matching condition is evaluated when read, over stats that are already current.
A drainUnder the kernel budget of its lane. The evaluation count per drain is capped by that budget and the rest spills to the next tick, logged with the key chain. One APC channel flip across a large area recomputes its machines' stats in the writer's call, and runs the hooks of the machines that flipped at the drain, spilling across drains if the budget runs out.
Map loadInitial evaluation is silent: stats take their values, nothing publishes and no hook runs. One settling drain follows. The type tables are built once per type from the parent's compiled form, not by re-running its ancestors.
MeasuredThe bench scenarios for stat and hold churn, the output drain and 1,000 idle machines (section 19), plus a boot-time gate for map load and type-table build.

Contexts

Handlers take (datum/act/A) (section 8 declares the fields). This is when they run and what a context may do.

  • Where each runs. Effects, hook handlers and then() run inside a trigger, with a pooled context that lives for that trigger. Conditions, outputs, every() work and a requirement asked outside an op (a menu, an early cancel) run outside one: the kernel passes a reused evaluation context that allocates nothing. It has holder, and cap and dt where they apply, and no actor, held item or route.
  • Never stored. A context may not be kept in a var or a list, or passed in with = or delayed(), and a lint rejects it. A.snapshot() returns a plain list of its fields for anything that must outlive the trigger.
  • Failure. The kernel wraps each handler and releases its context if it runtimes. A production watchdog frees any context held over 2 minutes with no pending op behind it, and logs the creator.
  • Nesting. performs() opens a child context. Nested actions and notices stop at depth 8.

Replaces:

  • Hand-written dependency lists: derived() with runs_while, drawn_from, ui_from, rust_push, derive(var, reads...) and derive_<v>(), rel(), rel_each and factor_dep; rel_one(watch =); req_proc(reads =); seq_step(reads =); on_change read lists; B4's special output table; the hand-written READS_AS for capability state.
  • Output registration by name: output(PROC_REF(x), ...) (now outputs()).
  • Watches and notifications: om_watch, observe used for state, on_channel, on_state_changed().
  • Manual refreshes: refresh_hud(), refresh_vision(), update_icon(), queue_icon_update(), SStgui.update_uis(), and the 368 manual *_changed() calls.

8Actions and action paths

An action is anything that can be refused. There are two families:

  • Ops: chosen by an actor (a player, an AI controller, an admin).
  • World actions: started by code (a move, a fall, a hit).

Both run with a typed, pooled context, and both take the same hooks: requirements, takeovers, modifiers and notices (section 10). The words act, part, stage, key, condition, requirement, route, affordance and intent are defined once, in the vocabulary.

Contexts

/datum/act                    // pooled; every trigger's context
	var/datum/holder              // the entity whose entry is running; src in PROC_REF handlers
	var/atom/target               // what the action is done to
	var/mob/actor                 // null for world actions without one
	var/obj/item/held
	var/route                     // ROUTE_*
	var/datum/source              // owner of any hold or grant this trigger places (default: the op itself, section 9)
	var/datum/capability/cap      // set when the entry belongs to a capability
	var/datum/request/answer      // the answered request, after asks() (section 13)
	var/list/args                 // validated UI or topic args (they also arrive as typed proc params), or an after(with =) payload
	var/dt                        // every() and sequence work: elapsed time
	// plus the typed fields ACTION() generates: landing, landed_on, packet, ...
on =Lands onAlso used by
ON_TARGET (default)A.target: what the action is done toreq(T, of =) and source = in parts name a participant the same way
ON_HOLDERA.holder: the entity whose entry is running
ON_ACTORA.actor
ON_HELDA.held

while_slotted() adds ON_CONTENTS (section 10).

Holder and target. The holder is the entity whose entry is running; the target is what the action is done to. For a target's own op they are the same entity. For a held item's at_target op the holder is the item, so a baton's stun op has the baton as holder and the struck mob as target, and a holds() part with the default on = lands on the mob. For an actor's own op the holder is the actor.

Lifetime. A context lives for one trigger and is never stored in a var or passed in with = or delayed(). Section 7 ("Contexts") has the rules: when handlers run, A.snapshot(), the kernel releasing a context when a handler runtimes, the production watchdog, and the reused evaluation context that conditions and outputs get outside a trigger. Three points are specific to actions:

  • performs() opens a child context, and a takeover that starts another action (a reflected projectile is a new hit) gets its own.
  • Notices are delivered synchronously when act_done() publishes, in publish order, so the publisher's next line sees the handlers' effects.
  • Nested actions and notices are capped at depth 8 (ACT_MAX_DEPTH): beyond it an error is logged and the nested one is dropped.

World actions

A world action is declared once with ACTION(). It is a marker the generator consumes, not a plain macro: arguments are a name token, typed fields, and optionally FIXED and notice =.

ACTION(fall, turf/landing, mob/living/landed_on)          // makes /datum/act/fall, act_fall(holder, landing, landed_on) and /datum/notice/fell
ACTION(move, turf/origin, turf/destination, direction)
ACTION(insert, obj/item/item, slot_id)
ACTION(hit, datum/damage_packet/packet)                   // subtypes per entry: ACTION(hit/projectile), hit/emp, hit/blob, ...
ACTION(buckle, mob/living/buckled)
ACTION(unbuckle, mob/living/unbuckled)
ACTION(slash, mob/living/slasher, FIXED)                  // FIXED: nothing can refuse it; a claw swipe publishes it
ACTION(op, datum/op_def/op, provider, notice = /datum/notice/op_done)   // the action every op runs as
ActionTyped fieldsNotice
move, z_changeturf/origin, turf/destination (and direction for a move)moved, z_changed
cross, uncrossatom/movable/crossercrossed, uncrossed
bump, stumbled_intoatom/bumped; atom/movable/stumbledbumped, stumbled_into
fallturf/landing, mob/living/landed_onfell
thrown_hitatom/movable/thrownthrown_hit
hit/<entry>: projectile, melee, explosion, emp, blob, fire, shock, ...datum/damage_packet/packethit/<entry>
irradiate, injure, body_statusthe body domain's fields (section 14)irradiated, injured, body_status_changed (declared with notice =)
insert, removeobj/item/item, slot_idinserted, removed
equip, unequipobj/item/item, slot_idequipped, unequipped
buckle, unbucklemob/living/buckled; mob/living/unbuckled (the occupant, not the actor who did it)buckled, unbuckled
climbatom/climbedclimbed
speak, emotemessage, language; emote_keyspoke, emoted
slash (FIXED)mob/living/slasherslashed
opdatum/op_def/op, providerop_done (carries the op key; see on_op)

The notice is the action's name in the past tense, generated: /datum/notice/fell, inserted, buckled, unbuckled, crossed, uncrossed, removed. A name that already reads as past tense (hit, stumbled_into, round_started) is kept. notice = overrides it, as the op action does. A subtype declared with a slash (hit/projectile) inherits its parent's fields; its proc and ACT_TRY token use an underscore (act_hit_projectile, hit_projectile).

Calling one. The generated act_fall() takes its fields positionally and typed, so DreamChecker checks the call. ACT_TRY(src, fall, T, M) expands to a table lookup first and builds the context only when something hooks the action:

var/datum/act/fall/F = ACT_TRY(src, fall, T, M)   // the context; ACT_PASS (nothing hooks it, nothing allocated); or null (refused or taken over)
if(!F)
	return
...do the fall, reading T and M (your own locals), not F...
act_done(F)                      // publishes /datum/notice/fell and releases the context; a no-op for ACT_PASS
// A path that decides not to finish:  act_cancel(F)  releases the context and publishes nothing.
  • ACT_PASS is one shared constant, so act_done(ACT_PASS) and act_cancel(ACT_PASS) do nothing. Only a hook's handler reads the context's fields.
  • A lint requires act_done or act_cancel on every path of the proc that called ACT_TRY. Forgetting leaks the context and holds its references.
  • A FIXED action has no ACT_TRY: PUBLISH(E, x, args...) announces it (section 10).
  • A taken-over action returns null from ACT_TRY, and its notice is still published with replaced = TRUE.

The world actions: move, z_change, cross, uncross, bump, stumbled_into, fall, thrown_hit, hit (by entry), irradiate, injure, body_status, insert, remove, equip, unequip, speak, emote, buckle, unbuckle, climb, slash and op.

Action paths: how input becomes an op

Every client input first becomes a typed event in the input inbox (section 2). It resolves through the paths below when the inbox drains it, and a target that has gone stale by then is dropped with the gate's reason.

InputPath
Click, shift, ctrl, alt and middle click, drag-drop, clicking the held itemA gesture (GESTURE_CLICK, _SHIFT, _CTRL, _ALT, _MIDDLE, _DRAG, _SELF) on ROUTE_PHYSICAL. The actor's bind profile and current stance map it to an ordered list of intents.
Context menu, radial, command bar, screentipsThe ops that match, by key. Ones that fail Require are shown greyed, with their reason: action_options(actor, target, held) returns each candidate's key, label, enabled flag and reason, and screentip_for(actor, target, held, gesture) returns the text for the op a click would run. Both evaluate lazily and are cached (see "Resolution cost").
UI buttonAn op with a ui_act() input, over ROUTE_UI
Topic linkAn op with a topic("key", args...) input, over ROUTE_TOPIC
Gameplay ability, action button, keybindAn op with a menu(button =, bind =) input, over ROUTE_MENU. All of an actor's menu ops are listed under one "Abilities" verb-panel entry, each with its action button and keybind. Only client, OOC and admin commands are verb_entry(), ADMIN_VERB or DEBUG_VERB (section 13); a layering lint rejects verb_entry under code/content.
Silicon remote use, AI speech commands, telekinesisROUTE_INTERFACE, ROUTE_SPEECH, ROUTE_TK. Interface and telekinesis have their own providers; the speech parser maps a phrase to an op key.
Acting on the container you are inside (struggling in a belly, a locker, a mech cockpit)ROUTE_INSIDE: the target is the actor's container
AI controllers, scripted actorsperform_op(actor, target, key, held) or perform_intent(actor, target, INTENT_X, held): the same path, the same requirements. perform_intent() returns the key of the op that ran, or null
Admin authorityROUTE_AUTHORITY with an authority datum: perform_op(actor, target, key, held, authority = A). The op decides what it asks of it with needs(req_rights(R_X))

Routes, affordances, intents and gestures

Four enumerations name every input. One table at the end of this subsection says what each input part implies, so authors rarely write them. A provider is whatever supplies an affordance and its reach: a hand, a gripper, a natural weapon, an AI interface. A gesture is the raw input, which the actor's bind profile and stance turn into an ordered list of intents.

RouteHow the actor reaches the targetReach and provider
ROUTE_PHYSICALThe actor's own hands or body on the targetAdjacent, through the containment ledger (the reach gate). A provider with AFF_MANIPULATE, AFF_HOLD or AFF_ATTACK
ROUTE_TKTelekinesisThe telekinesis provider's reach. Compartments still apply
ROUTE_INTERFACEA silicon's remote interfaceThe interface provider (AFF_CONTROL). No adjacency, and physical bays are ignored
ROUTE_UI, ROUTE_TOPICA button in an open tgui window, or a topic linkNo provider. The window's access requirement (range, conscious, rights) decides
ROUTE_MENUThe actor's own Abilities entry, action button or keybindNo provider: the op is the actor's own
ROUTE_SPEECHA recognised spoken commandNo provider
ROUTE_INSIDEThe container the actor is insideThe target is the actor's container; no reach test
ROUTE_AUTHORITYAn admin acting by authorityAn authority datum. Bypasses the actor gate
ROUTE_GHOSTAn observer's click or menuObserver ops only

Masks: ROUTE_ALL (every route) and ROUTE_NONE (no route). A stat such as acts_via holds a mask. The physical routes are ROUTE_PHYSICAL alone; ROUTE_TK obeys compartments but is not "physical" for the actor gate.

AffordanceWhat it adds beyond the routeGiven by
AFF_MANIPULATEHandling things: pressing, opening, inserting, takingHands, grippers, telekinesis
AFF_HOLDCarrying any itemHands
AFF_HOLD_SMALLCarrying small items onlyGrippers
AFF_ATTACKStrikingHands, natural weapons, weapons with reach
AFF_CONTROLOperating controls without handling anythingThe AI interface
IntentWhat the gesture means
INTENT_USETouch it, or apply the held item. The default
INTENT_ATTACKHarm it. The hostile stances put it first in the intent list
INTENT_TOGGLE, INTENT_OPEN, INTENT_EJECTFlip a state, open it, take its contents out
INTENT_DROP_ONTOSomething dragged onto the target
INTENT_EXAMINELook at it
INTENT_PRESENTShow the held item as a credential (presents(T))

Gestures: GESTURE_CLICK, _SHIFT, _CTRL, _ALT, _MIDDLE, _DRAG (the held or dragged atom is dropped onto the target) and _SELF (the held item used on itself). UI, topic, menu, speech and authority inputs carry no gesture and no intent: they name their op by key. Stances are today's I_* ids (I_HELP, I_HURT, ...), which the bind profile reads and stance(S...) and req_stance(S) test.

Input partRouteAffordanceIntentTier
hand()ROUTE_PHYSICALAFF_MANIPULATEINTENT_USE; INTENT_TOGGLE when the op toggles()NORMAL
tool(Q), any_of_tools(Q...)ROUTE_PHYSICALAFF_MANIPULATEINTENT_USEPART
item(T), stack(T, n)ROUTE_PHYSICALAFF_MANIPULATEINTENT_USE (INTENT_PRESENT with presents())PART
in_hand()ROUTE_PHYSICAL (the held item on itself)AFF_HOLDINTENT_USENORMAL
at_target(filter)ROUTE_PHYSICALAFF_HOLDINTENT_USENORMAL
menu(button =, bind =)ROUTE_MENUnonenone: chosen by keyNORMAL

topic("key", args...)ROUTE_TOPICnonenone: chosen by keyNORMAL

Select parts change one column. via(routes) (a mask: via(ROUTE_UI | ROUTE_INTERFACE)) replaces the set of routes the op accepts, and by(affordances) (a mask: by(AFF_MANIPULATE | AFF_CONTROL)) replaces the set of affordances it accepts; without them both sets come from the input part. stance(S...) is sugar for when(req_stance(S...)) and hostile() for answers(INTENT_ATTACK). gesture(G) pins a raw gesture and is rare. The op's route and affordance sets are matched against the route the input arrived on and the affordances of the provider on that route.

Reach lives on the provider. at_target() takes no range, and a physical route implies adjacency. A provider says how far it reaches, what it adds, and what it can carry. A borg's selected module counts as its held item, and what a gripper carries is A.held, with the gripper as the provider:

CAPABILITIES(/obj/item/gripper,                           // excerpt: the same list is in 16.8
	provides(AFF_MANIPULATE | AFF_HOLD_SMALL, reach = 1, accepts = list(/obj/item/cell, /obj/item/circuitboard)),
)
CAPABILITIES(/obj/item/melee/spear,
	provides(AFF_ATTACK, reach = 2),                          // an item that extends reach
)
CAPABILITIES(/mob/living/silicon/ai,
	provides(AFF_CONTROL, route = ROUTE_INTERFACE, reach = REACH_ANY),   // route defaults to ROUTE_PHYSICAL
)

Who may act, and what they can reach

Two gates run before any op can match or run. They apply the same way to players, AI controllers and perform_op().

Actor gate. Every actor has a stat acts_via (rule MASK_AND, base ROUTE_ALL) beside the existing can_act (ALL). Resolution first drops every candidate whose route is not in the actor's acts_via. An op with a physical input (hand(), tool(), item(), stack(), in_hand(), at_target()) also gets an implicit req_capable(), unless it declares req_capable(ignoring = ...) (struggling ignores restraints).

Conditions hold these stats, so nothing in content checks "is absorbed":

  • absorbed: acts_via = ROUTE_NONE, held with a reason. Prey cannot click, use a UI, use abilities or speak commands.
  • stunned or paralysed: the stun and paralysis statuses contribute FALSE to can_act (contributes(STAT_CAN_ACT, not(STAT_STUN))).
  • buckled or handcuffed: hold acts_via to ROUTE_ALL & ~ROUTE_PHYSICAL. ROUTE_INSIDE and ROUTE_UI stay as their sources choose.
  • dead: ROUTE_NONE. A ghost is its own mob and acts only through ROUTE_GHOST.

Ending the condition releases the hold. ROUTE_AUTHORITY bypasses the actor gate, and only with an authority datum.

Reach gate. For ROUTE_PHYSICAL the target must be reachable. Resolution walks the containment ledger from the target up to the first container the actor can touch, and every slot on the way must be exposed:

  • SLOT_EXPOSURE_EXTERNAL is always exposed.
  • A slot with at = BAY_X is exposed only while that bay's compartment gate passes for this route. compartment(BAY_X, door =, route_gate =): the door capability (for example cover()) closes the bay, and route_gate is a route mask naming the routes the door applies to. Default: ROUTE_PHYSICAL | ROUTE_TK. ROUTE_INTERFACE and the window routes never see physical bays.

Reaching the target is Match, so it is silent. An unreachable target is not a candidate, so a closed hatch stops every op aimed at the enclosed cell: its own ops, using a tool on it and drag-drop. Reach applies to the held item's at_target ops too. Examining an unreachable thing shows only what the bay exposes.

Reaching a slot is Require, so it is loud. An op that moves something into or out of a slot declared at = BAY_X (put_in or take_out) gets an implicit needs(...) that the bay is open, with the compartment's reason. The world actions insert and remove on that slot are refused the same way. at(BAY_X) is the explicit form of that requirement, for an op that works through a bay without moving a slot item (the APC's subversion reset reaches the hardware inside its hatch). An author who wants such an op silent instead adds when(...). The cell bay's take op, cell_bay.cell.take, already has when(nameof(cell)), and the APC adds extend("cell_bay.cell.take", when(COVER_OPEN)), so on a closed APC an empty-hand click does not match it and falls through to opening the window. See 16.1a.

Gate feedback. Both gates filter silently, so that input can fall through to another candidate. But if no candidate survives and a gate dropped something, the engine shows that gate's reason, rate-limited, so a click never just does nothing. The actor gate's reason comes from the holds on acts_via, which take one: hold(prey, STAT_ACTS_VIA, ROUTE_NONE, source = src, reason = MSG(vore/absorbed)). When several holds apply, the strongest hold's reason is shown. The reach gate's reason is the compartment's, which comes from its door capability ("The cover is closed.").

Resolution

  1. Candidates come from three places: the target's ops (its type table plus every capability granted to it), the held item's at_target ops, and the actor's own ops (abilities and in_hand self-uses).
  2. Filters, in order. These are the Match stage (section 9), so a candidate that fails one is dropped silently:
    • step 0, actor gate: candidates whose route is not in the actor's acts_via are dropped;
    • step 0.5, reach gate: for physical routes, a target that is not reachable is not a candidate;
    • the route the input arrived on is one the op accepts;
    • a provider on that route gives an affordance the op accepts, within its reach;
    • the op's when conditions hold.
  3. Order: the bind profile's intent list, then the op's tier, then relative priority (priority(above("cover.open")) puts an op just above the named one, whatever the tiers). Remaining ties go target first, then held item, then actor; then subtype before parent; then capability declaration order. A target op that names this held item's type (item(T)) is tier PART and the target wins ties, so it outranks the item's own generic use.
  4. Run: the first candidate runs through Require, Wait and Do. If Require refuses, the player sees the reason; the input never silently falls through. Only Match failures fall through. If no candidate survives and a gate dropped something, the engine shows the gate's reason (see "Gate feedback").
  5. Passing on: an op with a passes() part lets the next candidate run after the first one commits. The later candidates' refusals and Match failures are silent, because the player already got one op's result. explain_click shows the whole chain.
Tier, highest firstYielded by
OP_PRIORITY_SUBVERTThe emag() capability's op: subverting the holder comes before anything else the card could do
OP_PRIORITY_CLAWThe claw swipe: a species or mob op (a natural weapon) that swipes empty-handed at a machine, offered only where a reaction listens for /datum/notice/slashed (req_heard); it publishes the slash action. There is no claws() capability
OP_PRIORITY_TAKE_OUTtake_out(): taking what sits in an open bay or slot by hand, ahead of the holder's own empty-hand use
OP_PRIORITY_ATTACKhostile(), answers(INTENT_ATTACK)
OP_PRIORITY_PARTitem(T), stack(T, n), tool(Q), any_of_tools(), put_in(): a part worked with a named thing in hand (a cover, a panel, wires, a repair)
OP_PRIORITY_NORMALhand(), in_hand(), at_target(), menu(), ui_act(), topic()
OP_PRIORITY_DEFAULTThe interface() open op, pick-up: what a type does when nothing more specific answers

An op's tier is its default place in the order of candidates. It is the highest tier its parts yield, and priority(OP_PRIORITY_X) pins one. Only those two ops, the emag op and the claw swipe, pin the SUBVERT and CLAW tiers; everything else orders by priority(above(...)) and priority(below(...)), which name a key and so are greppable.

The same-input rule. Two ops on one target type with the same input, the same intent and the same tier are a build error naming both lines. The fix is a different input, a different tier or a relative priority, or passes() on the first. Ops whose when parts are mutually exclusive do not clash (when(c) against when(not(c)), or req_is(K) against req_is(K, FALSE)). A belly's "struggle" and "escape" cannot both be plain hand() ops at one tier: one of them gets a different input, such as menu().

Resolution cost

A click, a hover and a menu share one two-pass resolver, so cost stays flat as candidates grow.

  • Pass 1 builds the ordered candidate list from an interned index keyed by intent and held type, applying only the cheap gates: the route mask, reach and provider affordance. It runs no requirement code.
  • Pass 2 on a click walks that list testing each candidate's when conditions until one holds. That is the winner, and only its needs() run.
  • Menus and screentips run needs() for every candidate, lazily, with at_most = 0.2 SECONDS and a cache keyed by (actor, target, held, act generation). The act generation of an entity bumps when a key that one of its requirements reads is published.
  • The reach walk is cached per target and invalidated by the containment ledger's version. A UI window recomputes the reason of an op only when a key that op's requirements read changed.
  • Bench scenarios gate phase 1 on these paths: 100 clicks per second on a 30-candidate stack, hovering over 60 atoms, and 20 open UI windows (section 19).

Explaining a resolution

Every filter drops candidates silently, so the engine also says why. The tools below use the origin list the table builder keeps for each interned entry (__FILE__:__LINE__, kept in test builds).

explain_click(actor, target, held, gesture)         // admin verb "Explain Interaction": every candidate, the filter that dropped it (with the entry's file:line),
                                                    // the winner, its tier and the tie-break that placed it, and any passes() chain
perform_op(actor, target, key, held, trace = TRUE)  // the same path, with the trace printed
explain_type(/obj/machinery/power/apc)              // the merged op table, each part tagged with its origin: type, capability or extend site
assert_resolves(actor, target, held, GESTURE_CLICK, "cover.open")   // a unit-test helper

On an empty resolution the engine shows the best near-miss's reason, rate-limited: the highest-ranked candidate dropped by the route or provider filter, with a message such as "You can't do that without hands." A when miss has no reason by design and stays silent to the player; explain_click names the condition that was false.

Build errors are tagged with the tier that catches them:

TierCatches
DM compileAn unknown ACT_TRY token or wrong field types (the generated act_x() procs are typed); misused part constructors
GeneratorAn unknown read; an unannotated global call inside a requirement, condition or output
Boot (the table builder)An unresolved literal key in extend, without, configure, performs, on_op or priority(above()); a duplicate key; a requirement with no reason; two ops with the same input, intent and tier; a performs() cycle; an op with consumes() and an item-sourced hold; a cycle among stats and conditions
TestA requirement or condition that writes, publishes or messages (the purity guard, section 9)

Replaces:

  • Handler checks and gates: incapacitated()/can_act checks in handlers, INCAPACITATED_IGNORING, manual "is absorbed" checks, manual "panel open?" guards before reaching contents, hand_gate() (the operability check is extend(TAG_CONTROL, needs(...)) from machine_basics; unbuckle-on-touch is the buckle() capability's op).
  • Entry points: attackby, attack_hand, attack_self, afterattack, attack_generic, *_act tool procs and click_alt overrides; INTERACTION_ENTRY_*; the legacy interaction resolver; cap_use_at's separate pass; use_tool.
  • Results and types: INTERACTION_HANDLED_PASS (now passes()); guard_ctx; op_ctx as a separate type.
  • /datum/ability: an ability is an actor op granted to the actor, and its HUD button is menu(button =).

9Ops and parts

op(key, parts...) // key: a literal id. The menu name is label("Text"), else derived from the key, the tool verbs or toggles(). Nested lists flatten. extend(key | list(keys) | CAP_X | TAG_X | /datum/act/x, parts...) // TAG_X | TAG_Y also works without(key)

Every behaviour is a part. A part type declares how its values combine and implements any of the four stage hooks. The engine knows nothing else. Waits, costs, messages and effects are ordinary library parts, written the same way you'd add a new one.

Four stages

An op runs through four engine stages, always in this order.

StageQuestionWhat acts in itOn failure
MatchIs this what the actor is doing?The input part, answers, hostile, via, by, gesture, stance, presents, when(cond...), and the engine's actor gate and reach gateSilent. The op is not a candidate, the input falls through to the next candidate, and menus hide it.
RequireIs it allowed now?needs(req...), at(BAY_X), and the require half of costs (costs, cooldown)The reason is shown, menus grey the op out, and the input does not fall through. Re-checked after the wait and right before Do.
WaitHow long does it take?wait(t, keeps =), scaled by the held tool's speedCancelled when a keeps condition breaks, or when a changed read makes a requirement refuse. See "Waiting and asking".
DoWhat happens?Cost spending, chance, effects, asks, feedback, delayed, quietA failed chance() plays its else feedback and stops the rest.

Do runs in this order:

  1. Costs and chance: the do half of costs, consumes and cooldown is spent, then chance(p, else = parts) rolls. A failed roll plays its else feedback and stops everything after it, and the costs are already spent.
  2. Effects: then, toggles, sets, holds, releases, toggles_hold, grants, put_in, take_out, fixes, becomes, spawns, opens_ui, asks, performs, passes, cuts_all_wires, in declaration order. They never sleep.
  3. Feedback: says, plays, flash, logs.
  4. Later and quiet: delayed(t, parts...) schedules more parts on the holder's clock, owned by the holder and dying with it, and quiet() suppresses the notice.

asks() is a suspension point. It is a part in the effect list, not a container. Parts before it run when the op reaches them, and parts after it run after the answer. If the op has any asks(), step 1 moves to right after the last answer, so cancelling a prompt costs nothing. An effect declared before the first asks() runs before the prompt and before costs are spent, and the build warns about it. The answer arrives as A.answer; with several prompts it is the latest, so use one /datum/prompt/form when you need several answers together. After each answer the op's when and Require parts are checked again.

Metadata, not stages. The label() name, priority() and tag() say how an op is listed and ordered. They never run.

Combine rules stay per part. Requirements accumulate. Single values (input, wait, cooldown, priority, says, plays, verbs, label) replace, and the most specific declaration wins. Effects append and tags accumulate. Costs combine per resource: the most specific costs(RES_X, n) replaces, and costs(RES_X, +n) adds to it.

Parts

PartStage(s) it acts inCombine
Input: hand() · tool(Q) · any_of_tools(Q...) · item(T) · stack(T, n) · in_hand() · at_target(filter) · menu(button =, bind =) · ui_act(args...) or ui_act("name", args...) · topic("key", args...). What each implies is the input table in section 8. UI and topic args are shorthand over prompt kinds with one validator (section 13): arg_num(name, min, max) · arg_int(name, min, max) (bounds optional on both) · arg_text(name, max) · arg_bool · arg_choice(name, list | PROC_REF) · arg_ref(name, type, from =) · arg_path · arg_listMatchexactly one input; a more specific one replaces it
answers(INTENT_X...) · hostile() · via(routes) · by(affordances) · gesture(G) · stance(S...) · presents(T) (the held item is a credential, not "used"). via and by replace the routes and affordances the input part implies; stance and hostile are sugar for when(req_stance(...)) and answers(INTENT_ATTACK)Matchreplace
