(a prompt is a request, section 13) · confirms("text") (asks(/datum/prompt/yes_no, question = "text"); a "no" ends the op and nothing is spent) · performs(key) · passes() · undone(PROC_REF) (construction stages: runs going back, section 12). KEY is nameof(v) or a capability key id. on = is one of the four selectors in section 8. source = is described under "Holds, grants and sources"Doappend, in order
put_in(slot) · take_out(slot)Do; and Require, implicitly, when the slot is declared at = BAY_Xappend, in order
Feedback: says(msg_type | self, others =, blind =) · plays(SFX) · verbs(you, they) (the verb pair messages use: "You screw", "Rae screws") · flash(look_layer) · logs(LOG_GAME | LOG_ADMIN)Doreplace
delayed(t, parts...) · quiet() (no notice)Doappend
Metadata: label("Text") · priority(OP_PRIORITY_X | above(key) | below(key)) · tag(T)none; they never runlabel and priority replace; tags accumulate

When or needs

when() takes conditions and needs() takes requirements (below). They differ in what a failure means. A failed when(cond...) is a Match failure: the op is not what the actor is doing, so it is silently not a candidate and the input falls through. A failed needs(req...) is a Require failure: the actor is refused, with a reason.

when means "exists only while". It has two spellings with one meaning. On one op it is a part: op("eject", hand(), when(nameof(beaker)), then(PROC_REF(eject))). Around several ops it is the entry-level wrapper when(cond, op(...), op(...)), the same when as in section 5. Both compile to the same resolution-time applicability check. Nothing is added to or removed from the op table at runtime.

Rule of thumb. If the op shouldn't exist in that state, so it should be hidden, use when. If a player would ask "why can't I?", use needs.

Conditions and requirements

A condition is a boolean with no reason. A requirement is a condition plus a reason. The grammar of a condition is the same everywhere one is taken: when(), contributes(), every(when =), outputs(when =), look_layer(when =), on_change(cond, ...) and sequence steps.

Condition formMeaning
nameof(v)Truthy test of a tracked var or relation on the holder
STAT_X, or a capability key id (COVER_OPEN)Truthy test of that stat or key
not(c) · all_of(c...) · any_of(c...)Negation, and, or
PROC_REF(x) · CAP_PROC(x)x(datum/act/A) returns TRUE or FALSE
A library requirement datumIts boolean view (req_built_final() as a condition)

Strings are never conditions, and an expression such as !operable && integrity_broken is not one: write a PROC_REF.

When a condition is evaluated. A stat recomputes inline whenever its inputs change, so it is always current. A condition used for matching (when() on an op, menus, look_layer(when =)) is evaluated when it is read, at resolution or output time. A condition that drives a transition (an entry-level when() attaching or detaching entries, every(when =) starting and stopping, on_change edges, while_slotted entries) is evaluated at the drain (section 7).

needs() takes requirements only. Every requirement carries a resolvable reason, or the build fails:

  • Library constructors carry a default reason from MSG_DEF(req/<ctor>, ...). Capability keys declared with cap_keys() carry declared reasons, so needs(req_is(COVER_OPEN)) needs no because =. A declared reason is the message for the test the capability usually makes; testing a key the other way, or one with no declared reason, takes an explicit because =.
  • Generic tests take an explicit reason: req_is(KEY, value = TRUE, because = MSG(x)), req_at_least(KEY, n, because = MSG(x)) and req(PROC_REF(cond), because = MSG(x)). KEY is nameof(v), a STAT_X id or a capability key id.
  • A dynamic reason: because = PROC_REF(reason_proc), where reason_proc(datum/act/A) returns a /datum/msg type.
  • Combining: not(r, because = MSG(x)) needs its because inside needs(). any_of(...) joins its reasons ("needs a wrench or a welder"). all_of(...) reports the first one that fails.
  • Relaxing: a requirement may take id = "x", so a subtype can drop that one deliberately: extend("cover.open", drop = "x").

The old REQ_ON, REQ_TARGET_STATE and REQ_PROC become req(PROC_REF(x), because = ...) once their procs take (datum/act/A) and return a boolean; the mapping says so (section 17).

Purity. Requirements, conditions and because procs must not write state, publish or message. Test builds run them under the same no-write, no-publish, no-message guard as outputs, which is what lets menus, examine and early cancels ask at any time.

Reads. What a requirement reads is generated (section 7). Reads through A.actor, A.held and A.target are typed hops, subscribed for as long as an op is pending, so a wait notices the actor's hands or the held item's charge changing. A.args, world.time and GLOB reads do not subscribe. A global helper is followed through READS_FROM(arg), and an unannotated global call inside a requirement, condition or output is a build error with that hint.

GroupLibrary requirements
What is usedreq(T, of = ON_HELD) (the participant is of type T; a type path as the first argument) · req_empty_hand() · req_self_held() · req_component(T) (a machine has that component installed)
Target statereq_tag(TAG) · req_property_at_most(PROP, n, of = ON_TARGET) (a property such as a size class is at most n) · req_wire(W) (the wire is intact) · req_wire_cut(W) · req_built(STAGE_X) · req_built_final() · req_full(nameof(rel)) · req_empty(nameof(rel)) · req_not_subverted()
Actorreq_conscious() · req_alive() · req_capable(ignoring =) · req_fits_bodytype() (the item fits the wearer's body type; used on equips) · req_adjacent() · req_stance(S) · req_access() · req_rights(R_X) · req_heard(/datum/notice/x) (something on the target listens for that notice, so an op that only matters when a reaction exists is offered only then)
Routereq_on_route(routes, req = null): with one argument it tests the route; with two, req applies only on those routes
Genericreq_is(KEY, value = TRUE, because =) · req_at_least(KEY, n, because =) · req(PROC_REF(cond), because =). These replace the old req_set, req_clear, req_stat and req_stat_at_least; one test serves a var, a stat and a capability key.
Combiningall_of(r...) · any_of(r...) · not(r, because =)

The one signature

Every proc the engine calls takes (datum/act/A). PROC_REF(x) runs on the holder (src is the holder). CAP_PROC(x) runs on the capability datum, with A.holder the entity and A.cap the datum. The table lists, per place, which fields are set.

PlaceCalled asFields set
An effect: then(PROC_REF(x))x(datum/act/A)holder, target, actor, held, route, source, cap (if any), args, answer (after asks())
A requirement: req(PROC_REF(x), because =)x(datum/act/A) returns TRUE or FALSEAs for an effect, minus answer
A dynamic reason: because = PROC_REF(r)r(datum/act/A) returns a /datum/msg typeThe same as the requirement that failed
A condition: when(), contributes(), every(when =), look_layer(when =), on_change()x(datum/act/A) returns TRUE or FALSEThe evaluation context: holder, plus cap and dt where they apply. Inside instead() and in adjusts(when =) a condition gets the action's own typed context instead
A hook handler: instead, adjusts, on_notice, on_op, while_slottedx(datum/act/A)holder, target, actor, route and the action's typed fields. In an observe, holder is the listener. A hook granted by while_slotted(on = ON_HOLDER) has the granting item as source
every() and sequence workx(datum/act/A)holder, dt; for a system's per-member work (every(members =)) target is the member
after() and delayed() callbacksx(datum/act/A)holder; args carries the with = payload of after(), scalars and flyweights only
outputs(PROC_REF(x), at_most =, when =)x(datum/act/A)holder, dt. The standard outputs (draw, ui_data, push_to_rust, examine) keep the arguments section 13 gives them
CAP_PROC(x), anywhere abovex(datum/act/A), run on the capability datumholder is the entity, cap is the datum, then as for the place that called it
A UI op's handlerset_channel(datum/act/A, channel, mode)As for an effect; the declared args arrive as typed params after A, and the build checks their names against the arg_* declarations
A request callback: request(owner, ..., then(PROC_REF(x)))x(datum/act/A)holder is the owner; answer is the answered request
A part's hooks: match, require, reason, wait_time, dox(datum/act/A)As for an effect

Keys, tags and UI ops

Keys. Every key is a declared identifier that the build resolves. An unresolved one is a build error, and keys of different kinds are never interchangeable.

KindWritten asRules
Op keyA literal: "breaker" for a type's own op, "cover.open" for an op a capability bringsA capability's ops are namespaced by it automatically, and by its discriminator when it has one ("cell_bay.cell.insert", section 11). A bare key has no dot. Keys are unique in a type's compiled table; a duplicate is a build error naming both lines.
Entry keykey = "scan" on any other entry (every, on_notice, on_change, contributes, ...)Shares the op-key namespace on a type, so one extend, without or configure can address either
Capability keyCAP_X plus a discriminator (section 11)An identical repeat dedupes. The same key with different params is a build error naming both lines
TagTAG_XTags accumulate. Every ui_act() op gets TAG_UI and every topic() op gets TAG_TOPIC, automatically. An extend on a tag that matches nothing is fine.

The build resolves every literal reference in extend, without, configure, performs, perform_op, on_op and priority(above()) against the type's compiled table. perform_op with an unknown key at runtime is an error, not a no-op. Stats, sources and build stages have their own ids (STAT_X, SRC_X, STAGE_X), and their own sections.

UI ops. ui_act() with no name uses the op's key as the window action's name; ui_act("legacy") is only for a TSX interface that cannot change. Invalid or missing args reject the press and log it; the handler never runs. Whether the actor "can use the window" (range, conscious, rights) is checked as a requirement before the op's own. Sub-actions use dotted names, ui_act("tank.eject"), which replace UI_SUBACT. An op has one input, so ops share effects through performs("key") or a shared then().

Intents. The input part implies a default intent, and select parts override it (the input table in section 8).

Extend targets, overrides and precedence

extend takes one of: "key" (an op or entry key), list(keys), CAP_X (all of that capability's ops), TAG_X (or TAG_X | TAG_Y: ops with either tag), or /datum/act/x (hook parts: needs, instead, adjusts).

Precedence, lowest to highest: library defaults, then the op's own parts, then group extends (CAP, TAG, action; same level, in list order), then extend("key"). A subtype beats its parent at each level. Requirements accumulate, single values replace, effects append and tags accumulate. Capabilities granted at runtime form a second table with the same precedence, resolved at click time; extend(CAP_X, ...) is by key, so it applies to one granted later.

Extending a key that doesn't exist is a build error. The one exception is an inherited extend whose target a later without() removed: it is dropped quietly.

A subtype wants toWrite
Add a requirementextend("pry", needs(...)), or on CAP_X, TAG_X or an action
Relax one inherited requirementThe requirement is declared with id = "x"; extend("cover.open", drop = "x")
Swap an op's effectsextend("pry", replaces(then(PROC_REF(do_open))))
Retune an entry's named argsconfigure("scan", interval = 10 SECONDS) for an entry with a key; configure(CAP_X, param = v) for a capability: its params are the constructor's named args, the entries are rebuilt, and variant = subtype swaps the datum type. An unknown key or param is a build error
Remove something inheritedwithout(key): an op, an entry or a capability, applied at its position in the list

Costs, tool profiles and bundles

ResourceTaken from
RES_FUELThe held tool's fuel (a welder)
RES_USESThe held item's use count
RES_CHARGEThe held item's cell charge
RES_STACKUnits of the held stack
 
TOOL_PROFILE(TOOL_MULTITOOL,   wait(5 SECONDS), plays(SFX_MULTITOOL),   verbs("pulse", "pulses"))
TOOL_PROFILE(TOOL_SCREWDRIVER, wait(2 SECONDS), plays(SFX_SCREWDRIVER), verbs("screw", "unscrew"))
TOOL_PROFILE(TOOL_WELDER,      wait(3 SECONDS), plays(SFX_WELDER),      verbs("weld", "unweld"), costs(RES_FUEL, 1))
// tool(Q) expands to its profile as defaults per slot: the wait, the sound, the verbs and one costs() per resource.
// An op's own wait, plays, verbs or costs(RES_X, n) replaces that slot. Write costs(RES_FUEL, +1) to add one.
// A profile's wait() is the base the held tool's speed scales: tool_quality(Q, speed = 1.5) makes it shorter.
// any_of_tools(Q...) uses the profile of the tool actually in hand.
// Your own bundles are procs returning parts, and nest freely. A bundle is a global proc, so a holder proc it names
// is TYPE_PROC_REF(/type, x), never PROC_REF(x):
/proc/force_pry()
	return list(tool(TOOL_CROWBAR), hostile())
/proc/component_swap(item_type, delay = 5 SECONDS)
	return list(item(item_type), consumes(), wait(delay), fixes())

Holds, grants and sources

holds(), releases() and grants() place stat contributions and capabilities through the same store as hold() and grant() (section 5). Their source defaults to the op itself: the pair (holder, op key), owned by the target's store. That keeps the effect when the item that carried the op is used up, and it ends by releases(), by expiry or when the target goes. A timed hold runs its course even if its source is deleted first.

  • source = SRC_X names a SOURCE_DEF flyweight (SRC_AI_CONTROL).
  • source = SRC_HELD_ITEM makes the held item own it, so it ends when the item leaves. An op with consumes() and an item-sourced hold or grant is a build error.
  • source = ON_ACTOR makes the actor the source: one AI's bolts never release another's. In a handler's code the same is hold(E, STAT_X, v, source = A.actor).
  • bound = TRUE, as on hold(), makes a timed hold die with its source instead of running its course.

Waiting and asking

wait(t, keeps = WAIT_KEEPS_DEFAULT)            // keeps: HELD | ADJACENT | TARGET_PRESENT | ALIVE
op("pry", label("Pry open"), force_pry(), wait(5 SECONDS, keeps = HELD | ADJACENT), then(PROC_REF(do_open)))
op("rename", label("Rename"), in_hand(), asks(/datum/prompt/text/rename), then(PROC_REF(apply_name)))
/obj/item/proc/apply_name(datum/act/A)
	var/datum/prompt/text/rename/R = A.answer       // typed: the answered prompt
	name = R.value
KeepThe wait is cancelled when
HELDthe held item is dropped, swapped or deleted
ADJACENTthe actor is no longer adjacent to the target (physical routes only)
TARGET_PRESENTthe target moved away or was deleted
ALIVEthe actor dies or loses consciousness
  • Default: WAIT_KEEPS_DEFAULT is all four, where they apply: no HELD without a held item, no ADJACENT off the physical route. keeps = HELD narrows it.
  • A changed read re-runs the requirements. The wait is cancelled only if one now refuses, so a cell's charge ticking down does not interrupt a wait that is still allowed.
  • The pending op is a kernel record related to the actor, the target and the held item. Deleting any of them cancels it, with feedback. It subscribes to the reads of the requirements it will re-check, and drops them when it ends. VV and explain_click list an actor's pending op and its subscriptions.
  • A second input while busy: the actor's policy decides. BUSY_REPLACE for players: the old one is cancelled with "You stop prying." and the new one starts. BUSY_REFUSE for AI and scripted actors, so perform_op() during a wait is refused.
  • An open asks() takes the actor's one wait slot, with its own timeout, and the same policy applies. The prompt closes with the reason if a requirement now refuses (section 13).
  • performs(key) runs the target op's Require and Do in a child context, with no nested wait. A cycle of performs is a build error. To give an existing op a second input, declare a second op that performs it.

A new kind of part

// A part type declares how it combines and implements any of the stage hooks:
//   match(A)        Match: TRUE if the op matches (silent on failure)
//   require(A)      Require: TRUE if allowed; reason(A) returns the /datum/msg shown when it isn't
//   wait_time(A)    Wait: how long
//   do(A)           Do: the work
// Reads through A.actor, A.held and A.target are typed hops, so the pending op re-checks when they change.
/datum/part/both_hands
	combine = PART_ALL
/datum/part/both_hands/require(datum/act/A)
	return A.actor.hands_free() >= 2
/datum/part/both_hands/reason(datum/act/A)
	return /datum/msg/req_two_hands

// Acts in two stages: it refuses when there isn't enough, and spends it in Do.
/datum/part/spends_blood
	combine = PART_SUM
	var/amount
/datum/part/spends_blood/require(datum/act/A)
	return A.actor.blood_volume >= amount
/datum/part/spends_blood/reason(datum/act/A)
	return /datum/msg/req_blood
/datum/part/spends_blood/do(datum/act/A)
	A.actor.use_blood(amount)

The pipeline, every time

  1. Match. The actor gate, the reach gate, then the input, intent, route, affordance, stance and every when part. It runs when the action is resolved. A failure is silent: the op is not a candidate, the input falls through, and menus hide it.
  2. Require. Every needs, at and the require half of costs. These run when the menu is built (a failing op is greyed with its reason), when the op is chosen, after the wait and right before Do. A failure shows the reason, and the input does not fall through.
  3. Wait. wait(t, keeps =), scaled by the held tool's speed. It is cancelled when a keeps condition breaks or a requirement now refuses. An actor has one wait at a time, and an open prompt takes it.
  4. Do. Costs are spent, chance() rolls (a failure plays its else feedback and stops here), effects run in order and never sleep, feedback plays, and delayed() work is scheduled. An asks() part suspends the op: after the last answer the when and Require parts are checked again, costs are spent, and the parts after it run.

Then the notice, unless the op is quiet(): fingerprints are added, the log line is written, the holder is marked changed, and /datum/notice/op_done is published to whoever wants it (on_op).

Replaces:

  • Constructors: cap_op and its 35 keywords, cap_hand/cap_tool/cap_use_on/cap_insert/cap_control/cap_use_self/cap_use_at, lib_op, adopt_entry, op_attach.
  • Changing ops: refine(), cap_require(), replace().
  • Interactions: every INTERACT_* spec, DECLARE_INTERACTIONS/EXTEND_INTERACTIONS, dq_interaction_from_spec.
  • Requirement languages: the REQ_*/PRED_*/REQ_ON/REQ_BECAUSE/HOLD_* macros, REQ_FIELD*/REQ_PANEL/REQ_TARGET_STATE, /datum/predicate (outside slots).
  • Old gating and UI forms: else_say, works_broken/works_unpowered, behind/blocked_by/locked_by, UI_ACT/UI_ARG_*/UI_ACT_*/UI_SUBACT (now dotted names), TOPIC_ACTION/TOPIC_* args.
  • Housekeeping now done by the pipeline: add_fingerprint in handlers, manual admin logging in handlers.

10Hooks and events

Hooks come in two forms. A before hook is an extend on an action: it can refuse the action, take it over, or modify it. An after hook (on_notice, on_op, on_change) reacts once something has happened. Nothing else is a hook.

You wantWriteContract
To refuse an action or opextend(/datum/act/x | "op.key" | CAP_X | TAG_X, needs(req...))Pure. A condition plus a reason; every one is checked, and the first refusal in order is shown.
To take an action overextend(/datum/act/x, instead(parts..., order = ORDER_EARLY | ORDER_NORMAL | ORDER_LATE))Side effects allowed. The first taker in order ends the action (spontaneous vore, a shield blocking a hit, a mirror reflecting a projectile). chance() inside means "takes over with probability p"; when(cond) inside gates it. The notice is still published, with replaced = TRUE.
To modify an action in flightextend(/datum/act/x, adjusts(field, by =, scale =, when =))Changes one typed field of the action before it reaches its sink (armour, ablative shields). Never re-enters the action.
To react after something happenedon_notice(/datum/notice/x, parts...)Synchronous at act_done(), in publish order, never coalesced, never sleeps.
To react to one op finishingon_op("cover.open", parts...)Sugar for on_notice(/datum/notice/op_done, parts..., op = "cover.open"), which filters on the op key. The filter runs before anything is allocated.
To react to a stateon_change(cond, ENTER | EXIT | ANY, parts...)An edge trigger on a condition. The condition is evaluated at the drain, and the hook runs there (section 7). Replaces power_change() overrides.
An effect while something is in a slotwhile_slotted(SLOT_X, entries..., on = ON_CONTENTS | ON_HOLDER)The entries are granted with the slot entry as their source, and released on exit, deletion or the holder's deletion.
To announce what can't be refusedACTION(x, ..., FIXED), then PUBLISH(E, x, field = v)Allocates nothing when nobody listens.
World-wide occurrences (round start, shift change, alerts)PUBLISH(world_owner(), x, ...); systems declare on_noticeSame.
Another entity listens at runtimeobserve(source, /datum/notice/x, listener, parts...) / unobserve(source, /datum/notice/x, listener)A hook capability granted to source with the listener as its source. Either end dying drops it.

Hooks on an action

// Refuse: this slot takes only cells. Pure; the reason is shown.
extend(/datum/act/insert, needs(req(PROC_REF(takes_only_cells), because = MSG(slot/cells_only))))

// Take over: a blob hit rips the panel open and the hit ends here.
extend(/datum/act/hit/blob, instead(cuts_all_wires(), sets(PANEL_OPEN, TRUE), order = ORDER_EARLY))

// Take over with a gate and a chance: a mirror reflects some projectiles.
extend(/datum/act/hit/projectile, instead(when(PROC_REF(mirror_ready)), chance(30), then(PROC_REF(reflect))))

// Modify: ablative armour takes a third off, with no re-entry. field is a typed field of the action.
extend(/datum/act/hit/projectile, adjusts(packet.amount, scale = 0.67, when = PROC_REF(shield_up)))
  • Candidates. The hooks and ops that apply to an action are the target's type table plus every capability granted to it (grants, while_slotted, species, traits), with the same precedence as ops. A mirror worn on the wearer is a granted capability, so its hooks reach hits on the wearer.
  • Order. order = ORDER_EARLY | ORDER_NORMAL | ORDER_LATE, then runs_after = CAP_X, then declaration order. It matters for instead, where the first taker wins and later ones never run, and for adjusts, which all apply in order, each doing its scale then its by. needs hooks do not depend on it beyond which refusal is shown.
  • Takeover. A failed chance() or a false when inside instead means this hook does not take over; the next candidate is tried, and if none does the action proceeds. A hook that takes over may start new actions (a reflected projectile is a new hit), counted against the depth cap in section 8.
  • adjusts fields are named by the action's declared fields (packet.amount for a hit) and checked at build. Use it for armour, shields and resistances; use instead only when the action should not happen.
  • Conditions in hooks. A when() inside instead() and the when = of adjusts() receive the action's own typed context (a /datum/act/hit/projectile for a projectile hit), not the evaluation context, so they can read its fields.
  • Ops are actions too. extend(/datum/act/op, needs(...)) reaches every op on the target, and extend("cover.open", needs(...)) one of them. The target's op hooks apply to ops a capability brought.

After hooks

on_notice(/datum/notice/hit/melee, then(PROC_REF(on_hit)))
on_op("cover.open", then(PROC_REF(cover_opened)))
on_change(PROC_REF(is_powered), ENTER | EXIT, then(PROC_REF(power_flipped)))   // replaces a power_change() override
on_change(nameof(terminal.charge), then(PROC_REF(charge_changed)))               // a hop through a relation
  • Hook parts. The parts inside a hook are the Do parts of section 9 (then, sets, toggles, holds, says, ...). A hook has no input, Match or Wait. Its handler takes (datum/act/A), with A.holder set.
  • on_change(cond, ...) takes any condition (section 9), including a hop through a relation. ENTER fires when the condition becomes true, EXIT when it becomes false, and ANY (the default) on any change of its value. It runs once per drain.
  • Notices are named in the past tense and generated by ACTION() (section 8). A notice is delivered at act_done(), to a listener on the target's type table or a capability granted to it.

While something is in a slot

CAPABILITIES(/obj/machinery/stasis,                // excerpt: the full list is in 16.4
	buckle(slots = 1),
	while_slotted(SLOT_BUCKLE, holds(STAT_CLOCK_RATE_BIO, 0.1), on = ON_CONTENTS),   // the occupant, while buckled in
)
CAPABILITIES(/obj/item/shield/mirror,
	while_slotted(SLOT_ANY_WORN, blocks(25), reflects(PROJECTILES, 30), on = ON_HOLDER),   // the wearer, while it is worn
)
  • on = ON_CONTENTS, declared on the type that owns the slot, grants the entries to what is in the slot: a stasis bed's occupant, a belly's prey (absorption), cryo, a borg dock.
  • on = ON_HOLDER, declared on the item, grants them to the entity whose slot the item sits in: the wearer of a shield or mirror.
  • The entries are the usual ones: holds(STAT_X, v), contributes(...), a capability constructor, blocks(chance), reflects(kinds, chance). The slot entry is their source, so they end when the item leaves, however it leaves, or when either end is deleted. No code releases anything.
  • A hook granted with on = ON_HOLDER sees the granting item as A.source (the vest, the mirror).

Announcing and observing

ACTION(round_started, FIXED)                      // declared once; the notice is /datum/notice/round_started
PUBLISH(world_owner(), round_started)             // allocates nothing when nobody listens

observe(source, /datum/notice/hit/melee, src, then(PROC_REF(saw_hit)))   // handlers run on the listener, with A.target = source
unobserve(source, /datum/notice/hit/melee, src)
  • FIXED marks an action nothing can refuse, which is all a plain announcement is. It has no ACT_TRY; PUBLISH sends it.
  • world_owner() is the kernel's world entity. World-wide notices are published on it, and systems hook them with on_notice.
  • WANTS(E, /datum/notice/x) is TRUE when something on E listens for that notice. Test it before building an expensive payload; PUBLISH itself allocates nothing when nobody listens.
  • observe() is shorthand for granting source a hook_capability(on_notice(notice, parts...)) (section 11), with the listener as the grant's source. Revoking it is unobserve(), and deleting either end drops it. In an observe, PROC_REF runs on the listener, so A.holder is the listener and A.target is the observed source. For a lasting effect on the source, grant it a capability directly: it is the same mechanism with one teardown.

An occurrence whose emitter reads a result back ("did anyone object?", "what's your armour?") is a question, not an event. It becomes a requirement on an action, a stat, or an accessor proc. Events with no listener are deleted with their emit sites.

Replaces:

  • OM events: OM_EMIT, OM_EMIT_WORLD, om_hook/om_unhook/om_hooked, /datum/om/event/* (355 types), before/* veto events, EVENT_HANDLER.
  • Guards: guard(), guarded(), GUARD_* keys, before_op on guard keys.
  • Op and damage hooks: before_op/after_op on ops (now requirements and on_notice), damage rows before_op(damage(...)), DAMAGE_REACTION* (a takeover is instead, a modification is adjusts), REFLECTS.
  • Bridges and overrides: on_channel, power_change() overrides.

11Capabilities

A capability is a named, parameterised bundle of entries: ops, contributions, triggers, look layers, examine lines, slots and UI data. It can have typed per-instance data and procs of its own. It's the unit of reuse: anything two types share is a capability, and anything only one type does is entries on that type.

Defining one

CAPABILITY_DEF(name, CAP_X, param = default, ...) // a body that returns entries; generates name(param = default, ...) CAPABILITY_TYPE(name, CAP_X, /datum/capability/x, param = default, ...) // a datum with code of its own; generates the same kind of constructor cap_keys(CAP_X, KEY = MSG(reason), ...) // the capability's state: ids, accessors, reasons, read registration
// The capability's state. Generates COVER_OPEN and COVER_REMOVED, cover_open(holder) and cover_removed(holder),
// a default reason for each, and the read registration. There is no hand-written READS_AS for capability state.
cap_keys(CAP_COVER, OPEN = MSG(cover/closed), REMOVED = MSG(cover/removed))

/// A cover over the holder's insides (the door of BAY_HATCH). The params ARE the settings.
CAPABILITY_DEF(cover, CAP_COVER, open = tool(TOOL_CROWBAR), remove = null, replace = null)
	return list(
		op("open", open, toggles(COVER_OPEN), when(not(COVER_REMOVED))),
		remove ? op("remove", remove, label("Remove cover"), needs(req_is(COVER_REMOVED, FALSE)),
			sets(COVER_REMOVED, TRUE), sets(COVER_OPEN, TRUE)) : null,
		replace ? op("replace", replace, label("Replace cover"),
			when(any_of(COVER_REMOVED, not(STAT_OPERABLE))), sets(COVER_REMOVED, FALSE)) : null,
		look_layer(LOOK_COVER_OPEN, when = COVER_OPEN),
		examine_line(MSG(cover/open_examine), when = COVER_OPEN),
		examine_line(MSG(cover/removed_examine), when = COVER_REMOVED))

// A capability with code of its own is a datum. Its procs run as CAP_PROC(x), x(datum/act/A), on the capability datum,
// with A.holder the entity and A.cap this capability. powered(channel) sits over powered_by(system, role): there is one definition.
CAPABILITY_TYPE(powered, CAP_POWERED, /datum/capability/powered, channel = POWER_CHANNEL_EQUIPMENT)
/datum/capability/powered
	var/channel = POWER_CHANNEL_EQUIPMENT           // a param: the generated powered(channel) sets it
/datum/capability/powered/entries()
	return list(
		powered_by(/datum/system/power, role = POWER_ROLE_LOAD),
		contributes(STAT_OPERABLE, CAP_PROC(has_power), reason = MSG(power/unpowered)))
/datum/capability/powered/proc/has_power(datum/act/A)
	var/obj/machinery/M = A.holder
	return M.power_area?.channel_on(channel)         // the hop through power_area is a generated read
  • Params are the settings. A definition's params are what configure() can change: configure(CAP_COVER, remove = force_pry()) re-runs the body with that param replaced. An unknown param is a build error. There is no settings = list() to keep in step with the constructor. A param can name a var or a proc (PROC_REF), which is resolved per instance at runtime.
  • Two definition forms. CAPABILITY_DEF is entries only; its body is a generated global proc, so a holder proc it names is TYPE_PROC_REF(/type, x), as in any bundle. CAPABILITY_TYPE is for a capability that needs code: its vars are the params, entries() re-runs on configure, its procs are CAP_PROC(x), and the macro sets key = CAP_X on the datum. configure(CAP_X, variant = /datum/capability/x/other) swaps the datum type and keeps the params.
  • Op keys are namespaced. The ops a capability brings are prefixed with its name, the first argument of its definition: cover's "open" is "cover.open", and CAPABILITY_DEF(reagent_container, CAP_REAGENT_CONTAINER, ...) brings "reagent_container.inject". A capability that has a discriminator (below) adds it: "cell_bay.cell.insert". The prefix is the capability that declares the op, not the bundle that included it, so a hatch's cover still opens as "cover.open". extend("cover.open", ...) changes one op and extend(CAP_COVER, ...) all of them.
  • State keys are generated. cap_keys declares a capability's state, and each key is a boolean the capability's ops set (toggles(), sets()). Each key's accessor is <cap>_<key>(holder): cover_open, cover_removed, panel_open, lock_locked, emag_emagged. cap_keys() allocates one word per capability, and a word holds at most 24 keys; more is a build error naming the capability. A fact that several sources can force is a STAT, not a key. Each row's MSG is the default reason for the requirement the library itself writes on that key: req_is(COVER_OPEN) says the cover is closed, and req_is(COVER_REMOVED, FALSE) says it has been removed. A requirement written the other way round gives because =. READS_AS stays only for producer keys that aren't capability state, and the build checks that the accessor's own reads are covered by the key it names.
  • Lifecycle hooks: on_holder_preinit(A), on_holder_init(A) and on_holder_destroy(A), all x(datum/act/A) with A.holder and A.cap set, and A.mapload while the map loads. The order inside Initialize is: on_holder_preinit (before the base body runs, for work the parent's init reads: a part made in nullspace, an inherited icon cleared), the base body, on_holder_init, then the type's own code after its ..(). On a granted capability they run at grant and at revoke.
  • Per-instance data: cap_data(A) returns the typed /datum/cap_data/<cap> for A.cap, allocated on first use. Declare the data type next to the capability (/datum/cap_data/phase_shift), and assign the result to a typed var: cap_data() is RETURN_TYPE(/datum/cap_data), and test builds check that var's type against the capability's declared data type. No more cap_data(holder, KEY)["x"]. Code outside a trigger reaches a capability with cap_of(E, CAP_X), an indexed lookup that returns the datum or null.
  • Changing one: configure(CAP_X, param = v) rebuilds its entries from the new params; extend("cover.open", ...) changes one op and extend(CAP_X, ...) all of them (drop = "id" relaxes a requirement the capability named, replaces(parts...) swaps an effect list); without(CAP_X) drops it. Any entry inside a capability can carry key =, so these can address it.
  • Granting one: grant(E, what, source, lasts) attaches a capability (a type, or a constructor call such as cover()) to one instance at runtime (section 5). Granted capabilities are a second table beside the type's own, interned by (type table, sorted grants), so a grant or revoke swaps a table id. Type-level extend(CAP_X, ...) and without(CAP_X) address a capability by key, so they apply to a granted one the same way.
  • A hook capability is hook_capability(entries...): a capability of triggers only, with no key of its own. observe() grants one to the observed entity with the listener as its source (section 10).

Keys and repeats

A capability's key is (CAP_X, discriminator). The discriminator is the definition's first required param (the one with no default), so cell_bay's is its var and cover, which has none, appears once per type. An identical repeat (the same interned signature) dedupes. The same key with different params is a build error naming both lines, which the table builder knows because it records __FILE__:__LINE__ for every entry. (A second CAPABILITIES line for a type is a build error too.)

CapabilityDiscriminatorA second one is
compartment(BAY_X, door =, route_gate =)the bayanother bay: both stand
cell_bay(var, at =)the varanother bay on another var: both stand
toggle_setting(nameof(v), name, parts...)the vara toggle for another var: both stand
powered_by(system, role)the systemmembership in another system: both stand
interface(window, ...)the windowanother window: both stand; its open op is "interface.<window>.open"
cover(...), panel(), lock(...), emag(...), construction(...)nonean identical repeat dedupes; different params are a build error

Every shared behaviour on master, plus the planned pieces from B2/B3 and this design, all on parts:

Bundles and capabilities

A bundle is any proc that returns entries or parts. Wrap it in a capability only when someone must address it: configure, extend, without or grant it as a unit. These three wire other capabilities together, and are capabilities, so without(CAP_MACHINE_BASICS) and configure(CAP_MACHINE_BASICS, frame = NONE) work:

CapabilityContents
machine_basics(board, anchored_by =, repair =, frame =, powered =)breakable, powered, anchor, the construction ladder frame (default machine_frame(board), NONE for no ladder), and extend(TAG_CONTROL, needs(req_is(STAT_OPERABLE, because = MSG(machine/inoperable)))). The panel comes from whatever owns maintenance access (maintenance_hatch, service_panel(wires) or panel()), so declaring both here and in a hatch can't clash; machine_frame needs a panel and says so at boot if there isn't one
wall_machine(...)machine_basics without anchoring, plus wall_mount
maintenance_hatch(cover =, wires =, emag =, lock = TRUE, panel_needs_cover_closed = FALSE)compartment(BAY_HATCH, door = CAP_COVER), the cover you pass (default cover()), panel, wires(wires) when given, lock, emag(emag) when given, and the rules between them (the ID lock works only with the cover and panel closed; the panel opens only with the cover closed when panel_needs_cover_closed). A machine passes its wire set and its emag effect here instead of declaring wires and emag again

Plain bundles stay plain procs: console(board), atmos_device(uses_power, unwrench_delay), force_pry(), component_swap(type, delay). They return entries or parts, nest freely, and can't be addressed as a unit; the capabilities inside them can.

Replaces:

  • Every library constructor's private gating parameters (needs, else_say, works_*, log, open_tool, ...); cap_gating().
  • capability(KEY, entries..., settings = list()) and its hand-copied settings list; lib_op and the OP_SHAPE_* presets; /datum/capability/condition (now a capability granted with a duration).
  • Hand-written capability state: bit words such as COVER_OPEN | COVER_REMOVED, req_set/req_clear on them, and their READS_AS accessors (cover_is_open, panel_is_open, is_locked, is_emagged).
  • The untyped cap_data(holder, KEY)["x"].
  • The ~40 holder-interface procs on /atom (B2); RefreshParts() and parts(slots) (now components(slots)); deconstruct(board) and the machine dismantle = parameter (now frame =).
  • emag_act() and DECLARE_EMAG*; EMP_DISABLE, REFLECTS and hand-rolled EMP code; block(chance) (now blocks(chance)); the reach(range) item capability (now reach = on a provider); label() (now labelable(), since label("text") names an op).
  • om_attach behaviours; cap_trait(...) (now trait(...)).

12Construction

construction(start(STAGE_X), stage(STAGE_X, parts..., from = previous, undo = derived), ..., dismantle(parts..., ruined(condition, parts...)), at(BAY_X)) STAGE_DEF(group, name) // defines STAGE_<GROUP>_<NAME> and the stage key "<group>_<name>"; its text is MSG_DEF(stage/<group>/<name>, ...) built(E, STAGE_X) // TRUE once E's ladder is at or past that stage built_material(E, STAGE_X) // the material actually used at that stage, recorded at build; null if it took none // ruined(): when the condition holds, dismantling yields its parts instead (scrap rather than a reusable frame)

A build stage is an op (construction.build:<stage>) whose effect is "advance to this stage". Its undo is another op (construction.undo:<stage>), derived from the input part. Stage ids come from STAGE_DEF, so a misspelt stage is a compile error. The stage id is the first argument, and from = and undo = are named, so they come last. "Stage" here always means a build stage; the four steps of an op are op stages (section 9).

STAGE_DEF(intercom, frame)
STAGE_DEF(intercom, wired)
STAGE_DEF(intercom, boarded)
STAGE_DEF(intercom, secured)

/proc/intercom_frame()
	return construction(start(STAGE_INTERCOM_FRAME),
		stage(STAGE_INTERCOM_WIRED, stack(/obj/item/stack/cable_coil, 5)),
		stage(STAGE_INTERCOM_BOARDED, item(/obj/item/circuitboard/intercom), put_in(SLOT_CONSTRUCTION),
			then(TYPE_PROC_REF(/obj/item/radio/intercom, seat_board))),     // a global bundle names holder procs by type
		stage(STAGE_INTERCOM_SECURED, tool(TOOL_SCREWDRIVER)),
		dismantle(tool(TOOL_WELDER), becomes(/obj/item/frame/intercom)))

CAPABILITIES(/obj/item/radio/intercom,
	intercom_frame(),
	extend("construction.build:intercom_wired", wait(4 SECONDS)))         // the stage key is the STAGE_ id without its prefix, lowercase
ForwardDerived undoRefund
item(T) with put_in(SLOT_CONSTRUCTION)An empty hand takes it out (take_out(SLOT_CONSTRUCTION))The same item: SLOT_CONSTRUCTION is the ladder's own slot, so inserted items are its owned contents
stack(T, n)The material's tool (cable: wirecutters; sheets: wrench or welder)n units of the material actually used (recorded at build)
tool(Q)The same tool, reversed verbNothing
Anything elseundo = list(parts...), or undo = null for no way backFrom its parts

Write undo = only when the way back differs from the derived one. undo = list(tool(TOOL_WRENCH)) after tool(TOOL_WRENCH) adds nothing.

  • Hooks know their direction: then() going forward, undone() going back. undone() is a construction-only part, like ruined(). A preset is a global proc, so the holder procs it names are TYPE_PROC_REF(/type, x), never PROC_REF(x).
  • Dismantling: dismantle(parts...) runs its parts, then the holder goes: becomes(T) replaces it, and without becomes() it is deleted (any spawns() are dropped first).
  • Branches: from = STAGE_X or from = list(STAGE_X, STAGE_Y) lets one stage have several ways in or out (girder to wall, or girder to reinforced wall). A stage that works at any point is from = ANY_STAGE. A from that names a stage the ladder doesn't declare is a boot error.
  • Keys: the build op is "construction.build:<stage>", the undo op "construction.undo:<stage>" and the teardown "construction.dismantle". extend, without and perform_op address them, and the build resolves each literal against the declared stages.
  • Priority: when the same tool both builds and undoes from a stage, build wins a click and the menu offers both. A ladder that wants the undo to win a click says so on that stage with extend("construction.undo:<stage>", priority(above("construction.build:<other>"))).
  • State: the current stage is the capability's tracked state, a STAGE_* id. built(E, STAGE_X) and req_built(STAGE_X) read it, so a typo can't compile. start(STAGE_X) is the ladder's starting stage, and a subtype that begins part-built writes configure(CAP_CONSTRUCTION, start = STAGE_X).
  • Operability: the capability contributes req_built_final() to STAT_OPERABLE, so an unfinished machine doesn't work, and no type keeps its own maintenance flag.
  • Text: a stage's description is the MSG_DEF(stage/<group>/<name>, ...) that STAGE_DEF requires, and messages come from the tool profile's verbs. "Next: wrench it into place" is the next stage's message.
  • Bay: at(BAY_X) says which compartment's bay the ladder works inside, so a stage that moves an item in or out of SLOT_CONSTRUCTION needs that bay open, with the compartment's reason (section 8).
  • Conservation: the round-trip test covers every ladder, branches included.
  • Presets are plain bundles that return a construction(...): machine_frame(board), girder(), mech_chassis(...), door_assembly(). A machine takes one through machine_basics(frame = ...) or declares it directly.

Replaces: cap_construction with stage() (24 parameters), branch() (16) and ladder_options() (21); the construction step() and string stage names; insert(T) as a construction input (now item(T) with put_in()); build_insert/build_wire/build_fasten/build_weld/build_step/build_any_order; ladder_dismantle; /datum/construction_graph (16 files); state-var ladders in tool-act overrides; stage-name comparisons in hooks; hand-written refund hooks.

13Look, UI, verbs, prompts

Look

/obj/machinery/power/apc/draw(datum/look/look)      // an output: reads generated, runs once per frame after a read changed
	look.variant("broken", !operable && integrity_broken)
	look.layer("charging", charging)
	look.glow("screen", operable ? "on" : null)

CAPABILITIES(/obj/machinery/light,                                // excerpt: the full list is in 16.11
	look_layer("bulb-broken", when = nameof(bulb_broken)),          // declarative layers need no draw() at all
	sound_loop(SFX_LIGHT_HUM, when = nameof(on)),
)

// A disguise rewrites what the thing is called; a body is one composite of named layers, cached by what changes it.
/obj/item/clothing/mask/chameleon/draw(datum/look/look)
	look.identity(name = disguise_name, desc = disguise_desc)
/mob/living/carbon/human/draw(datum/look/look)
	look.composite("body", key = list(species.id, skin_tone, body_markings), layers = species.body_layers)
  • Look builder: the only writer of overlays, underlays, icon state, emissives, levels, names and descriptions. Its calls are look.variant(name, on), look.layer(name, on, under = FALSE), look.glow(name, state), look.level(plane, layer), look.identity(name =, desc =) and look.composite(name, key =, layers =), plus the transforms look.set_dir(), set_color(), set_alpha() and set_transform(). Everything else on /datum/look is internal. A builder call takes a value, and an entry takes a condition, so when = appears only on entries (look_layer, sound_loop, examine_line).
  • Output order: the engine runs a type's capability outputs first, then its own draw, ui_data, push_to_rust and examine, so an override does not call ..() to get the capabilities. Where a parent's body remains (a base type's draw), that proc is SHOULD_CALL_PARENT(TRUE), and DreamChecker flags a subtype that forgets. The standard outputs have fixed arguments: draw(datum/look/look), ui_data(datum/act/A), examine(datum/act/A) and push_to_rust(); any other output declared with outputs() takes (datum/act/A).
  • Names, descriptions and underlays: a computed name or description is look.identity(), and an underlay is look.layer(name, on, under = TRUE). Light is not a look call: STAT_LIGHT_RANGE (and the light's colour and power, declared the same way) are stats the engine applies, so look.light() is gone.
  • Composites: a layered body (species body, limbs, markings, hair, each slot's clothing) is built by look.composite() from named layers and cached by key, the list of inputs that change it, in the shared composite cache. Only the composite whose key changed is rebuilt. A worn item adds its layer to the wearer's composite through while_slotted(SLOT_ANY_WORN, look_layer(...), on = ON_HOLDER). overlays_standing and the hand-rolled mutable /icon caches go away.
  • Icon state names follow the standard dashed convention (<base>-<variant>, <base>-<layer>), checked by look_lacks() and the icon tests.
  • Flashes: a short-lived overlay is a flash(layer) feedback part, or look_flash() from code.
  • Banned: update_icon(), add_overlay and queue_icon_update() in content.

Examine

examine_line(text | PROC_REF, when =) entries, plus examine(datum/act/A) as an on-demand output for anything computed (A.actor is the examiner). Capabilities contribute their own lines (cover state, construction progress, "Next: wrench it into place"), and the engine adds them before the type's examine() runs.

UI

CAPABILITIES(/obj/machinery/power/apc,                          // excerpt: the full list is in 16.1
	interface("APC", title = "Area Power Controller"),            // declares the window and adds its open op
	ui_shape(operating = UI_BOOL, channels = UI_LIST(UI_ROW(name = UI_TEXT, mode = UI_NUM))),
	op("set_channel", ui_act(arg_choice("channel", APC_CHANNELS), arg_choice("mode", POWERCHAN_MODES)),
		then(PROC_REF(set_channel_mode))),
	op("breaker", ui_act(), toggles(nameof(operating))),         // ui_act() takes the op's key as its action name
)
// No lock clause: lock() gates every TAG_UI op (section 11).

/obj/machinery/power/apc/proc/set_channel_mode(datum/act/A, channel, mode)   // UI args arrive as typed parameters; the build checks the names against arg_*
	...

/obj/machinery/power/apc/ui_data(datum/act/A)       // an output: pushed at most once per tick, only to open windows, only when a read changed
	return list("operating" = operating, "channels" = channel_list())

// Generated from ui_shape and the declared ops (build.sh ui-types), so the interface can't drift from the server:
//   type ApcData = { operating: boolean; channels: { name: string; mode: number }[] };
//   type ApcActions = { breaker: {}; set_channel: { channel: string; mode: string } };
  • UI buttons are ops. Their arguments are typed and validated before the handler runs, by the same validator the prompts use. The same requirements apply as for clicking: a locked APC refuses the button and the click alike, and the reason shows in the window.
  • Opening: interface(window, title =, rights =, host =, input = hand()) declares the window and adds its open op, "interface.<window>.open", on input. By default that op accepts the physical routes and ROUTE_INTERFACE, so a silicon opens the window remotely; extend("interface.<window>.open", via(...)) narrows it. rights = adds req_rights(), and host = PROC_REF redirects to another datum. opens_ui() is the effect part for an op with a different input, and ui_open(E, actor) from code is perform_op(actor, E, "interface.<window>.open"). Code that ran as a window opened (the old ui_opening and ui_opened) is on_op("interface.<window>.open", parts...).
  • Window access is a requirement. Range, consciousness and rights are requirements on the open op, with generated reads, and the window stays open only while they hold. Walking away or fainting refuses one, and the window closes showing the reason. Nothing polls.
  • Data and shape: ui_data(datum/act/A) returns the data, and ui_shape(...) declares its keys and types: UI_BOOL, UI_NUM, UI_TEXT, UI_LIST(shape) and UI_ROW(field = shape, ...). TypeScript types are generated from the shape and from the window's ops, including each op's name and typed args. Test builds validate every push against the shape. A capability's own data is merged under caps.<key> and has its own ui_shape. ui_static_data(datum/act/A) is cached.
  • Transport: the tgui transport is a host system (section 2) that owns the window pool, prewarm, assets and open, close and transfer. A window is pushed in phase R after a read it uses changed, at most once per window per tick. The polling fire() and the update_uis INVOKE_ASYNC are deleted.
  • Banned: SStgui.update_uis, tgui_act/tgui_interact overrides, and hand parsing of params.

Verbs and abilities

// A gameplay ability is an op. menu() lists it under the one "Abilities" verb-panel entry;
// button and bind add an action button and a keybind.
CAPABILITY_DEF(regeneration, CAP_REGENERATION, recharge = 30 SECONDS)
	return list(
		op("regenerate", menu(button = "regenerate", bind = "shift+r"),
			cooldown(recharge), then(TYPE_PROC_REF(/mob/living, regenerate))))   // a definition body names holder procs by type
  • Abilities are ops, never verbs. Every menu() op the actor can use is listed under one verb-panel entry, "Abilities", with refused ones greyed and their reasons. button = gives the op an action button on the HUD whose state (ready, cooling down, refused) is an output with generated reads. bind = registers a keybind that performs the op, through the input inbox like any key. A bind that collides with another is a boot error. A species, trait or item gives an ability by granting the capability that holds the op.
  • Verbs are for client, OOC and admin commands only: verb_entry(path, login =, when =) on a type. The layering lint rejects verb_entry under code/content.
  • Runtime verbs: grant(E, granted_verb(path), source), and grant(E, hidden_verb(path), source) to hide one.
  • The verb store is the only writer of a verbs list.
  • Admin and debug commands: ADMIN_VERB(name, rights, "Name", "Description", VERB_CAT_X, args...) and DEBUG_VERB(...) with the same arguments, plus admin_require() / admin_can(). The body receives the calling client as user, so it never reads usr (16.9). Categories are the VERB_CAT_* defines: the admin, debug, mapping, server, OOC, preferences and IC families stay for verb entries, and the 50 VERB_CAT_ABILITIES_* categories go with the ability verbs, now ops under "Abilities".

Requests and prompts

// Inside any trigger. asks() is a suspension point in the Do list: the parts after it run once the answer is in,
// and costs are spent only after the last asks() is answered, so cancelling costs nothing.
op("rename", label("Rename"), in_hand(), asks(/datum/prompt/text/rename), then(PROC_REF(apply_name)))
op("smash", hand(), confirms("Really smash it?"), then(PROC_REF(smash)))   // confirms("text") is asks(/datum/prompt/yes_no, question = "text")

/datum/prompt/text/rename
	question = "What do you want to call it?"
	max_length = MAX_NAME_LEN

/obj/item/dogtag/proc/apply_name(datum/act/A)
	var/datum/prompt/text/rename/R = A.answer           // the answered request, typed
	name = R.value

// Outside a trigger (a system asking ghosts, a vote): an owner for the callback, and an explicit answerer.
request(src, /datum/prompt/yes_no/ghost_role, then(PROC_REF(role_answered)), answerer = ghost, role = role)

A request is anything that waits for an answer. There is one concept, /datum/request, in three families:

FamilyAnswered byKinds
/datum/prompt/*A playertext, number, list, checklist, yes_no, color, colormatrix, mob, typepath, bitfield, file, icon, form (below)
/datum/io/*A backendsql, http, rustg_job, path (served by a budgeted path system, so bot AI asks for a path instead of spinning on a mutex)
/datum/client_query/*The client's own interfacewinget, measure_text, ...
  • Shared by every kind: typed fields set by named args and checked at build time (role = role above), weak handles (an entity-typed field reads null once the entity is gone), a timeout, a re-check on answer, cancellation when the owner dies, and metrics.
  • Two call forms: asks(/datum/request/x, field = v, ...) inside a trigger, whose answer is A.answer; request(owner, /datum/request/x, then(PROC_REF(done)), field = v, ...) outside one. then runs done(datum/act/A) on the owner, with A.answer set; prompts also take answerer =. await() stays inside the kernel and the request layer. SQL goes through /datum/io/sql/* and sql_write() only.
  • Re-check: an answer re-runs the op's when and needs parts before the rest, so a prompt can't be answered into a state that no longer allows it.
  • Stale windows close themselves. An open prompt watches the op's requirements through their generated reads. When one refuses, the window closes and shows that reason.
  • One open prompt per answerer per op. Asking again focuses the window that is open. An open asks() takes the actor's one wait slot (section 9).
  • notify(user, text, title =) is an info popup. It is an output: nothing to answer.
  • Prompt-kind hooks. A kind may override three procs, each x(datum/act/A) with src the prompt: prepare() fills its choices before the window opens, valid() checks the answer itself (type, range; the answer is value), and on_cancel() runs when the answerer says no, closes the window or times out, or a requirement closes it. Checks on the world (still adjacent, still holding it) are requirements on the asking op, never valid().
  • UI args are shorthand over prompt kinds. arg_num(name, min, max), arg_int(name, min, max) (bounds optional), arg_text(name, max), arg_choice and the rest name the same validators, and arg(/datum/prompt/number/transfer_amount) uses a declared kind directly; the handler's parameter is named for the kind's last path segment (transfer_amount).
Prompt kindAnswer (value)It validates
textA stringLength, required
numberA numberMinimum, maximum, integer
listOne of the choicesThe choice is in the list
checklistA list of choicesEach is in the list, count limits
yes_noTRUE or FALSEAbsorbs confirm and a two-button alert
colorA colour stringIt parses, so no try around rgb2num
colormatrixA colour matrixTwenty numbers in range
mobA mob (weak)It is one of the candidates
typepathA type pathIt descends from the root type
bitfieldAn int of flagsOnly declared flags
fileAn uploaded fileAccepted types and size. A native client dialog, so the request layer runs it as a client query
iconAn icon fileSame: a native client dialog, never tgui
formTyped fields, one per questionEach field with its own kind. Several questions in one window; a second asks() replaces A.answer, so keep several answers with a form

Each kind carries its own validation, timeout and default. savefile_close(S) is the named form for the del() of a savefile. Banned: raw input(), alert(), tgui_input_*, NewQuery() and Execute().

Messages, sounds, effects, chat

These stay as they are, and feedback parts use them:

  • Messages: MSG_DEF / MSG_DEF_SELF message types with %U%, %T% and %I% tokens, used by says(); act_message() for code outside a trigger.
  • Sounds and effects: play_sfx(atom, SFX_ID) with SOUND_SET rows, and the plays(SFX) feedback part; fx_sparks().
  • Chat: to_chat with the span_* helpers.

Replaces:

  • Appearance: APPEARANCE_TEMPLATE, DECLARE_APPEARANCE*, APPEARANCE_LEVEL/EMISSIVE/SLOT, appearance_overlays(), update_icon() overrides and calls; the look calls look.state, look.part, look.overlay, look.gauge, look.light, look.hide/look.show and look.set_layer (now variant, layer, glow, level, a stat for light); overlays_standing and mutable /icon caches.
  • UI: DECLARE_UI*, UI_DATA*, UI_ACT*, UI_SUBACT, DECLARE_UI_MODAL, DECLARE_UI_STATE, ui_prepare/ui_opening/... hook procs, tgui_data naming, the ui_window(...) entry (now interface(window, ...)), the polling update_uis.
  • Verbs and abilities: DECLARE_VERB*, GRANT_VERB*, verb(path, ...) entries (now verb_entry(), client and admin only), and /datum/ability with its HUD button (now a menu(button =, bind =) op).
  • Prompts and requests: om_ask, act_ask, topic_ask, verb_ask, client_ask, rerun_ask(_on), flow_ask, om_ask_sequence, om_flow_start, prompt_flow, ask(...) outside triggers, io_request, /datum/om/prompt (now /datum/prompt, one kind of /datum/request), confirm and alert (now yes_no, notify()), and arg_* as a second validator.
  • Topics: TOPIC_ACTION.

14Domains

Damage

Entry points (bullet_act, hitby, ex_act, emp_act, fire_act, blob_act, melee, shocks) build a pooled packet and run the /datum/act/hit/<entry> action. The pipeline then runs in order:

  1. Requirements: immunities and godmode, as stats.
  2. Takeovers: extend(/datum/act/hit/x, instead(parts..., order = ORDER_EARLY | ORDER_NORMAL | ORDER_LATE)): a shield that absorbs the hit whole, a mirror, the APC's blob rip. chance() inside means "takes over with this probability". A taken-over hit stops here and publishes its notice with replaced = TRUE.
  3. Adjustments: extend(/datum/act/hit/x, adjusts(packet.amount, scale = 0.7, when = PROC_REF(shield_up))) modifies the packet in flight (ablative shields, armour that works as a hook). It never re-enters the action.
  4. Mitigation: armour stats, materials, resistances.
  5. The sink: damage_sink(), which is integrity for objects and injure() for mobs.
  6. The notice: /datum/notice/hit/<entry>.

Integrity's broken state is tracked; atom_break() and atom_fix() publish it. Library pieces: emp_disable(), reflects(), blocks(). Repair is a fixes() part, or repair_damage(). A worn piece of kit reaches the wearer through while_slotted, not through a hand-written hook on the mob:

CAPABILITIES(/obj/item/clothing/suit/armor/reflective,
	while_slotted(SLOT_ANY_WORN,                         // declared on the item: while it's worn, its wearer gets these
		blocks(10),
		reflects(PROJECTILES, 30),
		on = ON_HOLDER))

SLOT_ANY_WORN matches any worn equipment slot. on = ON_HOLDER grants the entries to the entity whose slot the item sits in, and the grant is released when the item leaves the slot, is deleted or its wearer is. A container type uses the other end, on = ON_CONTENTS, to give something to whatever it holds (stasis beds, cryo, bellies, borg docks).

Body and medical

The body's domain API stays: injure(), mend(), fully_heal(), vitality(), is_critical(), injury_load(), afflictions and their triggers, treatment tags, diagnosis and physiology. It plugs into this model in five places:

  • Factors are stats. Slowdown, accuracy, evasion, incoming injury, healing received, metabolism and the rest are STAT_* ids in the same namespace as every other stat; the old BF_* ids are gone and their combine rules are rows of the section 5 table, with one stat per armour kind and per injury category. Afflictions, reagents, species, traits and forms contribute to them with contributes(), and a worn item with while_slotted(SLOT_ANY_WORN, contributes(...), on = ON_HOLDER).
  • Timed statuses are numeric stats. STAT(/mob/living, stun, MAX, reapply = REAPPLY_MAX, units = LIFE_CYCLE) declares one, and status_at_least(), status_set(), status_adjust(), status_end(), status_remaining() and has_status() are the sugar over its holds (section 5). Immunity is immune_to(STATUS_X), a contribution that zeroes the status.
  • Body effects are capabilities granted with a duration. A species' own entries (immunities, abilities) are declared with SPECIES_CAPABILITIES(/datum/species/x, entries...) and granted by species_capabilities() while the species is set (section 11).
  • Periodic medical work (contagion spread, symptom ticks) is every() or a Life step.
  • Traits that change vars declare contributes() instead of a var_changes table, so releasing the trait restores the true value. The one sanctioned reflection write is vars_write(E, name, value), used by VV, SDQL, the serializers, DNA and record copies and the map loader; it routes through the setter or a hold, and its own file is its only exemption.

Mob Life

  • The sequence: Life is /datum/sequence/life. Its steps are procs declared with seq_step(), ordered by runs_after =, and their conditions are typed facts on the frame (F.alive, F.placed). Subtypes override the proc, and traits and organs add steps through granted capabilities.
  • Outputs: HUD and vision are outputs() entries with generated reads, at_most and a when = nameof(client) gate. client is a tracked base var that Login and Logout write through its setter. Nothing calls a refresh.
  • Stats: movement and acting are stats (STAT_CAN_MOVE, STAT_CAN_ACT), and what routes an actor may use is STAT_ACTS_VIA.
  • Observers: ghosts, AI eyes and blobs use every() on their own type.

Machines

  • Working: operable is a stat (STAT_OPERABLE). powered(), breakable(), construction(), emp_disable() and admin holds contribute to it.
  • Power draw: power_draw is a SUM stat: each load contributes its own draw (off, idle or active, from whatever is running), and the power system reads the total.
  • Work: machine work is every(interval, ..., when = STAT_OPERABLE), or a narrower condition.
  • Systems: machines join theirs through powered_by() or membership().
  • Components: the components() capability derives stats such as efficiency and capacity from the installed components.

Simulation and Rust

  • The frame: the native system runs vg_frame in phase N.
    • Its outbox becomes CHANGED publishes, notices (/datum/notice/native/<event>) and band crossings for on_cross.
    • DM reads Rust-owned values as native("key") (cached per frame), and those reads are generated like any other.
  • Pushes: DM-owned values Rust needs are tracked vars. push_to_rust() is an output that writes them with native_write(), and its reads are generated, so it runs once per frame after one of them changes.
  • Thresholds are watches: on_cross(native("pressure"), list(50, 100), parts..., urgent =).
  • Gas and heat: gas mixtures stay opaque handles (return_temperature(), set_temperature(), vg_pump, ...). The heat API stays: get_temperature(), add_heat(), set_temperature().
  • Bindings: the generated vg_* procs and the ABI handshake stay.
  • Scheduling: DM timers and keys live only in the kernel. The Rust world scheduler keeps native watches and rate models.
  • Lighting is a host system with a hot link() (below).

Host systems

The machinery that wraps BYOND (the input inbox, the database, the tgui transport, garbage, the ticker, air, verdigris and lighting) is made of ordinary systems, defined in section 2. What the domains add:

  • Lighting stays on the DM side. Its corner and source graph is the one place the relation rules would cost too much, so it declares link(/datum/light_source::effect_str, /datum/lighting_corner::affecting, hot = TRUE): a symmetric pair with no index and no publish, torn down from both ends by that one line. A hot link is for engine graphs like this one, not for content.
  • The tgui transport owns window delivery, and the windows themselves are declared with interface() (see UI above).
  • World queries. world/Topic belongs to the input system, which routes the status, ping and server-tool queries to registered handlers: world_query(KEY, PROC_REF(x)) on a system, with x(datum/act/A) reading the parsed query from A.args and returning the reply.

World systems

Every former world service and subsystem is a system:

  • Machines and mobs: machine, mob, plant.
  • Infrastructure: power, chemistry, radiation, sound, POIs, supply, research, chat, statpanels.
  • Rounds and events: votes, events, expedition, flight, sun, night shift, explosions, turf cascade.

Each has needs, every() work, membership (with roles = list(...) declared, and join(system, E, source, role) naming one), and an api.dm. On-demand systems park while idle. Long one-shot work, such as the map loader, expedition site generation, the POI queue and zone cleanup, runs as a job(owner, PROC_REF(step), budget =, then =): step returns JOB_MORE or JOB_DONE, budget = is a percent of a tick, and the job dies with its owner.

Clients

A /client is not a datum, so everything the game attaches to one lives on its /datum/client_session, created at Login and reached as C.session. Skybox, the view-variables panel, preference panels, the admin holder, per-client lists and client-only images are owned relations of the session (owns_one, owns_many), and the session's on_destroy is the client's teardown. A registry keyed by ckey is members_of(key, role) keyed by that ckey. client_kick(C, reason) and world_shutdown() are the only callers of del() on a client or the world. A client is ready when its data has arrived, and Login delivers the client's verbs then, so no code waits ten ticks for them.

Movement and containment primitives

Moving a thing without the world noticing is a named primitive: abstract_move(AM, dest) (camera eyes, jaunt holders, area membership during map load) runs no Moved, Crossed or Uncrossed, and dview(center, range) is the pooled view list. The commit points that write loc live in code/engine/containment and nowhere else.

Mob AI

ai(behaviours) is a capability. It attaches the brain of code/modules/combat_ai, and that module stays: a /datum/ai_brain scores each /datum/ai_behavior flyweight with evaluate() (which returns DQAI_RESULT(score, target)) and runs the winner through start(), tick() and stop(). Those are the brain's calls, not the engine's, so they keep their signatures. What changes is how the brain runs and what behaviours do:

  • Scheduling: the brain's strategic loop (2 s) and tactical loop (0.25 s) are keyed every() entries of the capability on the mob's own clock (CLOCK_OWN), so suspension pauses them. They park by relevance and wake on the notices and changes a behaviour's eval_triggers name, which become on_notice and on_change hooks.
  • Acting: a behaviour acts through perform_op() and perform_intent(), the same ops, requirements and messages a player uses, instead of calling attack_target() or shoot_target() directly. A behaviour that opts in to player use (get_player_verb_info()) becomes a menu() op under "Abilities", replacing the "Use Combat Move" dispatcher verb.
  • Pathing: dq_pathfind() becomes a /datum/io/path request, answered by the budgeted path system, so a bot step never sleeps.
  • State: aggro, targets and stances are stats and relations; cooldowns stay on the brain or the source item.

15Tooling and enforcement

One analysis engine

Every build-time check and generator is a query in one tool, the analysis engine at tools/analyze/, on one model of the tree. Today about 40 Python lints and check_grep.sh each re-read the ~5,800 files of the tree, one after another: a check_ratchets.sh run takes about 495 s and check_grep.sh about 146 s. The engine loads the tree once, in parallel, strips comments and strings once, and keeps a content-hash cache, so an incremental run rescans only the files that changed.

  • When. Phase 0b builds it, in Rust, and ports every existing lint to it, the legacy-form ratchets included, with the same baseline files and the same output. Each lint switches over only when a parity harness shows that the engine and the old script report identical findings on master. check_ratchets.sh and check_grep.sh then become thin wrappers over the engine, and are deleted. Phase 1's E5 adds the semantic queries below to the same engine; it does not start a new tool (section 19).
  • Backend. Text rules and parse-aware rules run on SpacemanDMM's dreammaker parser behind a backend trait. The new incremental DM compiler's front end and analysis API (parse, type and proc resolution, queries) replaces it behind that trait once it passes the phase-1 readiness gate. The engine only analyses: BYOND's dm.exe still produces the .dmb.
  • One of each. One diagnostics format, file:line: [rule] message -- hint, tagged with the tier that found it (below). One exemption mechanism, tools/ci/lint_scopes.toml and ALLOW(lint/CODE), parsed once, so the unused-ALLOW check is exact: the engine knows what fired. One incremental daemon, shared by agent worktrees.
  • Outside it. TGUI (Biome, tsc), Rust (clippy, cargo test), the icon repack and the Verdigris bindings keep their own toolchains. DreamChecker keeps running as a second opinion until the engine covers its rules.
QueryWhat it doesFrom
Today's lintsEvery ratchet and check_grep.sh rule, against today's baselines, at parity with the script it replacesPhase 0b
Generated readsWhat every condition, requirement and output reads, through A.actor, A.held, A.target and READS_FROM helpers, and the rank order that follows from it (section 7)E5
Semantic checksKey resolution for op, capability, stat, stage and source ids; handler signatures; context escape; ACT_TRY pairing; purity; layering; duplicate definitions; the compiled-table diff; the shim registryE5 and L
Generated identifiers and typesFrom ACTION(): /datum/act/x, act_x() and the notice type (FIXED actions are the only kind with no refuse path). From STAT, cap_keys, STAGE_DEF, SOURCE_DEF and CAPABILITY_DEF: their ids, accessors and constructors. All of it is written to code/engine/_generated/Phase 1
UI typesTypeScript from each ui_shape() and the window's ops, with each op's name and typed args (build.sh ui-types)Phase 2
The .dme generator, doc snippetsInclude order by layer, FILE_DIR and non-code includes, #ifdef UNIT_TESTS test includes (section 22); the DOC_SNIPPETS build (below)L; phase 2
Explain dumpsexplain_type offline, from the compiled tablesE5

Build errors, by tier

A string key that doesn't resolve to a declared identifier is a build error, and every build error says which tier found it:

TierFound byExamples
DM compileThe compiler and DreamChecker, on every buildA misspelt STAT_, STAGE_, SRC_ or COVER_ id; act_fall() called with the wrong argument types; a missing ..() where SHOULD_CALL_PARENT applies; a relative type path
GeneratorThe analysis engine's queries, on every buildUnknown reads; an unannotated global call inside a requirement, condition or output (add READS_FROM(arg)); a cycle in the stat and condition graph; a stat var written outside the engine; a tracked var written outside its setter; ACT_TRY without act_done or act_cancel; a stored context; UI arg_* names that don't match the handler's parameters
BootThe table builder, at server start (the unit-test boot runs it on every CI run)An op, capability or tag key in extend, without, configure, performs, on_op or priority(above()) that resolves to nothing; an unknown capability param; one capability key with different params (naming both lines); a duplicate op key without extend; two ops on one target with the same input, intent and tier; a requirement with no resolvable reason; consumes() beside an item-sourced hold; a second CAPABILITIES for a type; a hold or contribution on a type that doesn't declare the stat; a performs cycle; a from naming an undeclared stage; a keybind collision
TestTest buildsA requirement, condition or output that writes, publishes or messages (the purity guard); a UI push outside its ui_shape; perform_op with an unknown key; the drift, missed-wake and condition audits

Lints that stay

Retirement lints, one per legacy form, are shrink-only, reach zero, and switch on in the phase where their replacement lands (a column of the curated mapping, section 17). The lints below guard invariants that outlive the migration. Like every lint they are queries on the engine, and each has an end state, so "zero" means something:

LintProtectsTodayEnd state
initWhat an Initialize override may do5,204 baseline rows: initialize and unreasoned count the same ~2,470 overrides; 79 LateInitializeOne rule. An override stays only for per-instance state and carries a reason code. LateInitialize is after_init; world reads at init move to on_holder_init or membership(). The // INIT: form is deleted

lifecycle_counts (with qdel_src)Deleting through the lifecycle verbs1,868 qdel( sites, and 711 qdel(src) rows counted a second timeOne lint. qdel(src) is banned (use consume(), expire(), replace_with() or slot_clear()). Any other qdel carries a reason code
instance_listA per-instance list costs memory480 ALLOWs, 361 of them generated d: codesLIST_STATE(T, var, LAZY | ROWS | SET | TABLE) passes, an undeclared list() var fails, system types and test directories are exempt by scope
