Exemption policy

About 1,590 ALLOW tags exist today, a third of them for gaps this design closes and many for places no lint should look at. One mechanism covers every exemption:

  • Directory exemptions live in one lint-scope file, tools/ci/lint_scopes.toml, not in 24 lint sources: unit tests and benchmarks, vendored code (code/host/vendor/), and the engine layers for the lints about engine internals. A site in an exempt directory needs no annotation, and the scope file is the only place to read what is exempt.
  • Reason codes. An annotation is ALLOW(lint/CODE): reason, with a closed code list per lint (BYOND_BOUNDARY, POOLED, TEST_FIXTURE, ...). The engine rejects an unknown code.
  • Unused-ALLOW check. An annotation whose line no longer triggers its lint is an error, so annotations can't outlive their cause or sit on text the lint never reads. The engine makes it exact from phase 0b.
  • Reason quality. A reason shorter than about 20 characters, a bare word (kernel, MC, the accessor), "see above", "(allowlist)", "not edited here", a plan-phase label, or a generated d: code is rejected. The same reason repeated more than a set number of times needs a reason code instead.
  • Ceilings. Each lint has a ceiling on its annotation count, recorded in the repo and only allowed to fall. A new annotation needs an old one removed.
  • One mechanism. This replaces ALLOW(base_vars), // INIT: and ALLOW(init), and the allowlists embedded in check_grep.sh and its side files (the input ladder, tool istype and verb allowlists, the deadline-polling and rust-core lists). Every exemption is countable.

Explain tooling

explain_click(actor, target, held, gesture)      // admin verb "Explain Interaction": every candidate, the filter that dropped it (entry file:line), the winner, its tier
perform_op(actor, target, key, held, trace = TRUE)
explain_type(/obj/machinery/power/apc)           // the merged table, each part tagged with its origin: type, capability or extend site (file:line)
assert_resolves(actor, target, held, GESTURE_CLICK, "cover.open")   // the test helper

The compiled table is the product of four precedence levels, configure, without, bundles and granted capabilities, so source locations would otherwise be lost: the builder records __FILE__:__LINE__ per entry, and test builds keep an origin list per interned entry. When nothing resolves, the engine shows the best near-miss's reason (rate-limited), so a click never silently does nothing. After a passes() commit, later refusals are silent and explain_click prints the chain.

Tests

  • Kernel and tiers: kernel_test_begin()/kernel_test_end() drive the kernel in tests (they replace om_test_begin/om_test_end). Focused runs while developing; the normal tier at integration; the exhaustive tier in CI. Conservation tests for ladders; the op hard-case acceptance tests.
  • Primitives: TEST_RAW_SET(E, var, value) is how a fixture writes a framework-owned var (only under code/modules/unit_tests and benchmarks); await_ticks(n) is how a harness waits real ticks, in place of stoplag(); assert_resolves(); EXPECT_RUNTIME(call) for a call that is meant to fail.
  • Behaviour snapshots: a resolver-agnostic harness records, per type, actor stance, held item and target state, the message and the state delta, through whichever resolver is active. It replaces the interaction-id snapshots, and each old-API test has a listed replacement.
  • Doc examples are tests: the examples of section 16, and the snippets in sections 1 to 15 marked complete, are extracted and compiled by DOC_SNIPPETS (tools/ci/doc_snippets.py), required clean from phase 2. A snippet marked // excerpt shows part of a type that section 16 declares in full, and is not compiled on its own.
  • Benchmarks: scenarios for the new paths exist before phase 1, so master has numbers to compare with: 100 clicks per second on a 30-candidate stack, hover over 60 atoms, op-wait churn, stat-hold churn, a UI push storm with 20 open windows, output drain, and 1,000 idle machines.

Runtime audits, codemods and docs

ToolRole
Runtime auditsOutput drift, missed wakes, condition drift, ownership leaks (find-references), op contexts released, pools poisoned in tests. In production a watchdog frees any context held over two minutes with no pending op, and logs the creator
CodemodsOne idempotent script per mechanical form, in tools/dx/codemods/, on an AST rewriter over the analysis engine's parse tree (a regex can't safely rewrite nested INTERACT_*), with fixtures, idempotency tests and a key-collision report. The infrastructure lands in phase 2.5 (A4's 14, retargeted to the final forms)
DocsThis document becomes doc/rewrite/api.md plus chapters. AGENTS.md section 3 and section 9 are rewritten to these forms only in phase 2; until then an interim section says which forms compile today, and the archived OM docs are deleted afterwards

16Complex examples

Each example is complete for the parts it shows, and uses only forms from sections 1–15. From phase 2 they compile as DOC_SNIPPETS tests, together with the reference snippets marked complete (section 15); until then they are design sketches. The MSG_DEF lines behind each MSG(), and plain number defines such as BATON_HIT_COST, are left out.

A type has one CAPABILITIES list. Where an example declares a type that a reference section also samples (the human in section 3, the turret in section 5, the gripper in section 8, the stasis bed in section 10, the APC and the light in section 13), the example is that type's whole list, and the reference sample is marked as an excerpt. After each example, a note says what it shows and what it replaces.

16.1 APC: hatch, cell, construction, power, EMP, subversion, UI

/obj/machinery/power/apc
	var/cell_type = /obj/item/cell/apc        // the starting cell: a mapper or a subtype changes it here, and only here
	var/obj/item/cell/cell                    // the slot var cell_bay() works over
	var/area/area                             // link ends (below)
	var/obj/machinery/power/terminal/terminal
	var/mob/living/silicon/ai/hacker
	var/operating = TRUE                      // the breaker
	var/chargemode = TRUE
	var/coverlocked = TRUE
	var/equipment = POWERCHAN_ON_AUTO
	var/lighting = POWERCHAN_ON_AUTO
	var/environ = POWERCHAN_ON_AUTO
	var/nightshift_setting = NIGHTSHIFT_AUTO
TRACKED(/obj/machinery/power/apc, operating, chargemode, coverlocked, equipment, lighting, environ, nightshift_setting)

/obj/machinery/power/apc/high
	cell_type = /obj/item/cell/high           // no CAPABILITIES line: the type var is the one way to change the cell

STAGE_DEF(apc, frame)
STAGE_DEF(apc, board)
STAGE_DEF(apc, wired)
STAGE_DEF(apc, secured)

CAPABILITIES(/obj/machinery/power/apc,
	wall_machine(/obj/item/module/power_control, frame = apc_frame(), powered = FALSE),
	maintenance_hatch(                                        // compartment BAY_HATCH, cover, panel, wires, lock and emag, with the rules between them
		cover = cover(remove = force_pry(), replace = component_swap(/obj/item/frame/apc)),
		wires = /datum/wires/apc,
		emag = list(needs(req_not_subverted()), wait(0.6 SECONDS), sets(LOCK_LOCKED, FALSE)),
		panel_needs_cover_closed = TRUE),
	cell_bay(nameof(cell), at = BAY_HATCH, starts = nameof(cell_type)),
	powered_by(/datum/system/power, role = POWER_ROLE_SOURCE),
	emp_disable(between(24 SECONDS, 36 SECONDS)),
	subversion_reset(tool(TOOL_MULTITOOL), at(BAY_HATCH)),
	link(/obj/machinery/power/apc::terminal, /obj/machinery/power/terminal::master),
	link(/obj/machinery/power/apc::hacker, /mob/living/silicon/ai::hacked_apcs),
	link(/obj/machinery/power/apc::area, /area::apc),
	contributes_to(nameof(area), STAT_LIGHTS_NIGHTSHIFT, PROC_REF(wants_night_lights)),   // 16.11

	interface("APC", title = "Area Power Controller"),       // the window, and its open op "interface.APC.open"
	ui_shape(operating = UI_BOOL, chargemode = UI_BOOL, coverlocked = UI_BOOL,
		channels = UI_LIST(UI_ROW(name = UI_TEXT, mode = UI_NUM))),
	op("breaker", ui_act(), toggles(nameof(operating))),      // no lock clause: lock() gates every TAG_UI op
	op("chargemode", ui_act(), toggles(nameof(chargemode))),
	op("coverlock", ui_act(), toggles(nameof(coverlocked))),
	op("set_channel", ui_act(arg_choice("channel", APC_CHANNELS), arg_choice("mode", POWERCHAN_MODES)),
		then(PROC_REF(set_channel_mode))),

	extend("cover.open", needs(req(PROC_REF(cover_unlocked), because = MSG(apc/cover_locked)))),
	extend("cover.replace", needs(req_empty(nameof(cell)))),
	extend("cell_bay.cell.take", when(COVER_OPEN)),           // silent while closed, so an empty hand opens the window instead (16.1a)
	extend("cell_bay.cell.insert", needs(req_built(STAGE_APC_SECURED))),
	extend("construction.undo:apc_secured", needs(req_empty(nameof(cell)))),
	extend(CAP_LOCK, needs(req_not_subverted(), req_wire(WIRE_IDSCAN))),

	extend(/datum/act/hit/blob, instead(cuts_all_wires(), sets(PANEL_OPEN, TRUE), order = ORDER_EARLY)),
	on_notice(/datum/notice/slashed, then(PROC_REF(lights_out))),
)

/proc/apc_frame()                         // a plain bundle. It is a global proc, so holder procs are named with TYPE_PROC_REF
	return construction(start(STAGE_APC_FRAME),
		stage(STAGE_APC_BOARD, item(/obj/item/module/power_control), put_in(SLOT_CONSTRUCTION)),
		stage(STAGE_APC_WIRED, stack(/obj/item/stack/cable_coil, 10),
			needs(req(TYPE_PROC_REF(/obj/machinery/power/apc, floor_intact), because = MSG(apc/floor_blocks))),
			then(TYPE_PROC_REF(/obj/machinery/power/apc, make_terminal)),
			undone(TYPE_PROC_REF(/obj/machinery/power/apc, cut_terminal))),
		stage(STAGE_APC_SECURED, tool(TOOL_SCREWDRIVER)),
		dismantle(tool(TOOL_WELDER), becomes(/obj/item/frame/apc),
			ruined(TYPE_PROC_REF(/obj/machinery/power/apc, frame_ruined), becomes(/obj/item/stack/material/steel))),
		at(BAY_HATCH))
// floor_intact, make_terminal, cut_terminal and frame_ruined are ordinary (datum/act/A) procs, not shown.

/obj/machinery/power/apc/proc/cover_unlocked(datum/act/A)     // a condition: its reads (coverlocked, the cell hop, its charge) are generated
	return !coverlocked || cell_charge_percent(cell) <= CELL_BAY_LOW_PERCENT

/obj/machinery/power/apc/proc/set_channel_mode(datum/act/A, channel, mode)   // UI args arrive typed and validated
	switch(channel)
		if(APC_CHANNEL_EQUIPMENT)
			set_equipment(mode)
		if(APC_CHANNEL_LIGHTING)
			set_lighting(mode)
		if(APC_CHANNEL_ENVIRON)
			set_environ(mode)

/obj/machinery/power/apc/proc/lights_out(datum/act/A)         // a species' claw swipe is offered here only because this hook listens
	set_lighting(POWERCHAN_OFF)

/obj/machinery/power/apc/ui_data(datum/act/A)                // an output. Capability data is merged under caps.<key>, so there is no ..()
	return list("operating" = operating, "chargemode" = chargemode, "coverlocked" = coverlocked, "channels" = list(
		list("name" = "Equipment", "mode" = equipment),
		list("name" = "Lighting", "mode" = lighting),
		list("name" = "Environment", "mode" = environ)))

/obj/machinery/power/apc/push_to_rust()                      // an output: runs in phase R after a value it reads changed
	native_write(src, NATIVE_APC_OPERATING, operating && operable)
	native_write(src, NATIVE_APC_CHARGEMODE, chargemode)

What this shows. One list declares everything. The hatch takes the wire set and the emag effect as params, so the APC never declares wires or emag a second time. Every key is a declared identifier: STAGE_APC_*, COVER_OPEN, LOCK_LOCKED, and op keys namespaced by their capability, with the cell bay's discriminator (cell_bay.cell.take) and the window's (interface.APC.open). The lock gates the four UI ops without a clause on any of them. A blob hit is a takeover, not a damage row. The outputs need no ..().

What it replaces:

  • The MAINT bit, and the two hooks that kept it in step with the construction stage.
  • power_failed and its timed revert: emp_disable contributes to operable.
  • TRACKED_BRIDGED channel bridges and the manual changed(src) calls.
  • The UI_ACT table, and the lock check written into each handler.
  • The starting-cell Initialize, and configure(CAP_CELL_BAY, starts =) on subtypes beside the var.
  • set_nightshift(), called by the night-shift service on every APC (16.11).
  • The three damage rows and emag_act().

16.1a Reach: a closed cover and the cell

// What the APC above brings for its cell. cell_bay(nameof(cell), at = BAY_HATCH) declares two ops over its slot:
//   "cell_bay.cell.take"     hand(), when(nameof(cell)), take_out(nameof(cell))     tier TAKE_OUT
//   "cell_bay.cell.insert"   item(/obj/item/cell), put_in(nameof(cell))             tier PART
// maintenance_hatch() declares compartment(BAY_HATCH, door = CAP_COVER), so the cover closes the bay.
// The APC adds one line: extend("cell_bay.cell.take", when(COVER_OPEN)).
//
// 1. Empty hand, cover closed
//      cell_bay.cell.take is not matched: when(COVER_OPEN) is false, silently. interface.APC.open (tier DEFAULT)
//      is the only candidate left, and the window opens.
// 2. Empty hand, cover open, a cell inside
//      cell_bay.cell.take matches, and wins on tier TAKE_OUT. Require: the implicit "bay is open" requirement passes.
//      Wait: none. Do: take_out() runs the world action /datum/act/remove on the slot, /datum/notice/removed is
//      published, and whatever reads the cell (the charge, the look) follows through generated reads.
// 3. A cell in hand, cover closed
//      cell_bay.cell.insert matches (tier PART). Require: req_built(STAGE_APC_SECURED) passes, then the implicit bay
//      requirement refuses with COVER_OPEN's reason, "The cover is closed." The click does not fall through.
// 4. Target the cell itself (contents view, drag), cover closed
//      The reach gate drops every candidate: the cell's own ops, a tool used on it, drag-drop. None survives,
//      so the engine shows the gate's reason.
// 5. An AI or a borg acting on ROUTE_INTERFACE
//      That route never sees physical bays, so neither cell op is a candidate, whatever the cover.
//
// No APC code does any of this.

Each case is a different part of the pipeline. Cases 1 and 2 are Match: the take op exists only while the cover is open and there is a cell, so on a closed APC it is silently not a candidate and the click opens the window instead. Case 3 is Require: the cell is going into a slot at BAY_HATCH, whose compartment gate fails while the cover is closed, so the implicit requirement refuses with the cover's reason. Case 4 is the reach gate: the walk up the containment ledger from the cell to the APC fails at that slot, so the cell offers nothing. Because the gate dropped candidates and none survived, the engine shows the compartment's reason rather than doing nothing. The APC has no "is the panel open?" guard anywhere.

What "Explain Interaction" prints for case 1

Explain Interaction: Rae (human, empty hand) clicks the Engineering APC, GESTURE_CLICK
  intents      INTENT_USE                (bind profile "default", stance help)
  actor gate   pass                      acts_via = ROUTE_ALL
  reach gate   pass                      the APC is on an adjacent wall
  index        (INTENT_USE, nothing held): 3 candidates. Every other op needs something in hand or a UI route.

  #  candidate                       tier       result
  1  cell_bay.cell.take              TAKE_OUT   dropped at Match: when(COVER_OPEN) is false
                                                  code/content/machinery/power/apc.dm:52   extend("cell_bay.cell.take", when(COVER_OPEN))
  2  construction.undo:apc_board     TAKE_OUT   dropped at Match: the stage is apc_secured, not apc_board
                                                  code/content/machinery/power/apc.dm:71   stage(STAGE_APC_BOARD, ...), undo derived
  3  interface.APC.open              DEFAULT    WINNER: the only candidate left
                                                  code/content/machinery/power/apc.dm:42   interface("APC", title = ...)
     Require   window access: adjacent, conscious                                   pass
     Do        opens "APC" for Rae

  Not click candidates: breaker, chargemode, coverlock, set_channel (ui_act ops: ROUTE_UI only)

Every row names the entry that put the candidate there, with its file and line, because the table builder records them. The same trace prints from perform_op(actor, apc, "cell_bay.cell.take", null, trace = TRUE), and a test pins the behaviour with assert_resolves(actor, apc, null, GESTURE_CLICK, "interface.APC.open").

16.2 Airlock: bolts, electrification, AI control, wires, access, emag

/obj/machinery/door/airlock
	var/welded = FALSE
TRACKED(/obj/machinery/door/airlock, welded)
STAT(/obj/machinery/door/airlock, bolted, ANY)          // the AI, a cut wire, a lockdown and an admin can each hold it
STAT(/obj/machinery/door/airlock, electrified, ANY)     // timed holds for "30 seconds", untimed for "until released"
SOURCE_DEF(shock_wire)                                   // the wires' shock pulse: hold(src, STAT_ELECTRIFIED, source = SRC_SHOCK_WIRE, lasts = 30 SECONDS)
SOURCE_DEF(lockdown)                                     // a security lockdown: hold(door, STAT_BOLTED, source = SRC_LOCKDOWN)

CAPABILITIES(/obj/machinery/door/airlock,
	doors(open = PROC_REF(do_open), close = PROC_REF(do_close), autoclose = 15 SECONDS),   // "doors.open", "doors.close", DOOR_OPEN
	powered(POWER_CHANNEL_ENVIRON),
	access(),
	maintenance_hatch(
		cover = cover(open = tool(TOOL_SCREWDRIVER)),
		wires = /datum/wires/airlock,
		emag = list(then(PROC_REF(emag_open))),
		lock = FALSE),                                      // the door's ID check is access(), not a hatch lock
	contributes(STAT_BOLTED, req_wire_cut(WIRE_DOOR_BOLTS)),   // a cut bolt wire bolts the door
	extend(list("doors.open", "doors.close", "pry"), needs(
		req_is(STAT_BOLTED, FALSE, because = MSG(door/bolted)),
		req_is(nameof(welded), FALSE, because = MSG(door/welded)))),
	extend(list("doors.open", "doors.close"), needs(req_is(STAT_OPERABLE, because = MSG(door/unpowered)))),

	// Silicon control, in the door's own window. Each AI's holds have that AI as their source.
	interface("AiAirlock", title = "Airlock Control"),
	extend("interface.AiAirlock.open", via(ROUTE_INTERFACE), by(AFF_CONTROL)),   // silicons only
	ui_shape(bolted = UI_BOOL, electrified = UI_BOOL),
	op("bolts", ui_act(), toggles_hold(STAT_BOLTED, source = ON_ACTOR)),
	op("shock_off", ui_act(), releases(STAT_ELECTRIFIED, source = ON_ACTOR)),
	op("shock_30s", ui_act(), holds(STAT_ELECTRIFIED, lasts = 30 SECONDS, source = ON_ACTOR)),
	op("shock_on", ui_act(), holds(STAT_ELECTRIFIED, source = ON_ACTOR)),
	extend(TAG_UI, needs(req_wire(WIRE_AI_CONTROL))),
	extend(list("shock_30s", "shock_on"), needs(req_wire(WIRE_SHOCK))),

	op("pry", label("Pry open"), force_pry(), wait(5 SECONDS), when(not(DOOR_OPEN)),
		needs(req_is(STAT_OPERABLE, FALSE, because = MSG(door/powered_shut))),
		then(PROC_REF(do_open))),
	op("weld", tool(TOOL_WELDER), when(not(DOOR_OPEN)), toggles(nameof(welded))),

	// Touching a live door shocks you instead of doing what you meant. Remote and UI ops are not touches.
	extend(/datum/act/op, instead(when(STAT_ELECTRIFIED, req_on_route(ROUTE_PHYSICAL)),
		then(PROC_REF(shock_toucher)), order = ORDER_EARLY)),
)

/obj/machinery/door/airlock/proc/shock_toucher(datum/act/A)
	shock(A.actor, 100)

/obj/machinery/door/airlock/ui_data(datum/act/A)
	return list("bolted" = bolted, "electrified" = electrified)

What this shows. Two AIs bolt the door, and each one's hold has that AI as its source (source = ON_ACTOR), so one AI's "unbolt" releases only its own hold, and a deleted AI's bolts drop by themselves. An AI's "shock off" leaves a wire pulse (SRC_SHOCK_WIRE) running its 30 seconds. toggles_hold does the toggle that used to need a held_by() read. The door declares bolted and electrified itself, since doors() leaves bolts to the door type. One extend(TAG_UI, ...) puts the AI-control wire in front of every window button. The shock is a takeover of physical ops only.

What it replaces: string sources ("ai", "wire") and the one var that served every bolter; the ai_toggle_bolts and ai_shock handlers with their A.args["mode"] switch; separate wires() and emag() lines beside the hatch; intercept(); req_stat.

16.3 Portable turret: setting vs effective, EMP and emag, subtype params

/obj/machinery/porta_turret
	var/enabled = TRUE        // the player's switch: a setting
	var/lethal = FALSE
TRACKED(/obj/machinery/porta_turret, enabled, lethal)
STAT(/obj/machinery/porta_turret, armed, ALL)          // effective: whether it fires now

CAPABILITIES(/obj/machinery/porta_turret,
	powered(),
	breakable(),
	lock(),                                               // gates every TAG_CONTROL and TAG_UI op below
	contributes(STAT_ARMED, nameof(enabled)),
	contributes(STAT_ARMED, STAT_OPERABLE),               // power, damage, EMP and the emag's disable all arrive through operable
	emp_disable(between(6 SECONDS, 60 SECONDS)),
	on_notice(/datum/notice/hit/emp, then(PROC_REF(scramble_settings))),
	emag(then(PROC_REF(go_haywire)), disables_for = 6 SECONDS),
	popup_cover(raised_while = STAT_ARMED),
	every(PROC_REF(scan_interval), then(PROC_REF(scan)), when = STAT_ARMED, key = "scan"),
	interface("PortableTurret", title = "Turret Control"),
	ui_shape(enabled = UI_BOOL, lethal = UI_BOOL),
	op("enabled", ui_act(), toggles(nameof(enabled))),
	op("lethal", ui_act(), toggles(nameof(lethal))),
)
CAPABILITIES(/obj/machinery/porta_turret/alien,
	configure(CAP_EMP_DISABLE, lasts = between(1 MINUTE, 2 MINUTES), resist = 75),
	configure("scan", interval = 1 SECOND),
)

/obj/machinery/porta_turret/proc/scan_interval(datum/act/A)     // computed each run: fast while it has targets
	return length(targets) ? 1 SECOND : 3 SECONDS

/obj/machinery/porta_turret/proc/scramble_settings(datum/act/A)
	set_enabled(prob(50))
	set_lethal(prob(50))

/obj/machinery/porta_turret/ui_data(datum/act/A)
	return list("enabled" = enabled, "lethal" = lethal)
// scan and go_haywire are ordinary (datum/act/A) procs, not shown.

What this shows. enabled is what someone chose; armed is what is true now, a stat that the setting and operable both feed. An EMP is a timed hold on operable through emp_disable, so it ends by itself and never touches the setting. The scan work parks while the turret is not armed and wakes when it is, and the pop-up cover follows the same stat. The alien subtype changes params and retunes the keyed scan entry, with no code.

What it replaces: stat(nameof(armed), ALL, from = list(...)); req_clear(LOCKED) on each control; a toggle_setting by hand beside the same toggle in the UI; the hand-rolled EMP revert timer; polling process().

16.4 A human: Life, a stun, stasis, species, spontaneous vore

// /mob/living (code/base/). Its stats are the base table in section 5; these are its own entries.
CAPABILITIES(/mob/living,
	contributes(STAT_CAN_ACT, not(STAT_STUN)),           // the one stun path: nothing holds can_act by hand
	contributes(STAT_CAN_ACT, not(STAT_PARALYSIS)),
	contributes(STAT_CAN_MOVE, STAT_CAN_ACT),
	outputs(PROC_REF(life_hud), at_most = LIFE_PRESENT_MIN_INTERVAL, when = nameof(client)),
	outputs(PROC_REF(life_vision), at_most = LIFE_PRESENT_MIN_INTERVAL, when = nameof(client)),
)

TRACKED(/datum/vore_preferences, can_be_drop_pred, can_be_drop_prey)

CAPABILITIES(/mob/living/carbon/human,
	seq_step(PROC_REF(life_breathing), runs_after = LIFE_INPUT, when = PROC_REF(placed_and_alive),
		should_run = PROC_REF(life_breathing_due)),
	seq_step(PROC_REF(life_chemicals), runs_after = LIFE_BODY, when = PROC_REF(placed_and_alive),
		should_run = PROC_REF(has_reagents_to_process)),
	species_capabilities(),                               // the species' capabilities (its immunities, its abilities), granted while it is set
	owns_one(nameof(vore_prefs), /datum/vore_preferences),
	when(PROC_REF(spont_vore_on), spontaneous_vore()),    // takeovers of fall, stumbled_into and thrown_hit, attached while the prefs allow them
)

/mob/living/carbon/human/proc/placed_and_alive(datum/act/A)   // a condition over the frame's typed facts (section 3)
	var/datum/seq_frame/life/F = A
	return F.placed && F.alive

/mob/living/carbon/human/proc/spont_vore_on(datum/act/A)      // reads through the owned prefs: a generated hop
	return vore_prefs?.can_be_drop_pred || vore_prefs?.can_be_drop_prey
// life_hud, life_vision and the Life steps are ordinary procs, not shown.

// A stun baton. Its stun op has the baton as holder and the struck mob as target.
/obj/item/melee/baton
	var/on = FALSE
TRACKED(/obj/item/melee/baton, on)

CAPABILITIES(/obj/item/melee/baton,
	cell_holder(),
	op("toggle", in_hand(), toggles(nameof(on))),
	op("stun", at_target(/mob/living), hostile(), costs(RES_CHARGE, BATON_HIT_COST),
		needs(req_is(nameof(on), because = MSG(baton/off))),
		then(PROC_REF(stun)), plays(SFX_BATON_HIT), logs(LOG_ADMIN)),
)
/obj/item/melee/baton/proc/stun(datum/act/A)
	status_at_least(A.target, STATUS_STUN, BATON_STUN_UNITS, source = src)   // its own timer; a timed hold outlives the baton

// A stasis bed: while it works, its occupant's biology runs at a tenth of the speed.
CAPABILITIES(/obj/machinery/stasis,
	powered(),
	buckle(slots = 1),
	when(STAT_OPERABLE, while_slotted(SLOT_BUCKLE, holds(STAT_CLOCK_RATE_BIO, 0.1), on = ON_CONTENTS)),
)

A baton hit, then the bed:

  1. The stun op matches as an attack (hostile()). Require: the baton is on and its cell can pay. Do: the charge is spent, and status_at_least holds STATUS_STUN for BATON_STUN_UNITS Life cycles, sourced by the baton. can_act recomputes at once, so the mob's next click is refused by its own req_capable(). A species that grants immune_to(STATUS_STUN) reads 0 and refuses the hold, with no code here.
  2. The mob is buckled onto a working bed. The slot entry holds clock_rate_bio at 0.1. The mob's CLOCK_BIO runs at clock_rate times clock_rate_bio, so its Life frames, the stun's timer and every other status run at a tenth of the speed.
  3. The bed loses power. The entry-level when() detaches the while_slotted entry at the drain, and the hold goes. Unbuckling, or the occupant being deleted, ends it the same way. No code releases anything.
  4. The player turns off spontaneous vore in the vore panel. The setter writes the prefs, spont_vore_on reads them through the relation, and spontaneous_vore() detaches at the drain.

What it replaces: the stunned stat and the hold on can_act; the raw baton hold that bypassed the status layer and ended if the baton was deleted; the stasis bed's buckled/unbuckled notice pair, which put the hold on whoever did the buckling; set_stasis() calls; string facts in seq_step(when = list("placed", "alive")); refresh_hud() calls; Login and Logout hooks that re-check vore prefs.

16.5 Reagents: a beaker, a syringe, a donut

CAPABILITIES(/obj/item/reagent_containers/glass/beaker,
	reagent_container(volume = 60, transfer = list(5, 10, 15, 30, 60), lid = TRUE),
)
CAPABILITIES(/obj/item/reagent_containers/glass/beaker/large,
	configure(CAP_REAGENT_CONTAINER, volume = 120),     // re-runs the body: the holder, the fill look and the transfer prompt follow
)

CAPABILITIES(/obj/item/reagent_containers/syringe,
	reagent_container(volume = 15, transfer = list(5, 10, 15)),
	extend("reagent_container.inject", wait(3 SECONDS),
		needs(req(PROC_REF(skin_reachable), because = MSG(syringe/armour_blocks))),
		says(MSG(syringe/inject)), logs(LOG_ADMIN)),
)
/obj/item/reagent_containers/syringe/proc/skin_reachable(datum/act/A)   // reads the target's suit through A.target: a typed hop
	var/mob/living/carbon/human/H = A.target
	return !istype(H) || H.can_inject(A.actor, FALSE)

CAPABILITIES(/obj/item/reagent_containers/food/snacks/donut,
	edible(bites = 3),
	reagents(20, add = list(REAGENT_ID_NUTRIMENT = 3, REAGENT_ID_SUGAR = 2)),   // the keys are defines, which expand to reagent ids
)

What this shows. The library owns the ops: reagent_container() brings pour, fill, splash, drink, inject, spray, the transfer-amount prompt and the fill look. The large beaker changes one param. The syringe keeps all of that and changes one op with extend: a wait, a requirement with a reason, feedback and an admin log. Because the requirement reads the target through A.target, the pending injection re-checks when the target puts a suit on, and is cancelled only if it now refuses.

What it replaces: hand-written inject, draw and set-amount ops beside the library's; afterattack overrides; Initialize procs that only add reagents.

16.6 A belly: prey, struggling, absorption, digestion work

/obj/belly
	var/digest_mode = DM_HOLD          // a setting: the vore panel's UI op writes it through set_digest_mode()
	var/escapechance = 0
TRACKED(/obj/belly, digest_mode, escapechance)

CAPABILITIES(/obj/belly,
	interior(escape_wait = between(10 SECONDS, 60 SECONDS), escape_chance = nameof(escapechance)),   // SLOT_BELLY_INTERIOR and "interior.escape"
	slot(SLOT_BELLY_ABSORBED, accepts = list(/mob/living)),
	while_slotted(SLOT_BELLY_ABSORBED, absorbed(), on = ON_CONTENTS),
	op("struggle", hand(), via(ROUTE_INSIDE), cooldown(2 SECONDS), priority(above("interior.escape")),
		then(PROC_REF(struggle))),                     // the click from inside; the escape is in the context menu
	extend(/datum/act/insert, needs(req(PROC_REF(prey_consents), because = MSG(vore/no_consent)))),
	on_notice(/datum/notice/inserted, then(PROC_REF(prey_entered))),
	every(VORE_TICK, then(PROC_REF(process_prey)), when = PROC_REF(has_prey_to_process)),
)

/obj/belly/proc/has_prey_to_process(datum/act/A)      // the digestion work parks while this is false
	return digest_mode != DM_HOLD && contents_count(src)

/obj/belly/proc/absorb(mob/living/prey)              // called by process_prey when absorption completes
	slot_transfer(prey, src, SLOT_BELLY_ABSORBED)     // the absorbed capability follows from the slot
// struggle, prey_consents, prey_entered and process_prey are ordinary procs, not shown.

CAPABILITY_TYPE(absorbed, CAP_ABSORBED, /datum/capability/absorbed)
/datum/capability/absorbed/entries()
	return list(
		contributes(STAT_ACTS_VIA, ROUTE_NONE, reason = MSG(vore/absorbed)),   // the actor gate: no clicks, UIs, abilities or commands
		contributes(STAT_SUSPENDED, TRUE),                                     // its clocks stop: no Life frames, no status timers
		extend(/datum/act/speak, instead(then(CAP_PROC(speak_to_pred)), order = ORDER_EARLY)),
		examine_line(MSG(vore/absorbed_examine)))

/datum/capability/absorbed/proc/speak_to_pred(datum/act/A)    // A.holder is the prey
	var/datum/act/speak/S = A
	var/mob/living/prey = A.holder
	var/obj/belly/B = container_of(prey)               // a spatial read, not a raw loc
	to_chat(B.owner, span_notice("[prey] whispers from within you: [S.message]"))

What this shows. Prey are the contents of slots. Absorption is a move to the absorbed slot, and while_slotted grants the absorbed capability with the slot entry as its source. Prey that leave the belly, however they leave (digested, released, escaped, deleted), lose everything it gave them, and no release code exists. Struggling and the interior's escape are both a hand from inside, so the struggle takes the click by a relative priority (the same-input rule, section 8) and the escape is offered in the context menu. Consent is a requirement on the insert action.

What it replaces: the absorbed var, every "is absorbed" check and the release code that reset it; raw holds on absorbed prey; the setting() entry for the digest mode; intercept() on speech; two ops with the same input.

16.7 Simple mob AI: the same ops a player uses

CAPABILITIES(/mob/living/simple_mob/animal/space/carp,
	ai(list(/datum/ai_behavior/idle_wander, /datum/ai_behavior/approach_threat,
		/datum/ai_behavior/melee_attack, /datum/ai_behavior/flee_low_hp)),
	natural_weapon(/datum/natural_weapon/bite, damage = 10),   // gives AFF_ATTACK, and the bite op a player-controlled carp uses too
)

// The behaviours stay the flyweights of code/modules/combat_ai. The brain scores them with evaluate() and runs the
// winner through start(), tick() and stop(). Those are the brain's calls, not the engine's, so they keep their signatures.
/datum/ai_behavior/melee_attack/evaluate(datum/ai_brain/brain, atom/source)
	var/mob/living/owner = brain.get_owner()
	var/mob/threat = brain.primary_threat
	if(!threat || !owner.Adjacent(threat))
		return null
	return DQAI_RESULT(40, threat)

/datum/ai_behavior/melee_attack/start(datum/ai_brain/brain, atom/target, atom/source)
	. = ..()
	if(. == DQ_BEHAVIOR_FAILED)
		return
	perform_intent(brain.get_owner(), target, INTENT_ATTACK)   // the resolution, requirements, cooldown and messages of a player's click
	return DQ_BEHAVIOR_DONE

/datum/ai_behavior/flee_low_hp
	eval_triggers = list(/datum/notice/injured)                // ai() hooks it with on_notice, and the brain re-scores

What this shows. ai(behaviours) is the capability section 14 describes. It owns the /datum/ai_brain, runs the strategic (2 s) and tactical (0.25 s) loops as its own keyed every() entries, and turns each behaviour's eval_triggers into on_notice hooks. A behaviour acts through perform_intent() and perform_op(), so a carp's bite has the same requirements, cooldown and messages as a player's, and an AI actor's busy policy is BUSY_REFUSE. Movement behaviours keep step_to(); long paths become a /datum/io/path request.

What it replaces: the get_ai_behaviors TYPE_TABLE (now the list passed to ai()); attack_target() and shoot_target() calls in behaviours, with the checkClickCooldown() test in front of them; DQAI_TRIGGER_* signal keys (now notice types); the OM behaviours /datum/om/behaviour/ai_brain/strategic and tactical and their om_attach calls; dq_pathfind().

16.8 A cyborg: providers and affordances

CAPABILITIES(/obj/item/gripper,
	provides(AFF_MANIPULATE | AFF_HOLD_SMALL, reach = 1, accepts = list(/obj/item/cell, /obj/item/circuitboard)),
)
CAPABILITIES(/mob/living/silicon/robot,
	provides(AFF_CONTROL, route = ROUTE_INTERFACE, reach = BORG_INTERFACE_REACH),   // it operates machines it can see; it has no hands
)
// A borg-only op adds  when(req(/mob/living/silicon/robot, of = ON_ACTOR))  to hide it from everyone else,
// or  needs(req(/mob/living/silicon/robot, of = ON_ACTOR))  to refuse them with the library's reason.
The borg, at the APC of 16.1What resolves
Gripper carrying a cell, cover opencell_bay.cell.insert. The carried cell is A.held, so item(/obj/item/cell) matches, and the gripper is the provider of AFF_MANIPULATE.
Empty gripper, cover opencell_bay.cell.take. The cell goes into the gripper, because its accepts list allows cells.
Welder module selected, cover openNeither cell op: a welder provides no AFF_MANIPULATE. Its own tool ops still match (construction.dismantle). With nothing else matching, the near-miss reason shows: "You can't do that without hands."
Across the roomROUTE_INTERFACE only: the window's buttons. The bay is physical and that route never sees it.

What this shows. The APC has no borg code. The ops say which affordances they accept (an input part implies one, section 8), and providers say what they add, how far they reach and what they carry. A borg's selected module is its held item, so swapping modules swaps providers.

What it replaces: attack_robot() overrides and isrobot() branches in handlers; the gripper's special-cased attackby forwarding; holds = list(...) on providers (now accepts =).

16.9 An ability, a granted capability, an admin hold

/mob/living/carbon/human
	var/dark_energy = 100
TRACKED(/mob/living/carbon/human, dark_energy)

// Shadekin phase shift. The shadekin species grants phase_shift() through species_capabilities() (16.4).
CAPABILITY_TYPE(phase_shift, CAP_PHASE_SHIFT, /datum/capability/phase_shift, cost = 50)
/datum/capability/phase_shift
	var/cost = 50
/datum/capability/phase_shift/entries()
	return list(
		op("shift", label("Phase shift"), menu(button = "phase_shift", bind = "shift+f"),   // "phase_shift.shift", under Abilities
			needs(req(CAP_PROC(can_shift), because = MSG(phase/no_energy))),
			then(CAP_PROC(toggle_phase))))

/datum/capability/phase_shift/proc/can_shift(datum/act/A)     // phasing back in is free
	var/mob/living/carbon/human/H = A.holder
	return granted(H, /datum/capability/phased) || H.dark_energy >= cost

/datum/capability/phase_shift/proc/toggle_phase(datum/act/A)
	var/mob/living/carbon/human/H = A.holder
	if(granted(H, /datum/capability/phased))
		revoke(H, /datum/capability/phased, source = A.source)
	else
		H.set_dark_energy(H.dark_energy - cost)
		grant(H, /datum/capability/phased, source = A.source)   // A.source is this op on this mob

CAPABILITY_TYPE(phased, CAP_PHASED, /datum/capability/phased, drain = 1)
/datum/capability/phased
	var/drain = 1                                                // energy per second
/datum/capability/phased/entries()
	return list(
		contributes(STAT_DENSITY, FALSE, priority = PRIORITY_FORCE),        // TOP: through walls and people
		contributes(STAT_INVISIBILITY, INVISIBILITY_SHADEKIN),              // MAX
		contributes(STAT_ACTS_VIA, ROUTE_MENU, reason = MSG(phase/phased)), // MASK_AND: abilities only, so the way back still works
		every(1 SECOND, then(CAP_PROC(drain_energy))))

/datum/capability/phased/proc/drain_energy(datum/act/A)
	var/mob/living/carbon/human/H = A.holder
	H.set_dark_energy(max(0, H.dark_energy - drain * A.dt / (1 SECOND)))
	if(!H.dark_energy)
		perform_op(H, H, "phase_shift.shift")           // forced back through the button's own op: its log and notice apply

// An admin holds a machine off. ADMIN_VERB passes the calling client as user, so there is no usr.
ADMIN_VERB(hold_machine_off, R_ADMIN, "Hold Machine Off", "Hold a machine off until you release it.", VERB_CAT_ADMIN, obj/machinery/M in world)
	if(held_by_source(M, STAT_OPERABLE, user.holder))
		release(M, STAT_OPERABLE, source = user.holder)
	else
		hold(M, STAT_OPERABLE, source = user.holder, reason = MSG(admin/held_off))

What this shows. The ability is an op with a menu() input: one entry under "Abilities", an action button and a keybind, refused with its reason when the mob has too little energy. Being phased is a granted capability, so every effect of it (density, invisibility, the actor gate, the drain) arrives and leaves at once with one grant and one revoke. Both capabilities are CAPABILITY_TYPEs, since their procs run as CAP_PROC(x) on the capability datum with A.holder the mob. The admin's hold shows in VV with the admin as its source, and only that admin's release removes it. Editing operable in VV is vars_write(M, "operable", TRUE, source = user.holder), a hold_override that forces a broken machine on until released. Neither fights the power system's own value.

What it replaces: /datum/ability and the "Act" verb; the ability's legacy verb; hand-rolled "can act except this ability" checks; capability(KEY, ...) beside a /datum/capability/x with no key; usr.client.holder in admin code.

16.10 A girder: branches, runtime materials, becoming a wall

STAGE_DEF(girder, standing)
STAGE_DEF(girder, loose)
STAGE_DEF(girder, struts)
STAGE_DEF(girder, reinforced)
STAGE_DEF(girder, wall)

/proc/girder()
	return construction(start(STAGE_GIRDER_STANDING),
		stage(STAGE_GIRDER_LOOSE, tool(TOOL_WRENCH)),                            // the undo is derived: wrench it down again
		stage(STAGE_GIRDER_STRUTS, tool(TOOL_SCREWDRIVER), from = STAGE_GIRDER_STANDING),
		stage(STAGE_GIRDER_REINFORCED, stack(/obj/item/stack/material, 1)),     // from struts: any material hard enough
		stage(STAGE_GIRDER_WALL, stack(/obj/item/stack/material, 2), wait(4 SECONDS),
			then(TYPE_PROC_REF(/obj/structure/girder, raise_wall)),
			from = list(STAGE_GIRDER_STANDING, STAGE_GIRDER_REINFORCED), undo = null),
		dismantle(any_of_tools(TOOL_WELDER, TOOL_PLASMA_CUTTER, TOOL_DIAMOND_DRILL), spawns(/obj/item/stack/material/steel, 2)))

CAPABILITIES(/obj/structure/girder,
	girder(),
	extend("construction.build:girder_reinforced", needs(req(PROC_REF(stack_can_reinforce), because = MSG(girder/too_soft)))),
)

/obj/structure/girder/proc/stack_can_reinforce(datum/act/A)   // reads the held stack through A.held: a typed hop
	var/obj/item/stack/material/S = A.held
	return S.material.integrity >= GIRDER_REINFORCE_MIN_INTEGRITY

/obj/structure/girder/proc/raise_wall(datum/act/A)
	replace_with(/turf/simulated/wall, built_material(src, STAGE_GIRDER_WALL),
		built_material(src, STAGE_GIRDER_REINFORCED), girder_material)    // the reinforcement is null on the plain branch

What this shows. from = gives a stage several ways in: a wall rises from a plain or a reinforced girder. Tool stages write no undo =, because the derived undo is the same tool reversed. stack() records the material actually used, so undo refunds that material and the wall is built of it. any_of_tools() uses the profile of the tool in hand, so the plasma cutter's speed shortens its wait with no special code. Stage ids are STAGE_DEFs, so a misspelt stage does not compile, and the stage's op key (construction.build:girder_reinforced) is checked against the declared stages.

What it replaces: the girder's state var and its tool-act ladder; om_task_start timed tasks for reinforcing and walling; becomes(PROC_REF(...)) and becomes(T, n); string stage names.

16.11 A world system acting across entities: night shift

SYSTEM_DEF(nightshift)
/datum/system/nightshift
	lane = LANE_WORLD
	var/night = FALSE
TRACKED(/datum/system/nightshift, night)

CAPABILITIES(/datum/system/nightshift,
	every(1 MINUTE, then(PROC_REF(check_time))),
	on_change(nameof(night), then(PROC_REF(announce_change))),
)
/datum/system/nightshift/proc/check_time(datum/act/A)
	set_night(using_map.get_nightshift())          // the setter compares: on most minutes nothing changes and nothing else runs

/datum/system/nightshift/proc/announce_change(datum/act/A)
	GLOB.priority_announcement.Announce(night ? NIGHTSHIFT_EVENING_TEXT : NIGHTSHIFT_MORNING_TEXT,
		new_title = "Automated Lighting System Announcement")

STAT(/area, lights_nightshift, ANY)                 // fed by the area's APC: contributes_to in 16.1

/obj/machinery/power/apc/proc/wants_night_lights(datum/act/A)   // SSnightshift.night is a system's tracked var: a static, reactive hop
	switch(nightshift_setting)
		if(NIGHTSHIFT_ALWAYS)
			return TRUE
		if(NIGHTSHIFT_AUTO)
			return SSnightshift.night
	return FALSE

CAPABILITIES(/obj/machinery/light,
	look_layer("bulb-broken", when = nameof(bulb_broken)),      // section 13's two entries: one list per type
	sound_loop(SFX_LIGHT_HUM, when = nameof(on)),
	contributes(STAT_LIGHT_RANGE, PROC_REF(lit_range)),          // a numeric contribution: the proc returns the range
)
/obj/machinery/light/proc/lit_range(datum/act/A)                 // the hop through power_area is a generated read
	if(!on)
		return 0
	return power_area?.lights_nightshift ? nightshift_range : brightness_range

What happens at dusk. check_time sets night. Inside that write, the stats that read it recompute in rank order: each area's lights_nightshift, from its APC's wants_night_lights contribution, then each of that area's lights' light_range, from lit_range. The engine applies the new ranges. The announcement is an on_change hook at the drain. An APC set to "always" or "never" keeps its area as it is, because its condition does not read night in those modes.

What it replaces: the night-shift service's loop over every APC, resumed across ticks with TICK_CHECK; apc.set_nightshift() and its tracked nightshift_lights copy; the earlier draft's re-hold of every area each minute; derive_nightshift_enabled().

16.12 Worn mirror plating: takeover and modification

CAPABILITY_TYPE(mirror_plating, CAP_MIRROR_PLATING, /datum/capability/mirror_plating, reflect_chance = 30, absorb = 0.67)
/datum/capability/mirror_plating
	var/reflect_chance = 30
	var/absorb = 0.67
/datum/capability/mirror_plating/entries()
	return list(
		// Take over: some beams bounce back. The hit ends here, and its notice is published with replaced = TRUE.
		extend(/datum/act/hit/projectile, instead(when(CAP_PROC(is_beam)), chance(reflect_chance),
			then(CAP_PROC(reflect)), order = ORDER_EARLY)),
		// Modify: a beam that gets through loses a third. No re-entry: the same hit goes on to armour and the body.
		extend(/datum/act/hit/projectile, adjusts(packet.amount, scale = absorb, when = CAP_PROC(is_beam))))

/datum/capability/mirror_plating/proc/is_beam(datum/act/A)     // A.holder is the wearer; the hit's fields are typed
	var/datum/act/hit/projectile/H = A
	return istype(H.packet.source, /obj/item/projectile/beam)

/datum/capability/mirror_plating/proc/reflect(datum/act/A)
	var/datum/act/hit/projectile/H = A
	var/obj/item/projectile/P = H.packet.source
	P.redirect(P.starting.x, P.starting.y, get_turf(A.holder), A.holder)   // back where it came from: if it lands, that is a new hit

CAPABILITIES(/obj/item/clothing/suit/armor/laserproof,
	while_slotted(SLOT_ANY_WORN, mirror_plating(), on = ON_HOLDER),     // declared on the item: its wearer gets the hooks
)

What this shows. The vest declares while_slotted(..., on = ON_HOLDER), so while it is worn its wearer is granted the plating capability, with the slot entry as source. A granted capability's hooks are candidates for actions on the wearer, so a projectile hitting the wearer meets the takeover first (ORDER_EARLY). when() gates it to beams and chance() makes it take over 30% of the time. When it does not, the hit goes on, and adjusts scales its damage before mitigation and the sink, without starting the action again. Taking the vest off releases the grant. The library's reflects() and blocks() are built this way.

What it replaces: DAMAGE_REACTION and REFLECTS rows; armour checks hand-written into the mob's bullet_act; a hook on the mob registered at equip and removed at unequip.

16.13 A prompt and a database answer in one op

/obj/machinery/librarycomp
	var/selected_id                                // the archive row picked in the window
TRACKED(/obj/machinery/librarycomp, selected_id)

/// One archive row. A request kind: its parameterized query, the field it binds, and the fields the answer fills.
/datum/io/sql/library_book
	query = "SELECT author, title, content FROM library WHERE id = :book_id"
	var/book_id
	var/author
	var/title
	var/content

CAPABILITIES(/obj/machinery/librarycomp,
	interface("LibraryComp", title = "Book Inventory Management"),
	ui_shape(selected_id = UI_NUM),
	op("select", ui_act(arg_int("id")), then(PROC_REF(select_row))),
	op("print", ui_act(),
		needs(req_is(nameof(selected_id), because = MSG(library/nothing_selected))),
		confirms("Print the selected book?"),                               // suspension 1: the yes/no window
		asks(/datum/io/sql/library_book, book_id = nameof(selected_id)),   // suspension 2: the archive query
		cooldown(6 SECONDS),                                                // a cost: spent only after the last answer
		then(PROC_REF(print_book))),
)

/obj/machinery/librarycomp/proc/select_row(datum/act/A, id)
	set_selected_id(id)

/obj/machinery/librarycomp/proc/print_book(datum/act/A)
	var/datum/io/sql/library_book/Q = A.answer      // typed: the latest answer is the query (the yes came before it)
	if(!Q.title)
		return                                      // the row was deleted between the click and the answer
	new /obj/item/book(drop_location(), Q.title, Q.author, Q.content)

/obj/machinery/librarycomp/ui_data(datum/act/A)
	return list("selected_id" = selected_id)

One press of Print:

  1. Match and Require. The button matches the "print" op. Its requirements run: window access (range, consciousness), a selected row, and the require half of the cooldown.
  2. The first asks(). Do reaches confirms() and suspends. The yes/no window opens and takes the actor's one wait slot. Pressing Print again focuses that window rather than opening a second.
  3. Stale windows close themselves. While the window is open, the op's requirements are watched through their generated reads. If the reader walks away, faints, or someone else clears the selection, a requirement refuses, and the window closes showing that reason.
  4. "No", or a closed window. The op ends there. The cooldown was never started, so nothing is spent.
  5. "Yes". The when and Require parts run again. Do reaches the second asks(): the request goes to the database system, which answers in phase K. Nothing blocks, and the op stays suspended.
  6. The answer. The requirements run again. Only now are costs spent: the cooldown starts. Then print_book runs with A.answer set to the answered query, typed. With two answers, A.answer holds the latest, which is why the query comes last; several answers needed together go in one /datum/prompt/form.

What it replaces: act_ask() with a positional message and its callback chain; om_io() with a raw query string, a parameter list and a callback taking (list/result, error); COOLDOWN_START before the query, which spent the cooldown even when the answer never came; tgui_alert_async; hand validation of params["id"].

17Old to new

What this is. What each form game code uses today becomes. This table is curated: the 332 forms with 20 or more uses, or with a change in what content writes, in 225 rows, plus the constant families and the base-type overrides. The full generated table (all 1047 macros, procs and type procs and 1996 constants game code uses, with verdict, new form and phases) is doc/rewrite/api_mapping.tsv, written by mapping.py. Section 18 lists what stays.

Count method (stated once). A use is counted in game code only: every .dm under code/ outside the framework directories, unit tests and generated code, after stripping comments and string literals. Macros and constants count identifier occurrences. Global procs count call sites. A type proc counts its overrides plus its call sites, except where the same name belongs to an unrelated proc, when only overrides count. Rows are keyed by qualified name (/type/proc/name), so an English word such as system, after, leave or stage is never a row by itself. The codemod sizing in section 19 uses these figures: om_after 1310, om_ask 980, status_at_least 645, om_task_timed 424, om_hook 218, GRANT_VERB 263.

Verdicts over all 1047 macros, procs and type procs: 563 replaced, 33 renamed, 227 kept (section 18), 205 internal to the old engine (goes with its layer), 19 deleted outright. The 1996 constants follow their families: 1565 kept, 254 replaced or renamed, 85 deleted, 92 internal.

Lint on in phase. Each retirement lint switches on in the phase where its replacement lands (section 19), not before: until then the old form is the one that compiles. 1 (E1) to 1 (E6) are the engine workers, 1S the service ports, 2 the library, 3 the codemods, 4 and 5 the machine and Life tracks, 6 the content waves. The TSV also gives the phase in which the old form is gone.

Requirement rows change the contract, not only the name. The old requirement procs (REQ_TARGET_STATE, REQ_ON, REQ_PROC) took (actor, target, held) or one subject and returned TRUE-or-reason, or a boolean beside a reason string. The new ones are req(PROC_REF(x), because = ...) with x(datum/act/A) returning TRUE or FALSE; the reason text moves into because =. The conversion is mechanical once the signature changes, but a return "reason" left in place would read as TRUE, so the codemod rewrites the returns together with the signature.

RefreshParts() definitions57replacedcomponents(slots) capability1 (E3)
emp_act / ex_act / fire_act / blob_act overrides31replacedhit action hooks (the entry points are adapters)1 (E4)
refresh_hud() / refresh_vision() calls16deleteddeleted (outputs refresh themselves)1 (E6)
Topic() overrides3replacedops with a topic("key", args...) input; the input inbox owns client/Topic2
tgui_act() overrides1replacedUI ops: op("key", ui_act("name", ...))2

18What stays as it is

227 of the 1047 forms game code uses stay, plus 1565 constants; the rest of this section groups them by family. The full list is the kept rows of doc/rewrite/api_mapping.tsv.

  • Rate limits and time. COOLDOWN_* (and COOLDOWN_KEYED_*), STAMP / ELAPSED, the time defines (SECONDS, MINUTES), and the after() family.
  • dt math helpers. approach(), decay(), chance_over(), move_toward(): what an A.dt handler calls. They are pure functions and keep their names.
  • Loot, maps and variants. DECLARE_LOOT and the LOOT_* rows, loot_spawn(), MAP_VAR, MAP_RESOLVER_VARS, and the variant tables (code/datums/variants/README.md).
  • Caching and tables. TYPE_TABLE*, GLOBAL_TABLE*, DECLARE_SHARED_CACHE* and CACHED*.
  • Containment and lifecycle. the lifecycle verbs (consume(), replace_with(), expire(), slot_clear()), on_destroy(), lifecycle_keep(), QDELETED; the spatial reads (contents_of(), locate_within(), FOR_CONTENTS, is_in_holder), the slot reads and moves (slot_item(), slot_contents(), move_into(), slot_remove()), the SLOT_ID_* ids and owner_of().
  • Registries and memberships. the REGISTRY_* ids, members_of(), join() / leave(), membership().
  • Statuses, traits and body effects. has_status, apply_body_effect, remove_body_effect, has_body_effect, set_stasis stay as named sugar over holds and grants, with typed ids (STATUS_X). BF_* body factors are now STAT_* in the same namespace (renamed, not kept).
  • Messages, sounds, chat. MSG_* and MSG_DEF, SOUND_SET, play_sfx(), fx_sparks(), to_chat and the span_* helpers.
  • tgui transport internals. the window pool, prewarm and assets, TGUI_WINDOW_*, ui_open() for code outside an op, and ADMIN_VERB for admin commands.
  • Kernel and system API. SYSTEM_DEF and the system hooks, seq_step() and seq_anchor, native_write(), PUBLISH / PUBLISH_CHANGE, TRACKED, grant / revoke / granted, rel_set / rel_add / rel_remove / rel_clear.
  • Domain APIs. the body API (injure, mend, fully_heal, vitality, factor), the gas mixture handle API, the heat API and the generated vg_* bindings.
  • Input and capability vocabulary. ROUTE_*, AFF_*, INTENT_*, GESTURE_*, CAP_*, BAY_*, keybind and input-adapter ids, and the library constructors section 11 names (cell_bay, gas_store, powered_by, ...).
  • Language helpers. GLOBAL_* declarations, CONFIG_GET, logging, rust_g wrappers, the is* type helpers and the LAZY* list macros.

The rule. An om_* form not listed in section 17 is internal to the old engine: content does not call it, and it goes with its layer.

19Migration plan

Phases 0 to 7, plus 0b (a clean base), 1S (service ports) and 2.5 (codemod infrastructure). Each phase lands on master when it passes its gate, under the existing rules: one worktree per worker; compile, focused tests while developing, one full suite at integration; fast-forward to master; follow-ups fixed on master. Phases 1 and 2 build what this document defines. Phase 2.5 builds the tool that moves callers, and phases 3 to 6 move every caller. Phase 7 deletes what is left of the legacy layer.

Three rules hold the plan together. Old and new forms coexist only through a small registered set of shims, never through ad-hoc wrappers (Coexistence rules). Every gate is a number measured against a pinned baseline commit (Gates). Every file under code/ has a destination, including the ~3,900 that never had a legacy form (section 22).

Nothing in phase 1 should start on a red, slow or noisy base. Order of work: land the safe branches, run the ratchets, clear the fix-now items, build the analysis engine and port the lints to it, then pin and measure.

The analysis engine

Each lint re-reads the tree. On a clean worktree a check_ratchets.sh run takes about 495 s and check_grep.sh about 146 s (sys_lint 139 s, api_lints 61 s, ownership 42 s, state_schema 39 s, lifecycle 33 s, scheduler 30 s, ...), because about 40 Python scripts and a grep script each read about 5,800 files one after another. Phase 0b builds the analysis engine of section 15 now, so every later phase works on one fast tool:

  • The tool. tools/analyze/, in Rust: one parallel load of the tree; a content-hash cache, so an incremental run rescans only changed files; comment and string stripping done once; text rules and parse-aware rules on SpacemanDMM's dreammaker parser behind a backend trait, so the incremental compiler's analysis API can replace it later; ALLOW annotations, baselines and exemptions handled once, with the same baseline files and the same output format as today.
  • Every lint is ported, the legacy-form ratchets included. A lint switches to the engine only when a parity harness shows the engine and the old script report identical findings on master. check_ratchets.sh and check_grep.sh then become thin wrappers over the engine, and are deleted once CI calls the engine directly.
  • Later phases extend it. Phase 1's E5 adds the semantic queries (generated reads, key resolution, signatures, layering and the rest) to this engine rather than starting a new tool, and phase 2.5's codemods rewrite on its parse tree.
  • Gate: parity on every lint, and a full run measured cold and incremental, with the numbers recorded in doc/rewrite/baselines.md. The target is seconds, not minutes.

AGENTS.md, step one

AGENTS.md says "write what exists on master today": a legacy form stays correct until its replacement has landed and its retirement lint is on (the "switches on" column of section 17). A table gives each target form and what to write today. AGENTS.md stays the source of truth for what compiles; this document is the source of truth for where the code is going. Step two, the full rewrite to the new forms, lands with phase 2.

Measurement

  • Pin. The baseline for phase 1 is the first master commit with green ratchets after the merges above. It is recorded in doc/rewrite/baselines.md, one row per phase: commit, bench run ids, noise floors.
  • New bench scenarios on the old paths. The eight scenarios in Gates are written against public entry points (Click, tgui_act, om_hold, changed), so master has a number for each before phase 1 builds a replacement.
  • Noise floors. Five A/A pairs per scenario at the pin (method in Gates).
  • Zero-caller sweep. Delete OM machinery that git grep shows unused outside its own file (unused COMPONENT_* flags, unused library checks). bundle.dm, check_library.dm and derived.dm still have callers (/datum/om/bundle/* in library.dm; 51 OM_DERIVE_FIELD uses in 46 files), so they stay until their steps.

Phase 1 · engines

Phase 1 opens with three short gates that can fail the plan cheaply, run in parallel by one Opus worker: the name spike, the analysis-engine readiness gate and E0. Then seven workers run in parallel, each in its own directory against E0's contracts. Each engine is gated by a small fixture built from test-only capabilities (declared under code/tests/engine/ and compiled only under UNIT_TESTS), so no phase-1 gate needs the library.

Opening gates

GateWhat it checksIf it fails
Name compile spikeOne scratch file declares every renamed form (STAT, outputs, stage, verb_entry, blocks, plays, variant, runs_after, delayed, accepts, components, look_layer, ON_HOLDER, kernel_urgent, ...) on the pinned BYOND build (516.1682 or later) with dm.exe and with the analysis front end, and git grep checks each against master's symbols. Zero keyword, built-in or existing-symbol clashes.The name is changed everywhere in this document before E1 starts; the result is recorded in section 21.
Analysis readinessThe new incremental DM compiler's front end (in development on codex/compiler-architecture) parses every .dm under code/ with zero divergence from the dreammaker parse (the (type, var, proc, include) tables are identical), resolves types, procs and vars, and can stand behind the analysis engine's backend trait. It is used as an analysis tool only: dm.exe still produces the .dmb, so nothing waits on the compiler's code generation or runtime parity.The engine keeps its dreammaker backend for E5, and the front end swaps in behind the trait when it passes. No other phase-1 work changes.
E0, contracts firstOne Opus worker defines, before any engine starts: the compiled-table schema (per-type sections by entry kind, interned entry struct layouts, key tables, precedence, __FILE__:__LINE__ origins kept in test builds); the base entry struct; the /datum/act field layout and pool interface; the generators' output formats under code/engine/_generated/; the semantic query interface E5 adds to the analysis engine; the test-only capability registry; stubs for every engine's public procs. Output: code/engine/ stubs and doc/rewrite/engine_contracts.md.Engines do not start. Gate: the stubs compile, DreamChecker is clean, and an Opus cross-review confirms each of E1 to E6 and L has its inputs and outputs named.

Phase 1 lands when the opening gates passed and the six engine fixtures, the L checks and the budgets in Gates hold on a full suite. The library examples are not a phase-1 gate: they need phase 2.

Phase 1S · service ports

The survey counted 56 ports: the 12 host subsystems (E6, because they need kernel internals) and 44 world services (here). 1S runs beside phase 2 and must land before phase 3 starts, so the codemods never touch a service twice. The port list is regenerated from git grep '^/datum/world_service/' at the start of 1S (it finds 49 types today, including the dq_yield_probe test probe and the machine and mob services that phases 4 and 5 dissolve).

  • Each port: /datum/world_service/x becomes SYSTEM_DEF(x) with needs (replacing boot_after and order_after), a lane, every() or an on-demand declaration, roles and membership where it keeps a roster, and an api.dm; state becomes VAR_PRIVATE; LAZY_SERVICE accessors become system access. Each port is written in final forms into code/world/systems (or code/domains/<domain> for a domain's own system) and the old file is deleted, so no later step touches it.
  • Five clusters, one worker each: round flow (antag, emergency shuttle, transfer, events, persist, maintenance); client and presentation plumbing (chat, runechat, statpanels, character setup, asset loading, skybox, vis overlays); simulation (radiation, explosions, turf cascade, motion tracker, solars, nightshift, planets, instruments, sounds); data services (supply, research, chemistry, circuits, xenoarch, mail, radio, vote, transcore); world content (expedition, flight, POIs, pAI, plants).
  • End: when the last service is ported, /datum/world_service, world_lanes.dm, LAZY_SERVICE, boot_after and order_after are deleted.

Phase 2 · library

Eight Opus workers, one per library area. Every capability in section 11 is rewritten as a CAPABILITY_DEF or CAPABILITY_TYPE in code/library/<area>; each old cap_* constructor becomes a registered shim over the new definition (see Coexistence rules) so unconverted callers keep working. B1, B2 and B3 resume on the new forms: B2's landed capabilities are re-expressed, and B1 and B3 start from their open branches.

The five helper files of the old library (_library.dm, checks.dm, presets.dm, entry_points.dm, tool_helpers.dm) are not ported: they become req_* constructors, plain bundle procs, req_empty_hand() and tool profiles.

Behaviour snapshot harness

The old gate said "the i7 snapshots pass unchanged". Those snapshots are keyed by interaction id and change when interactions become ops, so they cannot hold. Phase 2 builds a resolver-agnostic harness first, records it on the old resolver, and uses it as the behaviour gate for phases 2 to 6.

  • What it records: per (type, actor stance, held item, target state), the messages shown and the delta of tracked state, through whichever resolver is active, as one line per case.
  • Matrix: the types the I7 snapshots list today, about 390 (dq_i7_bulk_capture.dm 287, dq_i7_structures_bulk_capture.dm 85, dq_i7_items_bulk_capture.dm 18) plus the types in the four gate examples; the intents and gestures of the input table; a fixed set of 12 held items (empty hand, crowbar, screwdriver, wrench, welder, wirecutters, multitool, ID card, beaker, cell, a stack, a weapon); the target states each type declares.
  • Recording: at the pinned commit, on the old resolver. Golden files are checked in under tools/dx/snapshots/ and regenerated only with a recorded reason. A diff is either a bug or an entry in intended_changes.md with its reason; the gate is zero diffs not in that file.
  • The 10 hard cases of doc/rewrite/operations_and_actions.md section 7 (hand lost while holding, blocked hand recovers, two-handed port loss, borg gripper loss, feral mouth, contained actor, prompt returns after the target moved, explosion over many targets, notice deleting its source, two features enrolling one entity) are rewritten as assert_resolves tests and kept.

Examples as tests, and AGENTS.md

  • Doc examples compile as tests. From phase 2, tools/ci/doc_snippets.py builds the examples of section 16, and the reference snippets marked complete, under -DDOC_SNIPPETS and the build fails on any form not defined in sections 1 to 15. This also retires the 152 doc_snippets baseline rows as old docs are rewritten or deleted.
  • AGENTS.md, step two. Sections 3 and 9 are rewritten to the forms that exist, with the "on master" column that says what to write while a replacement is still landing. Reason codes and per-lint ceilings follow in 2.5.

Phase 2.5 · codemod infrastructure

Phase 3 runs scripts that rewrite thousands of sites, so the tool is built and tested first. A regex cannot safely rewrite nested macro calls such as INTERACT_ITEM(name, effect, REQ_ON(...), ...). Inputs: the analysis engine with phase 1's queries (parse tree, resolution) and, for each codemod, its target form from phase 2. Workers: one Opus for the rewriter and key-collision report, one Sonnet for fixtures.

  • AST rewriter. Built on the analysis engine's parse tree, with each macro call and its nested arguments as nodes. Edits are span replacements that keep comments and formatting. The flat scripts landed in 0b (timers, ownership, fields, damage reactions, default children, verbs, grants, reagents, events) are ported to it and retargeted to the final forms; INTERACT_*, UI_ACT and TOPIC_ACTION are new.
  • Fixtures per codemod: input and expected output for every shape; "already converted" inputs that must be no-ops; and negative inputs that must stay untouched and be listed as residue. a4-skel's fixtures are the seed.
  • Key synthesis. An op needs a type-unique stable key. It is derived from the entry's name (UI and topic ops reuse the action name). Where an inherited or capability-brought key collides, the codemod emits extend() if the old semantics appended and a macro-kind prefix if the entries were independent. The key-collision report lists every synthesized key and every resolution before the freeze window.
  • Residue. A dry run on today's tree reports, per form, the count it cannot convert. Those integers are committed in tools/dx/codemods/residue.json before the step's window opens (Gates).
  • Reason codes and ceilings. ALLOW(lint/CODE) reason codes and per-lint ceilings switch on in the engine, and each lint's existing annotations are re-coded (section 15's exemption policy). The lints themselves moved onto the engine in phase 0b.

Coexistence rules

For most of phases 2 and 3, old and new forms run side by side on the same entities. Each seam below has one direction, one mechanism and one removal point. Nothing else is allowed to bridge old and new.

SeamDirectionMechanismRemoved when
Events: PUBLISH and on_notice against OM_EMIT and om_hookBoth waysEach side emits the other's twin, generated from tools/dx/codemods/om_event_map.json (CI already checks the generated file). No hand-written bridge.Per event, when its last om_hook or EVENT_HANDLER converts (step A7). The last event deletes the map.

OM_FIELD itself at step A4.

  • One registry. Every shim is one line in code/engine/shims.dm (name, the step that owns it, the step that removes it). The shim lint fails if a shim is still present once its removing step's post/<step> tag exists, or if a shim exists without a line.
  • Retirement lints switch on with the replacement. A retirement lint turns on as a ratchet (no new site) the moment its replacement is on master, and becomes a hard ban in the commit that deletes the last caller. The "switches on in" column of section 17 says when. Before that, AGENTS.md says to write the old form.

Phase 3 · codemods, tree-wide

Phase 3 converts every mechanical form across the whole tree. One form per commit, one freeze window per step, run by the script from 2.5, with a full suite per commit. A step starts when its target form is on master and its codemod passes the 2.5 gates. The steps are ordered by what they need.

Polarity and signature (B2). A requirement is now a boolean plus a reason, and every proc takes (datum/act/A). Old REQ_ON(subject, proc, "reason") was already a boolean predicate with a separate reason. Old REQ_TARGET_STATE procs took (actor, target, held) and returned TRUE or the reason text. The codemod rewrites the proc head and every return, and moves the reason text into a generated MSG_DEF. A reason string left in a return would read as TRUE and silently allow the op, so E5 flags any requirement proc that can return anything but TRUE or FALSE, and the fixtures include that shape. A body that fits neither shape is left and counted as residue.

Mob and machine forms are not mechanical at tree scale. status_at_least (645) and the other status_* procs (names kept as sugar), apply_body_effect (175), add_trait/has_trait/remove_trait (42, 120, 25), set_stasis (18), incapacitated( (250) and the BF_* ids belong to phase 5. operable() (311) and stat_add/stat_remove (27, 14) belong to phase 4.

Freeze windows

  1. Announce the window one working day ahead with the step, its tag names and the dry-run report: converted count, residue count and the key-collision report.
  2. Tag master pre/<step>. While the window is open only the step's own commits merge to master.
  3. Run the codemod as one commit whose message names the codemod and its counts; then DreamChecker, the focused tests, one full suite and the bench scenarios named in the step's gate.
  4. Merge, tag post/<step>, close the window. A window is hours; one that reaches a day is split.

In-flight branches rebase by re-running the codemod, not by merging around it (tools/dx/codemods/rebase_branch <branch> <step>):

  1. Merge pre/<step> into the branch. These are ordinary conflicts; none come from the codemod.
  2. Run the step's codemod on the branch's tree. It is idempotent: master's sites are no-ops, and the branch's own new sites convert.
  3. Merge post/<step>. The codemod is deterministic, so branch and master made identical edits to every converted site and git merges them cleanly. A conflict left is in a line the codemod does not own.

Order against phases 4, 5 and 6

Phases 4 and 5 start only after phase 3 has landed in full, icon rename included, because the tracks edit the same machine and mob files the codemods rewrite. They may then run beside each other and beside the waves that are neither machine nor mob. Machine waves wait for phase 4; mob waves wait for phase 5.

Tags, revert and soak

  • Tags. pre/<step> and post/<step> around every tree-wide commit: the phase-3 steps, the icon rename and each move wave. archive/<branch> for abandoned branches.
  • Revert recipe, nothing stacked yet: git revert the step's commit.
  • Revert recipe, later steps stacked (steps build on each other, so reverting one alone is not mechanical): git switch -c redo/<step> pre/<step>; fix the codemod; re-run it and then every later step's codemod in order, applying each step's recorded post-fixes (tools/dx/codemods/post_fixes/<step>.patch, the hand edits made after the script ran); cherry-pick master's non-codemod commits made since pre/<step>; land through a new window. Each step's revert is tried once on a scratch branch before its window opens: it must build and pass the focused tests.
  • Soak windows. The tagged build runs on the live server for at least 7 days and at least 10 rounds, whichever is longer. The scripted checklist below runs at the start and after every hotfix. Exit: no stuck-machine, unclickable-object or death-spiral report, and no new error signature in the runtime log against the week before. One soak follows step B1 (click resolution only, since resolution order changes for about 4,300 sites); one follows phase 4 and one follows phase 5, each before the pipeline it frees is deleted. The deletion lands one phase after the last caller converts.
Checklist areaStepsPass

APCCover off with a crowbar, cell out and in, cover on, lock with an ID, breaker from the UI, an EMP pulse, AI hack and resetEach change shows in the sprite, the UI and the area's power
AirlockOpen, close, AI bolt and unbolt, pulse each wire with a multitool, cut and mend, emag, crowbar when unpowered, weld shut and unweldState, sprite and sound agree each time

Phase 6 · content waves

Waves W01 to W35 (about 1,900 files) finish what the codemods left. Their file lists are regenerated after phase 3 from the lints' output: the files that still carry residue, which means the semantic leftovers (wait() conversions the script could not classify, verbs it could not sort, requirement bodies that fit neither shape, instance lists that need judgment, slot() declarations). Each wave does four things in order:

  1. Convert the files in the wave.
  2. Prove them: zero old forms per file, tests, the behaviour snapshot harness for the wave's types, the construction round-trip where it applies, and the bench scenarios.
  3. Move them in a pure git mv commit, once the duplicate-definition lint and the compiled-table diff pass (section 22).
  4. Merge.

Staffing: four to six Sonnet workers at once; Opus reviews and lands each wave. Order: machine waves W26 to W34 after phase 4 (W34, medical machines, after occupant); mob waves after phase 5. Two waves are domain work rather than file lists, and have their own tests:

Phase 7 · delete and lock

  • Delete whatever is left of the legacy layer (section 20). Most of it is already gone; this is the sweep, not the first deletion.
  • Lints: every legacy-form lint is at zero, so it becomes a hard ban and its baseline file is deleted. Lints that stay run as queries on the analysis engine; DreamChecker is removed once the engine covers its rules.
  • Docs: delete the archived OM docs; convert this document to doc/rewrite/api.md and chapters; the coverage script finds zero uses of any deleted form.
  • Completion test (section 22): every legacy-form lint at zero and the move table complete. Then the exhaustive tier and the bench against the phase-0 pin.

Gates

Every gate is a number. The method is the same for all of them.

  • Pinned baseline. Each phase's baseline is a master commit recorded in doc/rewrite/baselines.md when the phase starts: commit, bench run ids, noise floors. A gate compares against its own phase's pin, so "today's master" never moves under it. Phase 7 compares against the phase-0 pin.
  • Noise floor. For each scenario, five A/A pairs at the pin (two independent runs of bench --runs=3 per pair). The floor n is the largest relative difference inside any pair, rounded up to the next 0.5%. bench-compare's significance test must also reject "no difference" before a regression is called.
  • Pass rule. A metric passes when new ≤ pin × (1 + max(n, p)), p being the budget in the table below. A budget of "pin" means p = 0: only noise is allowed. The budgets here are the defaults, ratified when 0b records the first noise floors.
  • Residue. One integer per retired form in tools/dx/codemods/residue.json, set by the step's dry run (the count the codemod reports it cannot convert) and committed before the window opens. A step's gate is its lint count at or under that integer. Every integer is 0 by the end of phase 6; nonzero residues are listed by file in the wave plan.

Bench scenarios

Added in phase 0b, before phase 1. Each drives public entry points that old and new code both offer (Click, tgui_act, om_hold or hold, changed), so master has a number for every path the plan replaces. They join the existing tick, idle, Life, power and boot scenarios.

ScenarioDrivesMetrics
click_resolve_30100 clicks per second for 10 seconds on a target whose candidate stack has 30 entries (hand, item, alt and drag mixed)p50 and p99 cost per click; ticks over budget
hover_60Hover over 60 atoms in turn (screentip and menu evaluation)Cost per hover; cache hit rate
ui_windows_2020 open UI windows whose data changes every tick (the UI push storm)Cost per tick; bytes pushed
op_wait_churn1,000 waits (prying, welding) started per second, 30% cancelledCost per start and per cancel; pending records left at the end (must be 0)
stat_hold_churn10,000 hold and release pairs per second over 1,000 entities with 3 sources eachCost per pair; recomputes per pair
output_drain5,000 entities dirtied in one tick, each with a look, a UI and a Rust outputPhase R cost; outputs run per entity per frame (must be 1)
idle_machines_1k1,000 settled machinesWork items run per tick; tick cost
apc_flip_50One APC channel flips with 50 dependent machines (E3's spike)Cost per flip; recomputes per flip
