2The APC, turret, airlock and beaker examples compile under DOC_SNIPPETS and pass their tests unmodified; behaviour-snapshot diffs not listed in intended_changes.md = 0 over the I7 types and the 10 hard cases; click_resolve_30, hover_60, ui_windows_20 at pin (p = 0); DOC_SNIPPETS failures = 0; the boot gate of phase 1 still holds with the library loaded (map load completes, initial evaluation silent, boot time p = 1%); AGENTS.md rewritten; every retirement lint whose replacement landed is on.
2.5Per codemod: a second run changes 0 lines (idempotent), two runs are byte-identical (deterministic), every fixture passes, and DreamChecker compiles a 10% sample of the converted tree. The key-collision report has 0 unresolved collisions on today's tree; residue.json has an integer for every form.
3Per step: lint count at or under its residue.json integer; full suite green; snapshot diffs not in intended_changes.md = 0; click_resolve_30 and idle_machines_1k p = 1%, boot memory not up; the revert tried on a scratch branch; the B1 soak passed.
4Machine tests green; power bench (APC, SMES, cells) and apc_flip_50 at pin; settled machines run 0 work items per tick in idle_machines_1k; soak passed.
5Life tick p = 5%; missed-wake audit 0 misses over a full suite; medical and body tests green; soak passed.
6Per wave: old-forms lint 0 in its files; duplicate-definition lint 0 new; compiled-table diff empty; idle tick p = 1%; boot memory at or under the previous wave; full suite.
7Exhaustive tier green; every legacy lint at zero with its baseline deleted; every scenario at or under the phase-0 pin; boot memory below the pin; completion test passes; coverage script finds 0 uses of a deleted form.

Gates that apply to every phase

  • Retirement lints: no new site of a form whose replacement has landed. Shrink-only baselines keep counting down.
  • Runtime audits: output drift, missed wakes, condition drift and reference leaks are clean in the full suite.
  • Behaviour: zero snapshot or hard-case diffs that are not recorded in intended_changes.md with a reason.
  • Plan lints: the shim lint and the duplicate-definition lint are clean.
  • Bench: tick, idle, Life and boot memory are measured before and after every engine, library and track phase.

Risks

RiskMitigation
Per-instance cost of stats, inline recompute on a fan-out, or part-engine dispatch overheadBitfield contributions, interned compiled tables and integer keys; E3's apc_flip_50 spike and the idle and boot-memory budgets in phase 1.
The analysis front end is not ready, or its parse diverges from SpacemanDMM'sThe readiness gate (0 parse divergence over every file) opens phase 1. If it fails, the engine keeps its dreammaker backend behind the same trait and the front end swaps in later. The engine only analyses: dm.exe still produces the .dmb. Every lint moved onto the engine in 0b only after its parity harness matched the old script, and DreamChecker keeps running until the engine covers its rules.
The 0b engine takes longer than plannedLints switch over one at a time behind the parity harness, so the old scripts keep gating master until each port matches. E5 needs the engine's load, cache and backend, not every port.
Generated reads cannot cover real DM (dynamic vars[], call, global helpers)E5's spike on 20 handlers with an oracle; the fallback is a checker over hand-listed reads; an unannotated global call is a build error carrying the READS_FROM hint.
Builds get slowerOne-file incremental analysis within 5 s and a full pass within 20% of a clean compile; one daemon shared by agent worktrees.
Codemods that subtly change semantics (the reagent add/starts class of bug, requirement polarity)One form per commit; fixtures with negative cases; exact-value tests along inheritance chains; the snapshot harness; E5 flags a requirement proc that returns text; Opus reviews behaviour diffs.
Source conflicts: open branches against tree-wide commitsThe triage table closes 21 branches before phase 1; freeze windows; in-flight branches rebase by re-running the codemod, not by merging; pre/ and post/ tags.
A shim outlives its phase and becomes permanentOne registry file; the shim lint fails once the removing step has landed.
A file move changes which definition winsThe duplicate-definition lint and the compiled-table diff gate every move wave; the legacy layer keeps today's order.
Merge hazards in generated files, baselines, maps and dmi filesGenerated files are never hand-merged (the .dme, _generated, baselines via --update). Waves never touch maps or icons (varmap and rename run centrally).
The size of the UI and interaction conversion (~4,300 sites)Skeleton codemods first, agents finish. Each UI op keeps its old action name, so TSX doesn't change. A soak follows B1.
Click behaviour changes from the new resolution orderThe snapshot harness encodes today's behaviour; passes() preserves pass-through; legacy entries resolve in the same pass until B1.
A pipeline is deleted before the live server has exercised its replacementTags, the revert recipe, soak windows, and deletion one phase after the last caller.
Gates drift with a moving baseline, or sit below the noiseA pinned baseline per phase; noise floors from A/A pairs; the significance test.
Agents write code that cannot compile because AGENTS.md describes the targetThe two-step AGENTS.md with its "on master" column; a retirement lint switches on only when its replacement lands.

Corrections to older docs. om_changed() does not exist: the archived docs still name it, but the real procs are changed() and om_raise_change(). SSbehaviours no longer fires; it is already folded into the kernel tick and only keeps boot, the audit switch and bench counters. Neither needs a mapping row, since section 17 lists only forms game code uses.

21Decisions settled here

The docs synthesis found places where two documents, or a document and the code, define different forms for the same job. This design settles each one. The losing form joins the deletion list. Rows are grouped by area; the section that defines a form is the one that covers its area.

Names and vocabulary

QuestionSettled asLosing form(s)
Names that clash with BYOND built-insSTAT(T, name, RULE, ...); outputs(PROC_REF(x), at_most =, when =); stage(STAGE_X, ...) for construction; verb_entry(path, login =, when =); blocks(chance); plays(SFX); configure(KEY, variant = T); components(slots), req_component(T), component_swap; look.layer(...), look_layer(name, when =). The phase 1 compile spike confirms each on the pinned BYOND build, and a clash renames the form everywhere before E1 starts.stat() entries, output(), step(), verb() entries, block(), sound() parts, as =, machine parts()/req_part/part_swap, look.part/look_part
Overloaded wordsOne meaning each. Setting is a player-chosen tracked var (a capability's knobs are its params). Part is an op part. Act is /datum/act, and the verb-panel entry is "Abilities". Hold is a stat hold (a provider's filter is accepts =). After is the timer after(): ordering is runs_after =, the holder-clock form inside an op is delayed(t, parts...), and hook order is order =. Stage is an op stage or a build stage, never unqualified. The definitions live once, in the front-matter Vocabulary section; the section 8 box is a pointer to it.Capability settings, holds = filters, the "Act" panel entry, the after = ordering argument, then_later, a separate vocabulary box per section
Effect targetsON_TARGET (default), ON_HOLDER, ON_ACTOR, ON_HELD; ON_CONTENTS and ON_HOLDER for while_slottedON_SELF, OP_ACTOR/OP_HELD
Duplicate spellingsitem(T) plus put_in(slot); becomes(T) replaces the holder and spawns(T, n) adds; costs(RES_FUEL | RES_USES | RES_CHARGE | RES_STACK, n) beside consumes(); label("Rename") names an op, with no null name argument; labelable() is the item capabilityinsert(T); becomes(T, n) and becomes(PROC_REF); fuel(), uses(), uses_charge(); a null op name
Library namingPlain nouns for every library constructor (cover(), panel(), reagents(), trait()). cap_ is reserved for framework procs (cap_data). A capability's params are its settings: CAPABILITY_DEF and CAPABILITY_TYPE below.The cap_<noun> prefix rule, cap_trait, a separate reach(range)

Conditions, requirements and the one signature

QuestionSettled asLosing form(s)
ConditionsA boolean with no reason: nameof(v) or a STAT_X / capability key id (a truthy test), not(), all_of(), any_of(), PROC_REF(x) or CAP_PROC(x) returning TRUE or FALSE, a library requirement's boolean view. Strings are never conditions.when = list("placed", "alive") and other quoted fact names
Requirement languageA requirement is a condition plus a reason, in one language. Library constructors carry a default reason (MSG_DEF(req/<ctor>, ...)); generic tests give one: req_is(KEY, value, because =), req_at_least(KEY, n, because =), req(PROC_REF(c), because = MSG(x)); a dynamic reason is because = PROC_REF(reason_proc). A requirement with no resolvable reason is a build error. needs() takes requirements only, and when() takes conditions.REQ_*/PRED_*/REQ_ON/REQ_BECAUSE/HOLD_* macros, /datum/predicate, REQ_FIELD*, else_say, procs that return null to allow or a reason, req_stat/req_set/req_clear, a bare nameof(v) as a requirement
One handler signatureEvery proc the engine calls is x(datum/act/A) with A.holder always set: effects, requirements, conditions, hook handlers, every() and sequence work, outputs, then(), because = PROC_REF, CAP_PROC. PROC_REF runs on the holder, CAP_PROC on the capability datum. Outside a trigger the kernel passes a reused, non-allocating evaluation context. safe_call is a kernel utility and the one exemption.The (mob/user, atom/holder, obj/item/held) global check procs, (holder) condition procs, two-argument handlers such as toggle_phase(M, A)
Relaxing a requirementRequirements take id = "x"; a subtype relaxes one with extend("cover.open", drop = "x"). any_of joins reasons, all_of reports the first failing. req(T, of = ON_HELD) is the type test; none_of is dropped.Replacing a whole op to drop one check
PurityTest builds run requirements and conditions under the same no-write, no-publish, no-message guard as outputs.Review-only convention
Why a gate refusedThe actor and reach gates filter silently so input can fall through. If nothing survives, the engine shows the best near-miss's reason, rate-limited. Holds on acts_via take a reason.Per-handler "you can't do that" messages for being absorbed, restrained or out of reach
Reaching a slotReaching the target is Match (silent). A slot declared at = BAY_X adds an implicit needs(bay open) to put_in and take_out; add when(...) to make one silent.Manual "is the panel open?" guards before reaching contents

Stats, sources and keys

QuestionSettled asLosing form(s)
Declaring a statSTAT(T, name, RULE, ...) declares the var, its default and the id STAT_NAME together, so they cannot disagree. A stat var set in a type definition is a type-level contribution.A stat(nameof(v), RULE) entry beside a separate var/ line
Combine rulesOne table, each rule with a base value: ALL, ANY, SUM, PRODUCT, MAX, MIN, TOP (highest priority, ties to the most recent), SET (the union of its members, no ties), MASK_AND, MASK_OR, FORMULA. The contradictions are fixed: power_draw is SUM, acts_via is MASK_AND, density is TOP, opacity ANY, and the bio clock runs at clock_rate × clock_rate_bio, both MIN.The earlier table with no bitmask rule and conflicting rules for power draw, stasis and the body factors
When a stat is currentStats recompute eagerly and inline: when a contribution, a hold or a read the stat depends on changes, it recomputes at once in build-time rank order, before the writer's next line (bounded by the cycle check and DRAIN_MAX_PASSES = 8). A stat var read is a plain var read and is always current. Only hooks and outputs wait for the drain."Pulled on read" (DM has no property getters, so a var cannot recompute when read)
Body factors and statusesBF_* factors are STAT_* ids in the same namespace; BF_ARMOR(kind) and BF_INCOMING(category) become one stat per kind (STAT_ARMOR_BLUNT, ...) and per category (STAT_INCOMING_THERMAL, ...). A status is a numeric intensity stat (STAT(..., MAX, reapply = REAPPLY_MAX, units = LIFE_CYCLE)). The sugar is defined on it: status_at_least(M, STATUS_X, units, intensity =, source =), status_set (= hold_until), status_adjust, status_end, status_remaining, has_status. Immunity is immune_to(STATUS_X), which sets the companion stat STAT_<NAME>_IMMUNE. Stuns are statuses, nothing holds can_act by hand, and there is no stunned stat.A raw hold on a boolean stunned, EFFECT_IMMUNE_*, "immunities are ANY stats", a separate body-factor namespace
Derived valuesSTAT(T, v, FORMULA, formula = PROC_REF(f)), evaluated in the stat layer. A stat fed by several sources lists them as separate contributes() lines.derive(nameof(v)) with the magic derive_<v>(), stat(..., from = list(...))
Contributing across entitiescontributes_to(nameof(rel), STAT_X, cond | value, priority =): released when the relation changes or either end is deleted. A system keeps a tracked night var and areas contribute from it.A loop that re-holds every related entity on a timer (the night-shift system)
Holdssource is required on hold(). A hold with lasts = ends at its time even if its source is deleted (the baton's stun survives the baton); a hold without it ends with its source. reapply = REAPPLY_* on the stat sets what a repeat does; hold_until sets an absolute end; hold_override(E, STAT_X, value, priority = PRIORITY_ADMIN) is the forcing form. A hold on a clock-rate stat runs on CLOCK_WORLD, and remaining-time reads return deciseconds on their own clock. A source holding more than HOLD_RELEASE_BATCH holds (default 256) releases them in slices in phase G, coalesced per target.A timed hold dying with its short-lived source; per-caller "extend if longer" wrappers
SourcesA datum or a SOURCE_DEF(ai_control) flyweight (SRC_AI_CONTROL); text or null is an error. An op's holds() and grants() default to the op token as source, owned by the target's store; source = SRC_HELD_ITEM opts into "the item owns it" (an op with consumes() and an item-sourced hold is a build error); source = A.actor, or source = ON_ACTOR as a part, is the per-actor source ("this AI's bolts"). SRC_ALL and SRC_VV are the admin sources.String sources such as "ai" and "wire"
Capability state keyscap_keys(CAP_COVER, OPEN = MSG(cover/closed), ...) generates the COVER_OPEN ids, the <cap>_<key>(holder) accessors (cover_open, panel_open, lock_locked), default reasons and read registration. READS_AS stays only for non-capability producer keys, with a build check that the accessor's own reads are a subset. A cap key's declared reason serves its usual test; the opposite test needs because =. A stat or state word holds at most 24 flags, so cap_keys() allocates one word per capability.Hand-written READS_AS for capability state; accessors that duplicate a var; one shared bit space
Op, capability and tag keysOp keys are literals. Ops a capability brings are namespaced automatically ("cover.open"), and those of a discriminated capability include the discriminator (cell_bay.cell.insert); a type's own ops are bare; construction keys are construction.build:<stage>, construction.undo:<stage>, construction.dismantle. A capability key is (CAP_X, discriminator): an identical repeat dedupes, the same key with different params is a build error. The build resolves every literal in extend, without, configure, performs, perform_op, on_op and priority(above()); an unknown key at runtime is an error, not a no-op.Unprefixed keys that collide across capabilities, computed keys, a silent perform_op on an unknown key
Every string key is declaredBuild stages are STAGE_DEF ids, sources SOURCE_DEF, messages MSG_DEF; sequence facts are typed vars on the context; prompt and request payloads are typed vars set by named args; UI args arrive as typed proc parameters. An unresolved identifier is a build error and no key is computed.Free-text keys resolved at runtime
Typed actionsACTION(fall, turf/landing, mob/living/landed_on) is a generator marker producing /datum/act/fall, act_fall(...) and the notice /datum/notice/fell, always in the past tense. Call sites use ACT_TRY, ACT_PASS (a shared constant, nothing allocated), act_done, act_cancel; a lint requires one of the last two on every path. The op action's notice is /datum/notice/op_done; ACTION(move, ...) has origin, destination, direction.Hand-written act and notice pairs; slashed, ui_opened and other hand-named notices
Defining a capabilityCAPABILITY_DEF(name, CAP_X, params...) for the function form and CAPABILITY_TYPE(name, CAP_X, /datum/capability/x, params...) for the datum form. The params are the settings, configure(CAP_X, p = v) re-runs the body, and an unknown param is a build error. Bundles are plain procs returning entries; wrap one in a capability when something must address it. There is one definition of powered, a capability over powered_by(system, role).capability(KEY, settings...) with free settings, two definitions of powered, type procs as bundles
One list per typeOne CAPABILITIES(T, ...); a second on the same type is a build error. The table builder records __FILE__:__LINE__ per entry in test builds.capabilities(), reactions(), relations(), derived(), CAPABILITY() (the docs' "three table procs" rule); lists merged by include order
Capability datacap_data(A) returns a typed /datum/cap_data/<cap> (RETURN_TYPE).cap_data(holder, KEY)["x"]
Grant tokensgranted_trait(), granted_language(), granted_access(), granted_verb(), hidden_verb(); a granted verb is grant(E, granted_verb(path), source).Separate grant stores per kind
Busy stateAn actor's busy state is its pending op (busy policy BUSY_REPLACE or BUSY_REFUSE); there is no base busy stat. A thing occupied for a while (a slot machine spinning) declares a stat named for that state and holds it with lasts =.om_hold_busy, om_in_use, a shared STAT_BUSY

State, relations and lifecycle

QuestionSettled asLosing form(s)
Two state conceptsTRACKED(T, a, b, c) (variadic: settings and plain state) and STAT (composed, including FORMULA). Capability state is cap_keys(). TRACKED_BRIDGED survives only as the build-time shim table of section 19, until the machine and Life pipelines go.3-argument TRACKED, setting(nameof(v), choices =), from =, derive(), OM_FIELD and its relatives
Declaring links and ownershipFour relation entries in CAPABILITIES: ref_one/ref_many (a reference, with on_other_deleted = CLEAR | DELETE_ME), owns_one/owns_many (on_destroy = DELETE | SPILL | PRIVATE_COPY, copy_on_write =), link(a::x, b::y) (paired, declared once; hot = TRUE for the engine's hot paths), and slot(...). Writes are rel_set, rel_add, rel_remove, rel_clear, rel_move, rel_take (the last two pick a member with member = or key =), dispatching on the declared kind; own_* and proto_* merge into them. A copy-on-write var holds a shared prototype until rel_private(E, nameof(v)) returns the holder's private copy, made on the first call; rel_is_private asks.ownership(), owns/shares/proto, the 16 OWN/REL/PROTO macros, the ten-parameter rel_one, the ten own_* writers, OWN_SPILL/OWN_CONTAINED
Slot declarationsslot(id, accepts =, capacity =, exposure =, at = BAY_X, on_destroy =) over the containment ledger./datum/slot_def, /datum/om/relation/slot, SLOT_DROP_HOLDER
Lasting effects while something is in a slotwhile_slotted(SLOT_X, entries..., on = ON_CONTENTS | ON_HOLDER) grants the entries to the contained item (or to the holder) with the slot entry as source, released on exit, delete or holder delete. The slot owner declares ON_CONTENTS (stasis beds, bellies, cryo, borg docks); the item declares ON_HOLDER (worn shields and mirrors).Loose per-stat holds placed by the effect's owner and released by hand (the belly's two prey holds), a buckle notice pair for stasis
Per-instance listsLIST_STATE(T, var, LAZY | ROWS | SET | TABLE) declares a per-instance list with its allocation policy; the instance-list lint accepts declared ones.ALLOW(instance_list) (480 annotations)
Initial contentsstarts = T | list(T...) | pick_one(list) | PROC_REF(x) | when(cond, T), starts_args = list(...), and the capability hook on_holder_preinit(A) for work that must precede the parent's init.ALLOW(decl) (128), starts_from and starts_at
Writing a var by nameTrait var_changes become contributes() and may name only stats. vars_write(E, name, value, source = SRC_VV) is the one primitive for VV, SDQL and serialization.Raw vars[name] = x writers (about 50 ALLOW(api))
Entities carried in dataA generation-checked handle: ref_token(E) and resolve_token(token).Raw references or REF() text in player-copyable data
Initial evaluationInitial stat evaluation is silent (no notices, no hooks) until map load completes.A notice storm at boot
Rust-owned factsRead as native("key"). DM-owned settings are tracked vars pushed by the push_to_rust() output with native_write().Generated per-field get/set procs (rust_bindings.md sections 0 and 3); update_rust_device()
DM timers and keys in RustKernel only. The Rust world scheduler keeps native watches and rate models. The work is E6's, not phase 0's.om_world_at, DM-owned keys published into Rust, the reactor

Execution and hooks

QuestionSettled asLosing form(s)
Execution modelWrites are immediate and mark their dependents. Stats recompute inline. Hooks and outputs drain at the end of the current trigger and at the start of phases D, P and R, and on_change runs at the drain with the keys that changed. Ranks come from the generated reads graph (tracked vars, stats, conditions, hooks, outputs); a cycle in the condition and stat graph is a build error, and an iteration cap per drain logs the key chain and spills the rest to the next tick. Phase R runs only outputs. Notices deliver synchronously at act_done in publish order, to a depth of 8.Unspecified ordering between recompute, hooks and outputs
When conditions evaluateConditions used for matching (when() on ops, look_layer(when =), menus) are evaluated when read, at resolution or output time. Conditions that drive transitions (entry-level when() attaching and detaching entries, every(when =) start and stop, on_change(cond, ENTER | EXIT) edges, while_slotted) are evaluated at the drain.One undefined rule for both kinds
ContextsPooled /datum/act, not stored in vars or passed in with = (lint); A.snapshot() returns a plain list; A.args carries typed UI and topic arguments and after(..., with =) payloads (scalars and flyweights only); the kernel wraps each handler and releases its context on a runtime; a production watchdog frees contexts held over 2 minutes with no pending op and logs the creator; performs() opens a child.op_ctx, guard_ctx, stored contexts
Hook formsRefuse: extend(target, needs(...)). Take over: extend(/datum/act/x, instead(parts..., order = ORDER_EARLY | ORDER_NORMAL | ORDER_LATE)) (chance() inside means "takes over with probability p"; the notice is published with replaced = TRUE). Modify in flight: extend(/datum/act/hit, adjusts(field, by =, scale =, when =)). React: on_notice, on_op, on_change(cond, ENTER | EXIT | ANY). Unrefusable: ACTION(x, ..., FIXED) plus PUBLISH. Another entity listens: observe(source, /datum/notice/x, listener, parts...), whose handler runs on the listener (A.holder is the listener, A.target the observed entity).intercept(), NOTICE(), DAMAGE_REACTION, power_change overrides, guard/guarded
Hook candidates and orderA target's type table plus every capability granted to it (grants, while_slotted, species, traits), with the same precedence. Order is order =, then runs_after = CAP_X, then declaration order. Notice names come from the action in the past tense (inserted, buckled, unbuckled, fell, crossed, uncrossed, removed); the world actions include unbuckle, uncross, remove.Hooks that see only the base type table
Event coalescingNotices never coalesce. "Facts changed" is a stat or derived value.The coalescing material_facts_changed event
Mob Life target/datum/sequence/life with seq_step procs, compute_facts() on the frame and run_step_now(E, PROC_REF(step))./datum/system/life with step(mob, dt) (migration guide B30, kernel.md section 3.5); seq_condition, seq_replan, seq_plan_key
Machine pipeline targetPer-machine every(when =), stats and membership.Per-power-mode member_step variants on the power system

Actions, ops and verbs

QuestionSettled asLosing form(s)
Op grammarFour stages: Match (silent, when), Require (loud, needs), Wait, Do. Do order: costs and chance(), effects, feedback, delayed(). asks() is a suspension point inside the Do list, not a container; parts after it run after the answer, and costs are spent only after the last answer, so cancelling costs nothing. confirms("text") is asks(/datum/prompt/yes_no, question = "text").Hand-written guards that decide between hiding and refusing (applies_to, available_on, else_say, works_*)
Ties and priorityTarget, then held, then actor; a subtype before its parent; then capability declaration order. Tiers, high to low: SUBVERT, CLAW, TAKE_OUT, ATTACK, PART, NORMAL, DEFAULT (tool() yields PART). Relative: priority(above("cover.open")), priority(below(CAP_LOCK)). Two ops on one target with the same input, intent and tier are a build error, unless their when parts are mutually exclusive. The claw swipe is a species or mob op that yields CLAW.A silent "first declared wins"; a claws() capability
One input tableInput part to route, affordance, default intent and default tier, with full enumerations: ROUTE_* (PHYSICAL, TK, INTERFACE, UI, TOPIC, MENU, SPEECH, INSIDE, AUTHORITY, GHOST; ROUTE_ALL, ROUTE_NONE), AFF_* (MANIPULATE, HOLD, HOLD_SMALL, ATTACK, CONTROL), INTENT_* (including INTENT_PRESENT), GESTURE_*. Reach lives on the provider: provides(AFF_X, route =, reach =, accepts =); physical routes imply adjacency. via() and by() replace the routes and affordances the input part implies; stance() and hostile() are sugar.AFF_INTERFACE, AFF_TK, a range on at_target(), the defaults that lived in a code comment
Waitswait(t, keeps = WAIT_KEEPS_DEFAULT) (HELD | ADJACENT | TARGET_PRESENT | ALIVE). A read change re-runs requirements and cancels only if one now refuses. The pending op is a kernel record related to actor, target and held, so deleting any of them cancels it with feedback. BUSY_REPLACE for players ("You stop prying."), BUSY_REFUSE for AI; an open asks() takes the actor's one wait slot.om_task_*, om_busy
Costs and chainingcosts(RES_X, n), consumes(), cooldown(t). An op's own costs() replaces its tool profile's (write costs(RES_FUEL, +1) to add). performs(key) runs the target op's Require and Do with no nested wait, and a cycle is a build error. passes() lets later candidates run after it commits; their refusals are silent. toggles_hold(STAT_X, value, source =) is an op part.Four cost parts; manual re-dispatch
Gameplay verbsGameplay abilities are ops with menu(button = ICON_STATE, bind = "shift+f"), listed under one "Abilities" verb-panel entry with action buttons and keybinds. verb_entry(), ADMIN_VERB and DEBUG_VERB are only for client, OOC and admin commands; the layering lint forbids verb_entry under code/content.DECLARE_VERB for gameplay abilities, per-ability verb panels
AbilitiesActor ops in granted capabilities./datum/ability (rules.md section 5)
Override surfaceEvery entry takes an optional key =; extend, without and configure accept any entry key (configure("scan", interval = ...) retunes an inherited keyed entry); extend("x", drop = "id") relaxes a requirement; extend("x", replaces(parts...)) swaps an effect list.refine, replace, unnamed entries
Explain toolingexplain_click(actor, target, held, gesture) (admin verb "Explain Interaction"), perform_op(actor, target, key, held, authority =, trace =), explain_type(/type) (also offline, as an analysis query) and assert_resolves in tests. An empty resolution shows the best near-miss's reason, rate-limited. Build errors are tagged with their tier: DM compile, generator, boot, test."Why was this refused?" as a separate debug verb; emag_target as a special form
Resolution costTwo passes. Pass 1 uses an interned index by (intent, held type) and the cheap gates, running no requirement code. Pass 2 runs requirements for the winner only; menus and screentips evaluate lazily (at_most = 0.2 SECONDS) behind a cache keyed by (actor, target, held, act generation); the reach walk is cached per containment-ledger version.Evaluating every candidate's requirements on every click

Look, UI, prompts and requests

QuestionSettled asLosing form(s)
UI forminterface(window, title =, rights =, host =, input = hand()) (declaring it adds the open op), the ui_data(datum/act/A) output, and a declared ui_shape(operating = UI_BOOL, channels = UI_LIST(...)) from which the TypeScript types are generated, UI ops and their typed args included. Test builds validate pushed data against the shape. Window access (range, conscious, rights) is a requirement with generated reads. The tgui transport keeps a window pool, prewarm and assets, and has no polling.DECLARE_UI/UI_ACT/UI_DATA (AGENTS.md), ui_window() plus interface() plus auto-open, the polling fire() and update_uis INVOKE_ASYNC, the act_<x>() naming convention (migration guide A7)
PromptsKinds: text, number, list, checklist, yes_no (it absorbs confirm and alert), color, colormatrix, mob, typepath, bitfield, file, icon, form, all answering in a uniform value field. asks() inside a trigger; notify(user, text, title =) is an info popup with no answer. One open prompt per answerer per op: asking again focuses it. Stale windows close themselves, with the reason, when a requirement the prompt reads now refuses. UI arg_* are shorthand over prompt kinds with one validator.Eight *_ask variants, ask_text/ask_number as separate procs, raw input(), alert() and tgui_input_*
One request concept/datum/request, with kinds /datum/prompt/* (player answers), /datum/io/sql | http | rustg_job | path (backends) and /datum/client_query/winget | measure_text | .... They share typed fields, weak handles, a timeout, re-check on answer, cancel when the owner dies, and metrics. Inside a trigger: asks(/datum/request/x, field = v...); outside: request(owner, /datum/request/x, then(PROC_REF(done)), field = v...); prompts take answerer =. await() stays kernel and request-layer only. request_urgent() is renamed kernel_urgent(), so "request" means only /datum/request.io_request, ask(answerer, ...) outside triggers, request_urgent()
Look callslook.variant/layer/glow/level plus look.identity and look.composite, and look_layer entries, after the global icon rename. Phase 3 step C2 rewrites the calls.look.state/overlay/gauge/light (kept only until the rename finishes)
Chunked jobs and pathsjob(owner, PROC_REF(step), budget =, then =): the step returns JOB_MORE or JOB_DONE, and budget = is a percent of a tick (map loader, site generation, POI queue, zone cleanup). /datum/io/path is served by a budgeted path system.stoplag and INVOKE_ASYNC in those four, the pathfinder mutex
Faultssafe_call(PROC_REF(x), ...) returns a /datum/result (ok, value, error); try_parse_json(text); EXPECT_RUNTIME in tests. Raw try/catch only inside the kernel.65 ALLOW(silent_catch)
Body effectsCapabilities granted with a duration.The separate body-effect store

Kernel, time and I/O

QuestionSettled asLosing form(s)
Host systemsDatabase, tgui transport, garbage, ticker, verdigris, lighting, input and the rest are ordinary systems: SYSTEM_DEF with phase = KERNEL_PHASE_K (air runs in phase N) and latency_class = LATENCY_L0. "No subsystems" is now true, and the MC_* metric defines become KERNEL_*.SUBSYSTEM_DEF, fire(), the Master Controller, SS_KERNEL_HOSTED, the MC_* metric names
Systems' membersA system declares roles = list(...); join(system, E, source, role) and leave(system, E, source, role); every(members =) sweeps members in systems only, the member being A.target.System roster vars, a role-less join
InputOne typed inbox for every client entry point (click and modifiers, drag, Topic, verb, tgui act, say and emote, held keys). An event resolves on the spot if budget remains and queues otherwise; phase K drains in arrival order under the input budget and never sheds; each event resolves through the action path at drain time, and a stale target is dropped with the gate reason. The inbox owns client/Topic, and the tick meter's per-input wait metrics move to it.SSinput, SSverb_manager, the speech lane, the click holdback, opt-in queue macros and callbacks
Databasesystem(/datum/system/db) owns the connection, round records and schema check, with /datum/io/sql as the backend. Content uses only asks() or request() with /datum/io/sql/*, and sql_write(). db_query_now() is allowed only in world/IsBanned and the database system's boot step (schema check, admin and rank tables), by lint with no ALLOW. Login lookups are async, and the client is "ready" when its data arrives.NewQuery()/Execute(), synchronous Execute in handlers (49 sites)
Time formsFive: after(), every(), hold(lasts =), COOLDOWN (with COOLDOWN_KEYED_DECLARE/_START/_FINISHED for dynamic keys), STAMP/ELAPSED. Section 3 lists the 14 old spellings and what each became. every(interval, parts..., when =, lane =, key =) plus runs_after = for ordering. expire(N) is the lifecycle verb.The fourteen spellings; every() knobs beyond those
Repeating workevery(interval, parts..., when =)periodic_cadence/periodic_step/should_run, DECLARE_PERIODIC_WHILE, DECLARE_REPEAT, member_should_run/member_step, the OM behaviour every var
The one timerafter(owner, delay, parts..., key =, clock =, with =); delayed(t, parts...) is its holder-clock form inside an op.rx_after as public API, after_slot, OWN_TIMER, varargs after
Timed stateHolds. COOLDOWN_* for rate limits. STAMP/ELAPSED for "how long since".timed_set, EXPIRY_* (the two lints that contradict each other both retire), om_grant_for
Boot-phase I/O and vendored codeBlocking I/O is allowed only before the first tick and is named as such. Vendored code lives in code/host/vendor/, exempt from lints.ALLOW(scheduler) in modules/tgs

Tooling and exemptions

QuestionSettled asLosing form(s)
Build-time analysisOne analysis engine, tools/analyze/ in Rust, built in phase 0b: one parallel load of the tree, a content-hash cache for incremental runs, comments and strings stripped once, text and parse-aware rules on SpacemanDMM's dreammaker parser behind a backend trait, and ALLOW, baselines and exemptions handled once. Every existing lint is ported to it, legacy-form ratchets included, each switched over only when a parity harness shows identical findings to its script on master; check_ratchets.sh and check_grep.sh become wrappers, then go. Phase 1's E5 adds the semantic queries to the same engine: generated reads, key resolution, handler-signature checks, layering, duplicate definitions, the compiled-table diff, context escape, ACT_TRY pairing, purity, the shim registry, the .dme generator and the ACTION/CAPABILITY_DEF/STAT/ui_shape generators. One diagnostics format (file:line: [rule] message -- hint, tagged with its tier) and one incremental daemon shared by agent worktrees. The incremental DM compiler's front end replaces dreammaker behind the trait once it passes the phase-1 readiness gate; it analyses only, and dm.exe still produces the .dmb. TGUI, Rust, the icon pipeline and the Verdigris bindings keep their toolchains.About 40 Python lints and check_grep.sh each re-reading the tree (about 495 s and 146 s a run), DreamChecker rules and separate generators (DreamChecker stays as a second opinion until the engine covers its rules); "legacy ratchets stay Python until zero"
ExemptionsOne mechanism: directory scopes in tools/ci/lint_scopes.toml (unit tests, benchmarks, vendored code), ALLOW(lint/CODE) reason codes, a minimum reason quality, an exact unused-ALLOW check, and per-lint ceilings.ALLOW(base_vars), // INIT:, ALLOW(init), the three check_grep.sh allowlists
Test primitivesTEST_RAW_SET, await_ticks, assert_resolves, and the resolver-agnostic behaviour snapshot harness.Interaction-id snapshots, "unchanged snapshots" as a gate
Examples as testsThe examples of section 16, and the reference snippets marked complete, compile under DOC_SNIPPETS from phase 2; a snippet marked as an excerpt does not compile on its own, and a form not defined in sections 1 to 15 fails the build."Design, not compiled" examples
MappingSection 17, "Old to new", is curated (rows with at least 20 uses or any content-facing semantic change, plus a "switches on in phase N" column); the full generated table is doc/rewrite/api_mapping.tsv. Section 18 is 10 to 15 lines grouped by family, with the rule that an om_* form not listed is internal.One 897-row table and its copy

Plan and layout

QuestionSettled asLosing form(s)
Conversion strategyBranch triage, a clean base and the analysis engine first; engines; then the library on the new forms; then codemod infrastructure; then mechanical codemods tree-wide per form in freeze windows; then waves that finish every remaining form and move their files; then delete the legacy layer. Old and new forms coexist only through the registered shim set (section 19), each shim with an owner step and a lint that fails if it outlives it."Migrate no callers" vs "no shims" vs "one folder at a time" (all three retire); "wrappers exist only while callers remain" as the only rule
Phases and track namesPhases 0, 0b, 1, 1S, 2, 2.5, 3, 4, 5, 6, 7; steps A1 to C2 inside phase 3; waves W01 to W37 and move waves M01 to M12. The retired track names (S1 to S11, S0 to S4, A1 to A16, W1 to W6) are replaced by descriptions or commit refs.The overloaded track names
What gates phase 1Small fixtures built from test-only capabilities, one per engine, plus the opening gates (name spike, analysis readiness, E0 contracts). The library examples gate phase 2."The APC, turret, airlock and beaker built exactly as shown" as a phase-1 gate (it needed phase 2's library)
Gates are numbersA pinned baseline commit per phase, a noise floor from A/A pairs and bench-compare's significance test, numeric budgets, a residue integer per form, and eight new bench scenarios added before phase 1."Within 2% of master", "within budget", "residue" undefined, a baseline that moves
Open branchesA triage table in phase 0b: land, rebase after phase N, or abandon, one row per branch with commits ahead.Salvage by memory
AGENTS.mdTwo steps. Phase 0b: "write what exists on master today", a legacy form being banned only when its replacement has landed and its retirement lint is on. Phase 2: the full rewrite to the new forms, with an "on master" column.One rewrite in phase 0 that told agents to write forms that did not yet compile
LayoutEight layers in compile order: defines, host, base, engine, library, domains, content, world, with the unmigrated files as a legacy layer between base and engine in today's order, and code/tests/ outside the order. Base stat declarations live in code/base. A generated .dme; a duplicate-definition lint and a compiled-table diff gate every move; the ~3,900 files with no legacy form move in waves M01 to M12; the completion test is "legacy-form lints at zero and the move table complete".Six folders with no home for helpers, base types, tgui, admin or tests; "old directories empty" as the completion test; "a pure git mv changes nothing"
DocsThis document is doc/rewrite/final_api.html, and becomes doc/rewrite/api.md plus chapters at phase 7. AGENTS.md follows the two steps above. Archived OM docs and stale chapter sections are deleted at phase 7.Status markers that disagree, and "archived but still describes the code" pointers

22Source layout

The new tree is organised by what a file is for, not by where it came from. Framework code, shared capabilities, large systems with their own model, ordinary content, and the glue every layer needs each get a top-level folder under code/. Every file under code/ has a destination, including the ~3,900 that never had a legacy form. A file moves into the new tree only when it is fully migrated, or, if it has nothing to migrate, in a bulk move wave.

The tree

code/defines/{engine,library,host,base,content}
    // macros and constants only; replaces __defines/. Files whose macros depend on each other keep their order (order.txt).
code/host/{helpers,globals,click,client,admin,tgui,assets,config,vendor}
    // glue every layer needs: helpers, global vars, click and HUD glue, client and admin plumbing,
    // the tgui backend, assets, config. vendor/ is third-party code (TGS), exempt from lints.
code/base/{atom,area,turf,obj,mob}
    // the root types /atom /atom/movable /obj /obj/item /obj/machinery /obj/structure /mob /mob/living /turf /area:
    // their vars, base procs and base STAT, ACTION and TRACKED declarations.
code/engine/{declare,kernel,time,state,stats,refs,change,actions,parts,hooks,present,io,_generated}
    // the framework, one folder per concept. _generated holds the analysis engine's output
    // (reads tables, STAT ids, ACTION types) and is never hand-edited.
code/library/{machine,access,containers,reagents,items,structures,construction,providers,mobs}
    // shared capabilities and bundles (section 11)
code/domains/{body,damage,atmos,power,lighting,life,ai,verdigris}
    // big systems with a model of their own
code/content/<domain>/...
    // e.g. content/machinery/power/apc.dm, content/doors/airlock.dm
code/world/{systems,round,maps}
    // world-level systems (vote, supply, research, ...), map glue, round flow
code/tests/
    // cross-cutting tests, snapshot capture and benchmarks; compiled only under UNIT_TESTS

Layers

Compile order is the order of this table, with the legacy layer between base and engine. The legacy layer is every file not yet moved, kept in exactly today's relative include order. code/engine/_generated/ compiles last of all, as code/_generated/ does today, because it names types defined everywhere.

OrderLayerHoldsMay reference
1definesMacros and constants onlyNothing
2hostHelpers, globals, click and HUD glue, client and admin plumbing, tgui backend, assets, config, vendored codeDefines; base types by path
3baseThe root types, their base vars and procs, and the base declarations (below)Defines, host. Not the procs or types of engine, library, domains, content or world
·legacyFiles not yet migrated, in today's order, shrinking as they moveEverything (it is what is being retired)
4engineThe frameworkDefines, host, base
5libraryShared capabilities and bundles+ engine
6domainsBig systems with their own model+ library
7contentOrdinary content+ domains
8worldWorld-level systems, map glue, round flowEverything above

Rules

RuleDetail
One type, one fileA type's CAPABILITIES list, its procs, its MSG_DEFs and its SYSTEM_DEFs live together. The root types split: vars, base procs and standalone declaration lines in code/base, and the one CAPABILITIES list in the library area that composes it (code/library/machine/machine.dm for /obj/machinery), so base never references the library.
Tests beside codeA test of one file sits next to it (apc.dm, apc_test.dm). Cross-cutting tests, snapshot capture (the 1,968-line dq_i7_bulk_capture.dm snapshots hundreds of types) and benchmarks sit in code/tests/. Neither is in the generated .dme directly: the generator writes code/tests/_tests.dm, an include list under #if defined(UNIT_TESTS), in place of today's hand-sorted _unit_tests.dm list.
Generated .dmedeepquarry.dme is generated by a query on the analysis engine (phase 1, tool L) from a checked-in template and the tree. The template holds what must not move: code\genesis_call.dme first, the FILE_DIR block, the BEGIN and END markers, the root files (code/__byond_version_compat.dm, __odlint.dm, _compile_options.dm, world.dm and the rest of the loose code/*.dm) in today's order, and the tail: interface\*.dm, interface\skin.dmf (the .dmf last, as today) and the #ifdef DOC_SNIPPETS block. Between head and tail the generator writes the layers in order: sorted by path inside a layer, except where a directory's order.txt says otherwise; the legacy layer from tools/build/dme_legacy_order.txt; _generated last. A hand edit to the generated file fails the check.
Layering lintA layer references its own layer and earlier ones; host and base are readable by all. STAT, ACTION, TRACKED and MSG_DEF lines are declarations the generator reads, not references. No verb_entry under code/content. Nothing under engine, library, domains or content may use an old (pre-design) form. Exempt by path in lint_scopes.toml: the engine's own raw tracked and stat writes and its catch, code/tests/, and code/host/vendor/. The legacy layer is exempt from the old-form rule but not from the next one.
Duplicate-definition lintNo (type, var) or (type, proc) is defined in two files. Re-opening a type in several files to set different vars is normal; defining the same member twice means the last file compiled wins, and a move changes which that is. Ratcheted with a baseline in phase 1; no wave may add a pair, and a wave that moves a file in a pair must resolve it.
Compiled-table diffBefore and after each move commit, a tool walks the object tree and dumps, per type, the hash of every var's winning definition text and each proc's ordered list of defining files. The diff may show only file paths and line numbers changing. A changed winner, initial value or override order fails the wave.
IconsIcons follow the same domain split, in the phase-3 icon rename. Waves never touch maps or icons.

Base declarations: where the engine's stats live

The engine's stats name base types: operable, can_move, can_act and acts_via on /obj/machinery and /mob/living, and the world actions (move, cross, fall, hit) name /turf and /mob/living. A layering rule that said "engine may not reference content" read as if these declarations had to sit in the engine. They don't.

  • They are standalone STAT(...) and ACTION(...) lines in code/base/<type>/, layer 3, below the engine. The macros are defines (layer 1).
  • The analysis engine reads them and emits the resulting var/ lines, STAT_* ids and /datum/act types into code/engine/_generated/. So the engine reads generated ids, never base files, and a base file never names an engine proc or type.
  • Stats and actions of a type that is not a root (a turret's armed) live with that type, in its capability or content file.

Where the other files go

Section 19's phase 6 waves cover about 1,900 files that still carry a legacy form after phase 3. The other ~3,900 files have nothing to convert, so each directory below has a destination and a bulk move wave. A move wave is a pure git mv in its own freeze window, regenerates the .dme, and must pass the duplicate-definition lint and the compiled-table diff. A directory's files split between its move wave and the content waves: a content wave moves its own files when it proves them.

Migration rule

A file lives in the new tree only when it is fully migrated or has nothing to migrate. Each content wave does the same four things in order:

  1. Convert the files in the wave.
  2. Prove them: zero old forms (per-file ratchet), tests, the behaviour snapshot harness, the construction round-trip and the bench.
  3. Move them in a pure git mv commit, with no edits, so history follows. The duplicate-definition lint and the compiled-table diff must pass first, because moving a file under a generated .dme changes compile order and so can change which definition wins.
  4. Merge.

Old APIs are deleted in the commit their last caller goes. The lint for that form is a ratchet from the day its replacement lands and a hard ban from that commit (Coexistence rules).

Completion test. The migration is done when every legacy-form lint is at zero with its baseline deleted, and the move table is complete: every .dm file under code/ is in a layer folder, the legacy list dme_legacy_order.txt is empty and the shim registry is empty. An old directory that still exists (it may hold non-code files) is not itself a failure, and an empty one is not proof of completion.

The design is against origin/master 6dcd1e30f9. The inventories behind it are five read-only surveys (the OM layer, the sys layer, the current framework, subsystems and services, and the docs) plus a mechanical scan of every framework macro, proc and hook (api_surface.py). Section 17 and the full table beside this page, doc/rewrite/api_mapping.tsv, are generated from that scan (mapping.py), and coverage.py checks that every form game code uses is named in this document or in that table. Code in sections 1–16 is design, not compiled: from phase 2 the examples of section 16 compile as DOC_SNIPPETS tests.
